Martingale Hardy spaces

Contents

Key words:
Harmonic analysis, Vilenkin groups, Vilenkin systems, Lebesgue spaces, Weak-
L_{p}
spaces, modulus of continuity, Vilenkin-Fourier coefficients, partial
sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series, Lebesgue constants, Fejér means, Cesàro
means, Nörlund means, Riesz and Nörlund logarithmic means, martingales, martingale Hardy spaces, maximal operators,
strong convergence, inequalities, approximation.

Abstract

The classical theory of Fourier series deals with decomposition of a
function into sinusoidal waves. Unlike these continuous waves the Vilenkin
(Walsh) functions are rectangular waves. Such waves have already been used
frequently in the theory of signal transmission, multiplexing, filtering,
image enhancement, codic theory, digital signal processing and pattern
recognition. The development of the theory of Vilenkin-Fourier series has
been strongly influenced by the classical theory of trigonometric series.
Because of this it is inevitable to compare results of Vilenkin series to
those on trigonometric series. There are many similarities between these
theories, but there exist differences also. Much of these can be explained
by modern abstract harmonic analysis, which studies orthonormal systems from the
point of view of the structure of a topological group.

In this PhD thesis we discuss, develop and apply this fascinating theory
connected to modern harmonic analysis. In particular we make new estimations
of Vilenkin-Fourier coefficients and prove some new results concerning
boundedness of maximal operators of partial sums. Moreover, we derive
necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity so that
norm convergence of the partial sums is valid and develop new methods to
prove Hardy type inequalities for the partial sums with respect to the
Vilenkin systems. We also do the similar investigation for the Fejér means.
Furthermore, we investigate some Nörlund means but only in the case when their coefficients are monotone. Some well-know examples of Nörlund means are
Fejér means, Cesàro means and Nörlund logarithmic means. In addition, we
consider Riesz logarithmic means, which are not example of Nörlund
means. It is also proved that these results are the best possible in a
special sense. As applications both some well-known and new results are
pointed out.

This PhD is written as a monograph consisting of four Chapters:
Preliminaries, Fourier coefficients and partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier
series on martingale Hardy spaces, Vilenkin-Fejér means on
martingale Hardy spaces, Vilenkin-Nörlund means on martingale Hardy
spaces. It is based on 15 papers with the candidate as author or coauthor,
but also some new results are presented for the first time.

In Chapter 1 we first present some definitions and notations, which are
crucial for our further investigations. After that we also define some
summabilitity methods and remind about some classical facts and results. We investigate some well-known results and prove new estimates for the kernels of these summabilitity methods, which are very important to prove our main results. Moreover, we define martingale Hardy spaces and construct martingales, which help us to prove sharpness of our main results in the later chapters.

Chapter 2 is devoted to present and prove some new and known results about Vilenkin-Fourier coefficients and partial sums of martingales in Hardy spaces.
First, we show that Fourier coefficients of martingales are not uniformly bounded when
0<p<1
. By applying these results we prove some known Hardy and Paley type inequalities with a new method. After that we investigate partial sums with respect to Vilenkin systems and prove boundedness of maximal operators of partial sums. Moreover, we find necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity for which norm convergence of partial sums hold and we present a new proof of a Hardy type inequality for it.

In Chapter 3 we investigate some analogous problems concerning the partial sums of Fejér means. First we consider some weighted maximal operators of Fejér means and prove some boundedness results for them. After that we apply these results to find necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity for which norm convergence of Fejér means hold. Finally, we prove some new Hardy type inequalities for Fejér means, which is a main part of this PhD thesis. We also prove sharpness of all our main results in this Chapter.

In Chapter 4 we consider boundedness of maximal operators of Nörlund means. After that we prove some strong convergence theorems for these summablility methods.
Since Fejér means, Cesàro means are well-know examples of Nörlund means some well-known and new results are pointed out. We also investigate Riesz and Nörlund logarithmic means simultaneously at the end of this chapter.

1
Preliminaries

1.1
Basic notations

Denote by
\mathbb{N}_{+}
the set of the positive integers,
\mathbb{N}:=\mathbb{N}_{+}\cup\{0\}.
Let
m:=(m_{0,}

m_{1},\ldots)
be a sequence of positive
integers not less than 2. Denote by

Z_{m_{k}}:=\{0,1,\ldots,m_{k}-1\}

the additive group of integers modulo
m_{k}
.

Define the group
G_{m}
as the complete direct product of the groups
Z_{m_{i}}
with the product of the discrete topologies of
Z_{m_{i}}
.

The direct product
\mu
of the measures

\mu_{k}\left(\{j\}\right):=1/m_{k}\text{ \ \ \ }(j\in Z_{m_{k}})

is the Haar measure on
G_{m_{k\text{ }}}
with
\mu\left(G_{m}\right)=1.

In this paper we discuss bounded Vilenkin groups,

i.e. the case
when
\sup_{n}m_{n}<\infty.

The elements of
G_{m}
are represented by sequences

x:=\left(x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{j},\ldots\right)\ \left(x_{j}\in Z_{m_{j}}\right).

It is easy to give a base for the neighborhoods of
G_{m}:

\displaystyle\text{ }I_{0}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=G_{m},\text{ \ }

\displaystyle I_{n}(x)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=\{y\in G_{m}\mid y_{0}=x_{0},\ldots,y_{n-1}=x_{n-1}\}\,\,\left(x\in G_{m},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right).

Let

e_{n}:=\left(0,\ldots,0,x_{n}=1,0,\ldots\right)\in G_{m}\qquad\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right).

If we define
I_{n}:=I_{n}\left(0\right),
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ \overline{I_{n}}:=G_{m}

\backslash

I_{n},
then

\overline{I_{N}}=\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\bigcup}}I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}=\left(\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\bigcup}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\bigcup}}I_{N}^{k,l}\right)\bigcup\left(\underset{k=1}{\bigcup\limits^{N-1}}I_{N}^{k,N}\right),

(1.1)

where

I_{N}^{k,l}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\text{ }I_{N}(0,\ldots,0,x_{k}\neq 0,0,...,0,x_{l}\neq 0,x_{l+1\text{ }},\ldots,x_{N-1\text{ }},\ldots),\\
\text{for }k<l<N,\\
\text{ }I_{N}(0,\ldots,0,x_{k}\neq 0,x_{k+1}=0,\ldots,x_{N-1\text{ }}=0,\text{ }x_{N\text{ }},\ldots),\\
\text{for }l=N.\end{array}\text{ }\right.

The norm (or quasi-norm when
0<p<1
) of the space
L_{p}(G_{m})

\left(0<p<\infty\right)
is defined by

\left\|f\right\|_{p}:=\left(\int_{G_{m}}\left|f\right|^{p}d\mu\right)^{1/p}.

The space
weak-L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
consists of all measurable
functions
f,
for which

\left\|f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}:=\underset{\lambda>0}{\sup}\lambda\mu\left(f>\lambda\right)^{1/p}<+\infty.

The norm of the space of continuous functions
C(G_{m})
is defined by

\left\|f\right\|_{C}:=\underset{x\in G_{m}}{\sup}\left|f(x)\right|<c<\infty.

The best approximation of
f\in L_{p}(G_{m})
(
1\leq p\in\infty
) is
defined as

E_{n}\left(f,L_{p}\right):=\inf_{\psi\in\emph{P}_{n}}\left\|f-\psi\right\|_{p},

where
\emph{P}_{n}
is set of all Vilenkin polynomials of order less than
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

The modulus of continuity of
f\in L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
and
\ f\in C\left(G_{m}\right)
are defined by

\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right):=\sup\limits_{h\in I_{n}}\left\|f\left(\cdot-h\right)-f\left(\cdot\right)\right\|_{p}

and

\omega_{C}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right):=\underset{h\in I_{n}}{\sup}\left\|f\left(\cdot-h\right)-f\left(\cdot\right)\right\|_{C},

respectively.

If we define the so-called generalized number system based on
m
in the
following way :

M_{0}:=1,\ M_{k+1}:=m_{k}M_{k}\,\,\,\ \ (k\in\mathbb{N}),

then every
n\in\mathbb{N}
can be uniquely expressed as

n=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}n_{j}M_{j},

where
n_{j}\in Z_{m_{j}}

(j\in\mathbb{N}_{+})
and only a finite number of
n_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}
s differ from zero.

Let

\left\langle n\right\rangle:=\min\{j\in\mathbb{N}:n_{j}\neq 0\}\text{ \ and \ }\left|n\right|:=\max\{j\in\mathbb{N}:n_{j}\neq 0\},

that is
M_{\left|n\right|}\leq n\leq M_{\left|n\right|+1}.
Set

d\left(n\right):=\left|n\right|-\left\langle n\right\rangle,\text{ \ for \ all \ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}.

For the natural number
n=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}n_{j}M_{j},
we define
functions
v
and
v^{\ast}
by (for details see Lukomskii
[
38
]
)

v\left(n\right):=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left|\delta_{j+1}-\delta_{j}\right|+\delta_{0},\text{ \ \ }v^{\ast}\left(n\right):=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\delta_{j}^{\ast},

where

\delta_{j}=sign\left(n_{j}\right)=sign\left(\ominus n_{j}\right)\text{
\ and \ \ \ }\delta_{j}^{\ast}=\left|\ominus n_{j}-1\right|\delta_{j}

and
\ominus
is the inverse operation for

a_{k}\oplus b_{k}:=(a_{k}+b_{k})\text{mod}m_{k}.

Next, we introduce on
G_{m}
an orthonormal systems, which are called
Vilenkin systems.

At first, we define the complex-valued function
r_{k}\left(x\right):G_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{C},
the generalized Rademacher functions, by

r_{k}\left(x\right):=\exp\left(2\pi ix_{k}/m_{k}\right),\text{ }\left(i^{2}=-1,x\in G_{m},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right).

Now, define Vilenkin systems
\,\,\,\psi:=(\psi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N})
on
G_{m}
as:

\psi_{n}(x):=\prod\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}r_{k}^{n_{k}}\left(x\right),\,\,\ \ \,\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right).

The Vilenkin systems are orthonormal and complete in
L_{2}\left(G_{m}\right)
(see e.g. Vilenkin

[
77
]
).

Specifically, we call this system the Walsh-Paley system when
m\equiv 2.

Next, we introduce some analogues of the usual definitions in
Fourier-analysis. If
f\in L_{1}\left(G_{m}\right)
we can define the
Fourier coefficients, the partial sums of the Fourier series, the Dirichlet
kernels with respect to Vilenkin systems in the usual manner:

\widehat{f}\left(n\right):=\int_{G_{m}}f\overline{\psi}_{n}d\mu,\,\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \,\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right),

S_{n}f:=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\widehat{f}\left(k\right)\psi_{k},\text{ \ \ \ }\left(n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right),

D_{n}:=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\psi_{k\text{ }},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right),

respectively.

The
n
-th Lebesgue constant is defined in the following way:

L_{n}:=\left\|D_{n}\right\|_{1}.

1.2
Definition and examples of Nörlund means and its maximal
operators

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of nonnegative numbers. The
n
-th Nörlund means for the Fourier series of
f
is defined by

t_{n}f:=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}q_{n-k}S_{k}f,

(1.2)

where

Q_{n}:=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}q_{k}.

We always assume that
q_{0}>0
and

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}Q_{n}=\infty.\

In this case it is well-known that the summability method generated by
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
is regular if and only if

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}=0.

(1.3)

Concerning this fact and related basic results, we refer to
[
40
]
.

The next remark is due to Persson,
Tephnadze and Wall
[
51
]
:

Remark 1.1

a) Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be
non-increasing. Then the summability method generated by
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is regular.

b) Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be non-decreasing. Then the
summability method generated by
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is not always
regular.

Proof
:
Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be non-increasing. Then

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{q_{n-1}}{nq_{n-1}}=\frac{1}{n}\rightarrow 0,\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

According to (
1.3
) we conclude that in this case the summability
method is regular.

Now, we prove part b) of theorem and construct Nörlund mean, with
non-decreasing coefficients
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\},
which is not
regular.

Let
\{q_{k}=2^{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}.
Then

Q_{n}=\overset{n-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}2^{k}=2^{n}-1\leq 2^{n}

and

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}=\frac{2^{n-1}}{2^{n}-1}\geq\frac{2^{n-1}}{2^{n}}=\frac{1}{2}\nrightarrow 0,\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

By using again (
1.3
) we obtain that when the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is non-decreasing, then the summability method is not always
regular.

The proof is complete.

Let
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with monotone and bounded sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\},
such that

q:=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}q_{n}>c>0.

Then, if the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is non-decreasing, we get
that

nq_{0}\leq Q_{n}\leq nq.

In the case when the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is non-increasing,
then

nq\leq Q_{n}\leq nq_{0}.

(1.4)

In both cases we can conclude that

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\text{ \ \ when \ \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.5)

In the special case when
\{q_{k}=1:k\in\mathbb{N}\},
we get Fejér means

\sigma_{n}f:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{k}f\,.

The
\left(C,\alpha\right)
-means (Cesàro means) of the Vilenkin-Fourier
series are defined by

\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}f:=\frac{1}{A_{n}^{\alpha}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}A_{n-k}^{\alpha-1}S_{k}f,

where

A_{0}^{\alpha}:=0,\text{ \ \ }A_{n}^{\alpha}:=\frac{\left(\alpha+1\right)...\left(\alpha+n\right)}{n!},~~\alpha\neq-1,-2,...

It is well-known that (see e.g. Zygmund
[
88
]
)

A_{n}^{\alpha}=\overset{n}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}A_{n-k}^{\alpha-1},

(1.6)

A_{n}^{\alpha}-A_{n-1}^{\alpha}=A_{n}^{\alpha-1},\ \ \ \ \ \ A_{n}^{\alpha}\backsim n^{\alpha}.

(1.7)

In the literature, there is the notion of Riesz means (
\left(R,\alpha\right)
-means) of the Fourier series. Let
\beta_{n}^{\alpha}
denote
the Nörlund mean, where

\left\{q_{0}=1,\text{ }q_{k}=k^{\alpha-1}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\},

that is

\beta_{n}^{\alpha}f:=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left(n-k\right)^{\alpha-1}S_{k}f,\text{ \ \ }0<\alpha<1.

It is obvious that

\frac{\left|q_{n}-q_{n+1}\right|}{n^{\alpha-2}}=O\left(1\right),\ \ \text{when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.8)

and

\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{\alpha}}\right),\text{ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.9)

The
n
-th Nörlund logarithmic mean
L_{n}
and the Riesz logarithmic mean
R_{n}
are defined by

L_{n}f:=\frac{1}{l_{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\frac{S_{k}f}{n-k},\text{ \ \ }R_{n}f:=\frac{1}{l_{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\frac{S_{k}f}{k},

respectively, where

l_{n}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\frac{1}{k}.

Up to now we have considered Nörlund mean in the case when the sequence
\ \{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is bounded but now we consider Nörlund
summabilities with unbounded sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+},

\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and

\log^{(\beta)}x:=\overset{\beta\text{ times}}{\overbrace{\log...\log}}x.

If we define the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
by

\left\{q_{0}=0\text{ and }q_{k}=\log^{\left(\beta\right)}k^{\alpha}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\},

then we get the class of Nörlund means with non-decreasing coefficients:

\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}f:=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\log^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(n-k\right)^{\alpha}S_{k}f.

First we note that
\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}
are
well-defined for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, if we rewrite them as:

\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}f=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\log^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(n-k\right)^{\alpha}}{Q_{n}}S_{k}f.

It is obvious that

\frac{n}{2}\log^{\left(\beta\right)}\frac{n^{\alpha}}{2^{\alpha}}\leq Q_{n}\leq n\log^{\left(\beta\right)}n^{\alpha}.

It follows that

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{c\log^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(n-1\right)^{\alpha}}{n\log^{\left(\beta\right)}n^{\alpha}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\rightarrow 0,\text{ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.10)

For the function
\ f
we consider the following maximal operators:

S^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{n}f\right|,\text{ \ \ \ \ }t^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|t_{n}f\right|,\text{ \ \ \ \ \ }\sigma^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|,

\text{\ }\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|,\text{ \ \ }R^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|R_{n}f\right|,\text{ \ \ }L^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|L_{n}f\right|,

\kappa^{\alpha,\beta,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}f\right|,\text{ \ \ }\beta^{\alpha,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\beta_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|.

We also consider the following weighted maximal operators:

\overset{\sim}{t}_{p,\alpha}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ (0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right),\text{ }0<\alpha\leq 1),

\widetilde{t}_{\alpha}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\log^{1+\alpha}\left(n+1\right)},\text{ \ \ }\ \left(0<\alpha\leq 1\right),

\overset{\sim}{\sigma}_{p}^{\alpha,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{ }(0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right),\text{ }0<\alpha\leq 1),

\overset{\sim}{\beta}_{p}^{\alpha,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\beta_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}},\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ }(0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right),\text{ }0<\alpha\leq 1),

\widetilde{\sigma}^{\alpha,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|}{\log^{1+\alpha}\left(n+1\right)},\text{ \ \ }\widetilde{\beta}^{\alpha,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\beta_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|}{\log^{1+\alpha}\left(n+1\right)},\text{ }\left(0<\alpha<1\right),

\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}},\text{ \ \ \ }\widetilde{\kappa}_{p}^{\alpha,\beta,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}},\text{ \ }\left(0<p<1/2\right),

and

\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)},\text{ \ \ \ \ }\widetilde{\kappa}^{\alpha,\beta,\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}f\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}.

1.3
Dirichlet kernels and Lebesgue constants with respect to
Vilenkin systems

It is easy to see that

S_{n}f\left(x\right)=\int_{G_{m}}f\left(t\right)\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\psi_{k}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)

=\int_{G_{m}}f\left(t\right)D_{n}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)=\left(f\ast D_{n}\right)\left(x\right).

The next well-known identities with respect to Dirichlet kernels (see Lemmas
1.2
and
1.3
, Corollaries
1.4
and
1.5
) will be
used many time in the proofs of our main results. The first equality can be
found in Vilenkin
[
77
]
and the second identity in Gàt and

Goginava
[
19
]
:

Lemma 1.2

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
1\leq s_{n}\leq m_{n}-1.
Then

D_{j+M_{n}}=D_{M_{n}}+\psi_{M_{n}}D_{j}=D_{M_{n}}+r_{n}D_{j},\text{ \ \ }\,j\leq\left(m_{n}-1\right)M_{n}

(1.11)

and

D_{s_{n}M_{n}-j}=D_{s_{n}M_{n}}-\psi_{s_{n}M_{n}-1}\overline{D_{j}},\text{ }\,j<M_{n}.

(1.12)

Lemma 1.3

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq s_{n}\leq m_{n}-1.
Then

D_{s_{n}M_{n}}=D_{M_{n}}\sum_{k=0}^{s_{n}-1}\psi_{kM_{n}}=D_{M_{n}}\sum_{k=0}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{k}

(1.13)

and

D_{n}=\psi_{n}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}D_{M_{j}}\sum_{k=m_{j}-n_{j}}^{m_{j}-1}r_{j}^{k}\right),

(1.14)

for
n=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}n_{i}M_{i}.

Corollary 1.4

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Then

D_{M_{n+1}}=\prod_{k=0}^{n}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{m_{k}-1}r_{k}^{s}\right).

Corollary 1.5

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Then

D_{M_{n}}\left(x\right)\text{\thinspace}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{n},&x\in I_{n},\\
0,&x\notin I_{n}.\end{array}\right.

We also need the following estimate (see Tephnadze
[
71
]
):

Lemma 1.6

Let
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},

s=0,...,N-1.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|D_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{s}}{M_{N}},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
By combining (
1.14
) in Lemma
1.3
and Corollary
1.5
we
have that

\left|D_{n}\left(x\right)\right|\leq\underset{j=0}{\overset{l}{\sum}}n_{j}D_{M_{j}}\left(x\right)=\underset{j=0}{\overset{l}{\sum}}n_{j}M_{j}\leq cM_{l}.

Since
t\in I_{N}
and
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},

s=0,...,N-1,
we
obtain that
x-t\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}
. By using the estimate above we get
that

\left|D_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|\leq cM_{s}

and

\int_{I_{N}}\left|D_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\,\frac{cM_{s}}{M_{N}}.

The proof is complete.

To study the Dirichlet kernels we need an estimate of some sums of
Rademacher functions. This Lemma can be found in Persson and Tephnadze
[
53
]
.

Lemma 1.7

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
x_{n}=1.
Then

\left|\sum_{u=0}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{u}\left(x\right)\right|\geq 1,\text{ for some }1\leq s_{n}\leq m_{n}-1

and

\left|\sum_{u=1}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{u}\left(x\right)\right|\geq 1,\text{ \ for some }2\leq s_{n}\leq m_{n}-1.

Proof
:
Let
x_{n}=1.
Then we readily get that

\left|\sum_{u=0}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{u}\left(x\right)\right|=\left|\frac{r_{n}^{s_{n}}\left(x\right)-1}{r_{n}\left(x\right)-1}\right|

=\frac{\sin\left(\pi s_{n}x_{n}/m_{n}\right)}{\sin\left(\pi x_{n}/m_{n}\right)}=\frac{\sin\left(\pi s_{n}/m_{n}\right)}{\sin\left(\pi/m_{n}\right)}\geq 1.

Analogously, we can prove that

\left|\sum_{u=1}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{u}\left(x\right)\right|=\left|r_{n}\left(x\right)\frac{r_{n}^{s_{n}-1}\left(x\right)-1}{r_{n}\left(x\right)-1}\right|=\frac{\sin\left(\pi\left(s_{n}-1\right)/m_{n}\right)}{\sin\left(\pi/m_{n}\right)}\geq 1.

The proof is complete.

The next Lemma can be found in Tephnadze
[
71
]
:

Lemma 1.8

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\left|n\right|\neq\left\langle n\right\rangle
and
x\in I_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle n\right\rangle+1}.
Then

\left|D_{n}\right|=\left|D_{n-M_{\left|n\right|}}\right|\geq M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Proof
:

Let
x\in I_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle n\right\rangle+1}.

Since

n=n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+\sum_{j=\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{\left|n\right|-1}n_{j}M_{j}+n_{\left|n\right|}M_{\left|n\right|}

and

n-M_{\left|n\right|}=n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+\sum_{j=\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{\left|n\right|-1}n_{j}M_{j}+\left(n_{\left|n\right|}-1\right)M_{\left|n\right|},

if we apply Lemma
1.7
, Corollary
1.5
and (
1.14
) in Lemma
1.3
we can conclude that

\left|D_{n-M_{\left|n\right|}}\right|

\geq\left|\psi_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}D_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\sum_{s=m_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{m_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-1}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{s}\right|-\left|\psi_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\sum_{j=\left\langle n\right\rangle+1}^{\left|n\right|}D_{M_{j}}\sum_{s=m_{j}-n_{j}}^{m_{j}-1}r_{j}^{s}\right|

=\left|D_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\sum_{s=m_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{m_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-1}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{s}\right|=\left|D_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{m_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-\alpha_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\sum_{s=0}^{n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-1}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{s}\right|

=D_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\left|\sum_{s=0}^{n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-1}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{s}\right|\geq D_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\geq M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Analogously we can show that

\left|D_{n}\right|=D_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\left|\sum_{s=0}^{n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{s}\right|\geq M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}

and the proof is complete.

The next Lemma can be found in Lukomskii
[
38
]
:

Lemma 1.9

Let
n=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}n_{i}M_{i}
. Then

\frac{1}{4\lambda}v\left(n\right)+\frac{1}{\lambda}v^{\ast}\left(n\right)+\frac{1}{2\lambda}\leq L_{n}\leq\frac{3}{2}v\left(n\right)+4v^{\ast}\left(n\right)-1,

where
\lambda:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}.

The next result for the Walsh system can be found in the book
[
55
]
and for
bounded Vilenkin systems in the book
[
2
]
.

Corollary 1.10

Let
q_{n_{k}}

=M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2n_{k}-2}+M_{2}+M_{0}
. Then

\frac{n_{k}}{2\lambda}\leq\left\|D_{q_{n_{k}}}\right\|_{1}\leq\lambda n_{k},

where
\lambda:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}.

Proof
:
The proof readily follows by just using Theorem
1.9
and the following identity

v\left(q_{n_{k}}\right)=2n_{k}.

Thus, we leave out the details.

1.4
Fejér Kernels with respect to Vilenkin systems

It is obvious that

\sigma_{n}f\left(x\right)=\frac{1}{n}\overset{n-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\left(D_{k}\ast f\right)\left(x\right)

=\left(f\ast K_{n}\right)\left(x\right)=\int_{G_{m}}f\left(t\right)K_{n}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right).

We frequently use the following well-known result, which was proved in Gát

[
17
]
:

Lemma 1.11

Let
n>t,

t,n\in\mathbb{N}.
Then

K_{M_{n}}\left(x\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{M_{t}}{1-r_{t}\left(x\right)},&x\in I_{t}\backslash I_{t+1},\text{
}x-x_{t}e_{t}\in I_{n},\\
\frac{M_{n}+1}{2},&x\in I_{n},\\
0,&\text{otherwise. }\end{array}\right.

The proof of the next lemma can easily be done by using last lemma (c.f. also the book
[
2
]
and Tephnadze
[
67
,
68
]
:

Lemma 1.12

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},
where
k<l.

Then

K_{M_{n}}\left(x\right)=0,\,\,\text{if \thinspace\thinspace}n>l.

(1.15)

and

\left|K_{M_{n}}\left(x\right)\right|\leq cM_{k}.

(1.16)

Moreover,

\int_{G_{m}}\left|K_{M_{n}}\right|d\mu\leq c<\infty,

(1.17)

where
c
is an absolute constant.

We also need the following useful result:

Lemma 1.13

Let
t,s_{n},n\in\mathbb{N},
and
1\leq s_{n}\leq m_{n}-1
. Then

s_{n}M_{n}K_{s_{n}M_{n}}=\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}r_{n}^{i}\right)M_{n}D_{M_{n}}+\left(\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{l}\right)M_{n}K_{M_{n}}

(1.18)

and

\left|K_{s_{n}M_{n}}\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{M_{n}}{2\pi s_{n}},\text{ for }x\in I_{n+1}\left(e_{n-1}+e_{n}\right).

(1.19)

Moreover, if
x\in I_{t}\backslash I_{t+1},

x-x_{t}e_{t}\notin I_{n}

and
n>t,
then

K_{s_{n}M_{n}}(x)=0.

Remark 1.14

This result was proved by Blahota and Tephnadze
[
5
]
, but here we will give a completely
different and simpler proof.

Proof
:
We can write that

s_{n}M_{n}K_{s_{n}M_{n}}=\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}\sum_{k=lM_{n}}^{\left(l+1\right)M_{n}-1}D_{k}

(1.20)

=\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}\sum_{k=lM_{n}}^{\left(l+1\right)M_{n}-1}D_{k}=\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{n}-1}D_{k+lM_{n}}.

Let
0\leq k<M_{n}
. Then, by using (
1.13
) in Lemma
1.2
we have
that

D_{k+lM_{n}}=\sum_{m=0}^{lM_{n}-1}\psi_{m}+\sum_{m=lM_{n}}^{lM_{n}+k-1}\psi_{m}

=D_{lM_{n}}+\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}\psi_{m+lM_{n}}=D_{lM_{n}}+r_{n}^{l}\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}\psi_{m}

=\left(\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}r_{n}^{i}\right)D_{M_{n}}+r_{n}^{l}D_{k}.

According to (
1.20
) we readily get that

s_{n}M_{n}K_{s_{n}M_{n}}=\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{n}-1}D_{k+lM_{n}}

=\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{n}-1}\left(\left(\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}r_{n}^{i}\right)D_{M_{n}}+r_{n}^{l}D_{k}\right)

=\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}r_{n}^{i}\right)M_{n}D_{M_{n}}+\left(\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{l}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{M_{n}-1}D_{k}

=\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}r_{n}^{i}\right)M_{n}D_{M_{n}}+\left(\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{l}\right)M_{n}K_{M_{n}}.

The first part of the Lemma is proved.

Now, let
t,s_{n},n\in\mathbb{N},\ n>t,\ x\in I_{t}\backslash I_{t+1}
. If
x-x_{t}e_{t}\notin I_{n}
, then by combining Corollary
1.5
and Lemma
1.11
we obtain that

D_{M_{n}}(x)=K_{M_{n}}(x)=0.

(1.21)

Let
x\in I_{n+1}\left(e_{n-1}+e_{n}\right).
By Lemma
1.11
we
have that

\left|K_{M_{n}}\left(x\right)\right|=\frac{M_{n-1}}{\left|1-r_{n-1}\left(x\right)\right|}=\frac{M_{n-1}}{2\sin\pi/m_{n-1}}.

By combining Lemmas
1.7
and
1.11
and the first part of Lemma
1.13
we immediately get that

\left|s_{n}M_{n}K_{s_{n}M_{n}}\left(x\right)\right|=\left|\left(\sum_{l=0}^{s_{n}-1}r_{n}^{l}\left(x\right)\right)M_{n}K_{M_{n}}\left(x\right)\right|

\frac{M_{n}M_{n-1}}{\left|1-r_{n}\left(x\right)\right|}=\frac{M_{n}M_{n-1}}{2\sin\pi/m_{n-1}}\geq\frac{M_{n}M_{n-1}m_{n-1}}{2\pi}\geq\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{2\pi}.

Now, let
t,s_{n},n\in\mathbb{N},\ n>t,\ x\in I_{t}\backslash I_{t+1}
. If
x-x_{t}e_{t}\notin I_{n}
, then, by using the first part of Lemma
1.13
, with identities (
1.21
) we immediately get that

K_{s_{n}M_{n}}(x)=0.

The proof is complete.

In the same paper Blahota and Tephnadze
[
5
]
also proved the
following result:

Lemma 1.15

Let
n=\sum_{i=1}^{r}s_{n_{i}}M_{n_{i}}
, where
n_{1}>n_{2}>\dots>n_{r}\geq 0
and
1\leq s_{n_{i}}<m_{n_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
as well as
n^{(k)}=n-\sum_{i=1}^{k}s_{n_{i}}M_{n_{i}}
,
where
0<k\leq r
. Then

nK_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}r_{n_{j}}^{s_{n_{j}}}\right)s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}K_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}+\sum_{k=1}^{r-1}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}r_{n_{j}}^{s_{n_{j}}}\right)n^{(k)}D_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}.

Proof
:
Let
k,n\in\mathbb{N},\ 0\leq k<M_{n}
. If we use the identity (
1.11
)
in Lemma
1.2
we readily get that

nK_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}D_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}D_{k}+\sum_{k=s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}+1}^{n}D_{k}

=s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}K_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n^{(1)}}D_{k+s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}

=s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}K_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n^{(1)}}\left(D_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}+r_{n_{1}}^{s_{n_{1}}}D_{k}\right)

=s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}K_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}+n^{(1)}D_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}+r_{n_{1}}^{s_{n_{1}}}n^{(1)}K_{n^{(1)}}.

If we calculate
n^{(1)}K_{n^{(1)}}
in similar way, we get that

n^{(1)}K_{n^{(1)}}=s_{n_{2}}M_{n_{2}}K_{s_{n_{2}}M_{n_{2}}}+n^{(2)}D_{s_{n_{2}}M_{n_{2}}}+r_{n_{2}}^{s_{n_{2}}}n^{(2)}K_{n^{(2)}},

so

nK_{n}=s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}K_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}+r_{n_{1}}^{s_{n_{1}}}s_{n_{2}}M_{n_{2}}K_{s_{n_{2}}M_{n_{2}}}

+r_{n_{1}}^{s_{n_{1}}}r_{n_{2}}^{s_{n_{2}}}n^{(2)}K_{n^{(2)}}+n^{(1)}D_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}+r_{n_{1}}^{s_{n_{1}}}n^{(2)}D_{s_{n_{2}}M_{n_{2}}}.

By using this method successively with
n^{(2)}K_{n^{(2)}},\dots,n^{(r-1)}K_{n^{(r-1)}}
, we obtain that

nK_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}r_{n_{j}}^{s_{n_{j}}}\right)s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}K_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}

+\left(\prod_{j=1}^{r}r_{n_{j}}^{s_{n_{j}}}\right)n^{(r)}K_{n^{(r)}}+\sum_{k=1}^{r-1}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}r_{n_{j}}^{s_{n_{j}}}\right)n^{(k)}D_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}.

Since
n^{(r)}=0
we conclude that the proof is complete.

Corollary 1.16

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Then

n\left|K_{n}\right|\leq c\sum_{l=\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{\left|n\right|}M_{l}\left|K_{M_{l}}\right|\leq c\sum_{l=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{l}\left|K_{M_{l}}\right|

(1.22)

and

\sup_{n}\int_{G_{m}}\left|K_{n}\right|d\mu\leq c<\infty,

(1.23)

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Remark 1.17

Corollary
1.16
is known (see the book
[
2
]
), but it is also a simple consequence of
Lemmas
1.12
and
1.15
.

The next lemma can be found as a part of a more general result by Persson and Tephnadze
[
53
]
:

Lemma 1.18

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\left\langle n\right\rangle\neq\left|n\right|
and
x\in I_{\left\langle n\right\rangle+1}\left(e_{\left\langle n\right\rangle-1}+e_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\right).
Then

\left|nK_{n}\right|=\left|\left(n-M_{\left|n\right|}\right)K_{-M_{\left|n\right|}}\right|\geq\frac{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{2}}{2\pi\lambda},

where
\lambda:=\sup m_{n}.

Proof
:
Let
x\in

I_{{}_{\left\langle n\right\rangle+1}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle-1,\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Since

n=n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+\sum_{j=\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{\left|n\right|-1}n_{j}M_{j}+n_{\left|n\right|}M_{\left|n\right|}

and

n-M_{\left|n\right|}=n_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+\sum_{j=\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{\left|n\right|-1}n_{j}M_{j}+\left(n_{\left|n\right|}-1\right)M_{\left|n\right|},

if we combine (
1.13
), (
1.19
) and invoke Corollary
1.5
, Lemmas
1.11
and
1.15
we obtain that

n\left|K_{n}\right|=\left(n-M_{\left|n\right|}\right)\left|K_{n-M_{\left|n\right|}}\right|

=\left|\left(\overset{\left\langle n\right\rangle-1}{\underset{j=1}{\prod}}\psi_{M_{n_{j}}}^{j}\right)s_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}K_{s_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\right|=\left|s_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}K_{s_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\right|

=\left|\sum_{l=0}^{s_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-1}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{i}\right)M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}D_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}+\left(\sum_{l=0}^{s_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-1}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{l}\right)M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}K_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\right|

=\left|\left(\sum_{l=0}^{s_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}-1}r_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{l}\right)M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}K_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\right|\geq\left|M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}K_{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}\right|\geq\frac{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{2}}{2\pi\lambda}.

The proof is complete.

The next result is proved in Blahota, Gát and Goginava

[
9
]
and
[
10
]
, (see also Tephnadze
[
70
]
):

Lemma 1.19

Let
2<n\in\mathbb{N}_{+},

k\leq s<n
and
q_{n}=M_{2n}+M_{2n-2}+...+M_{2}+M_{0}.
Then

q_{n-1}\left|K_{q_{n-1}}(x)\right|\geq\frac{M_{2k}M_{2s}}{8},

for

x\in I_{2n}\left(0,...,x_{2k}\neq 0,0,...,0,x_{2s}\neq 0,x_{2s+1},...,x_{2n-1}\right),

k=0,1,...,n-3,\text{ \ \ }s=k+2,k+3,...,n-1.

The next lemma can be found in Blahota and Tephnadze
[
5
]
(see also
Tephnadze
[
67
]
and
[
68
]
):

Lemma 1.20

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},

k=0,\dots,N-2,

l=k+1,\dots,N-1.

Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{nM_{N}}.

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},

k=0,\dots,N-1.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},
for
0\leq k<l\leq N-1
and
t\in I_{N}.
Since
x-t\in

I_{N}^{k,l}
and
n\geq M_{N},
by combining Lemma
1.11

and (
1.22
) in Corollary
1.16
we obtain that

n\left|K_{n}\left(x\right)\right|

\leq c\underset{i=0}{\overset{l}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq c\overset{l}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}M_{k}\leq cM_{k}M_{l}

and

\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{nM_{N}}.

(1.24)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N}
. Then by applying Lemma
1.11
and (
1.22
)
in Corollary
1.16
, we have that

\int_{I_{N}}n\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

(1.25)

Let

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}x=\left(0,\ldots,0,x_{k}\neq 0,\ldots,x_{N-1}=0,x_{N},x_{N+1},x_{q},...,x_{\left|n\right|-1},\ldots\right),\\
t=\left(0,\ldots,0,x_{N},\ldots,x_{q-1},t_{q}\neq x_{q},t_{q+1},\ldots,t_{\left|n\right|-1},\ldots\right),\,\,q=N,\ldots,\left|n\right|-1.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

By using Lemmas
1.11
and
1.12
in (
1.25
) it is easy to
see that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(1.26)

\leq\frac{c}{n}\underset{i=0}{\overset{q-1}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}M_{q}}{nM_{N}}\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

Let

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\text{ }x=\left(0,\ldots,0,x_{m}\neq 0,0,\ldots,0,x_{N},x_{N+1},x_{q},\ldots,x_{\left|n\right|-1},\ldots\right),\text{ }\\
t=\left(0,,\ldots,x_{N}=0,\ldots,x_{{}_{\left|n\right|-1}},\ldots\right).\end{array}\right.

If we apply again Lemmas
1.11
and
1.12
in (
1.25
) we
obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c}{n}\overset{\left|n\right|-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

(1.27)

By combining (
1.24
), (
1.26
) and (
1.27
) we can complete
the proof.

Also the next lemma is due to Tephnadze
[
67
,
68
]
, but it
is also a simple consequence of Lemma
1.20
.

Lemma 1.21

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},

k=0,\dots,N-1,

l=k+1,\dots,N.

Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}},\text{ \ for }n\geq M_{N},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Since
n\geq M_{N}
if we apply Lemma
1.20
we immediately get a proof
of this estimate.

1.5
Nörlund Kernels with respect to Vilenkin systems

A representation

t_{n}f\left(x\right)=\underset{G}{\int}f\left(t\right)F_{n}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)

plays a central role in the sequel, where

F_{n}:=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}

is the so-called Nörlund kernel.

In this section we study Nörlund kernels with respect to Vilenkin
systems. The next results (Lemmas
1.22
-
1.27
) are due to
Persson, Tephnadze and Wall
[
51
]
:

Lemma 1.22

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of
non-decreasing numbers, satisfying the condition

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\text{ \ \ when \ \ }\ n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.28)

Then

\left|F_{n}\right|\leq\frac{c}{n}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|\right\},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
First, we invoke Abel transformation to obtain the following
identities

Q_{n}:=\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}q_{j}=\overset{n}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}q_{n-j}\cdot 1=\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)j+q_{0}n

(1.29)

and

F_{n}=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)jK_{j}+q_{0}nK_{n}\right).

(1.30)

Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be non-decreasing. Then, by using (
1.28
), we get that

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right|+q_{0}\right)

\leq\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)+q_{0}\right)\leq\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{c}{n}.

Under condition (
1.28
) if we apply (
1.22
) in Corollary
1.16
and use the equalities (
1.29
) and (
1.30
) we immediately get
that

\left|F_{n}\right|\leq\left(\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right|+q_{0}\right)\right)\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

=\left(\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)+q_{0}\right)\right)\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

\leq\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|\leq\frac{c}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|.

The proof is complete by just combining the estimates above.

Corollary 1.23

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of
non-decreasing numbers. Then

\sup_{n}\int_{G_{m}}\left|F_{n}\right|d\mu\leq c<\infty,

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
If we apply (
1.23
) in Corollary
1.16
and invoke the
identities (
1.29
) and (
1.30
) we readily get the proof.
So, we leave out the details.

Lemma 1.24

Let
n\geq M_{N}
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a
sequence of non-decreasing numbers. Then

\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-j}D_{j}\right|\leq\frac{c}{M_{N}}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|\right\},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let
M_{N}\leq j\leq n.
By using (
1.22
) in Corollary
1.16
we
get that

\left|K_{j}\right|\leq\frac{1}{j}\sum_{l=0}^{\left|j\right|}M_{l}\left|K_{M_{l}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{M_{N}}\sum_{l=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{l}\left|K_{M_{l}}\right|.

Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be non-decreasing. Then

\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left|q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right|j+q_{0}n\leq\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left|q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right|j+q_{0}n

=\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)j+q_{0}n=Q_{n}.

By using Abel transformation we can write that

\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-j}D_{j}\right|

=\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)jK_{j}+q_{0}nK_{n}\right)\right|

\left(\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left|q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right|j+q_{0}n\right)\right)\frac{1}{M_{N}}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

\leq\frac{1}{M_{N}}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|.

The proof is complete.

Lemma 1.25

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},

k=0,\dots,N-2,

l=k+1,\dots,N-1

and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-decreasing numbers,
satisfying condition (
1.28
). Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{nM_{N}}.

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},

k=0,\dots,N-1.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

Here
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},
for
0\leq k<l\leq N-1
and
t\in I_{N}.
First, we observe that
x-t\in

I_{N}^{k,l}.
Next, we apply Lemma
1.22
and invoke (
1.15
) and (
1.16
) in Lemma
1.12

to obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(1.31)

\leq\frac{c}{n}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{c}{n}\int_{I_{N}}\overset{l}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{nM_{N}}

and the first estimate is proved.

Now, let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N}
. Since
x-t\in I_{N}^{k,N}
for
t\in I_{N},
by
combining Lemmas
1.11
and
1.22
with (
1.15
) and (
1.16
) in
1.12
, we have that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(1.32)

\leq\frac{c}{n}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{c}{n}\overset{\left|n\right|-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

By combining (
1.31
) and (
1.32
) we complete the proof.

Lemma 1.26

Let
n\geq M_{N},

\ x\in I_{N}^{k,l},

k=0,\dots,N-1,

l=k+1,\dots,N
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of
non-decreasing sequence, satisfying condition (
1.28
). Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Since
n\geq M_{N}
if we apply Lemma
1.25
we immediately get the proof.

Next, we state analogical estimate, but now without any restriction like (
1.28
):

Lemma 1.27

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},

k=0,\dots,N-1,

l=k+1,\dots,N

and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-decreasing sequence.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},
for
0\leq k<l\leq N-1
and
t\in I_{N}.
Since
x-t\in

I_{N}^{k,l}
and
n\geq M_{N},
if we combine Lemmas
1.11
and
1.24
and invoke (
1.15
) and (
1.16
) in Lemma
1.12
we readily obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(1.33)

\leq\frac{c}{M_{N}}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{c}{M_{N}}\overset{l}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{M_{N}^{2}}

and the first estimate is proved.

Now, let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N}
. Since
x-t\in I_{N}^{k,N}
for
t\in I_{N},
if
we apply again Lemmas
1.11
,
1.22
and (
1.15
) and (
1.16
) in Lemma
1.12
we can conclude that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(1.34)

\leq\frac{c}{M_{N}}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{c}{M_{N}}\overset{\left|n\right|-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

By combining (
1.33
) and (
1.34
) we complete the proof.

The next results (Lemmas
1.28
-
1.34
) are due to
Blahota, Persson and Tephnadze
[
7
]
:

Lemma 1.28

Let
s_{n}M_{n}<r\leq\left(s_{n}+1\right)M_{n},
where
1\leq s_{n}\leq m_{n}-1.
Then

Q_{r}F_{r}=Q_{r}D_{s_{n}M_{n}}-\psi_{s_{n}M_{n}-1}\overset{s_{n}M_{n}-2}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}+l}-q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}+l+1}\right)l\overline{K_{l}}

-\psi_{s_{n}M_{n}-1}\left(s_{n}M_{n}-1\right)q_{r-1}\overline{K_{s_{n}M_{n}-1}}+\psi_{s_{n}M_{n}}Q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}}F_{r-s_{n}M_{n}}.

Remark 1.29

We note that Lemma
1.28
is true for every Nörlund mean, without
any restriction on the generative sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}.

Proof
:
Let
s_{n}M_{n}<r\leq\left(s_{n}+1\right)M_{n},
where
1\leq s_{n}\leq m_{n}-1.
It is easy to see that

Q_{r}F_{r}=\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}q_{r-k}D_{k}=\overset{s_{n}M_{n}}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}q_{r-l}D_{l}+\overset{r}{\underset{l=s_{n}M_{n}+1}{\sum}}q_{r-l}D_{l}:=I+II.

(1.35)

We apply (
1.12
) in Lemma
1.2
and invoke Abel transformation to
obtain that

I=\overset{s_{n}M_{n}-1}{\underset{l=0}{\sum}}q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}+l}D_{s_{n}M_{n}-l}

(1.36)

=\overset{s_{n}M_{n}-1}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}+l}D_{s_{n}M_{n}-l}+q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}}D_{s_{n}M_{n}}

=D_{s_{n}M_{n}}\overset{s_{n}M_{n}-1}{\underset{l=0}{\sum}}q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}+l}-\psi_{s_{n}M_{n}-1}\overset{s_{n}M_{n}-1}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}+l}\overline{D_{l}}

=\left(Q_{r}-Q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}}\right)D_{s_{n}M_{n}}

-\psi_{s_{n}M_{n}-1}\overset{s_{n}M_{n}-2}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}+l}-q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}+l+1}\right)l\overline{K_{l}}

-\psi_{s_{n}M_{n}-1}q_{r-1}\left(s_{n}M_{n}-1\right)\overline{K_{s_{n}M_{n}-1}}.

By using (
1.11
) in Lemma
1.2
we can rewrite
II
as

II=\overset{r-s_{n}M_{n}}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}-l}D_{l+s_{n}M_{n}}

(1.37)

=Q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}}D_{s_{n}M_{n}}+\psi_{s_{n}M_{n}}Q_{r-s_{n}M_{n}}F_{r-s_{n}M_{n}}.

The proof is complete by just combining (
1.35
-
1.37
).

Lemma 1.30

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of
non-increasing numbers satisfying the condition

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\text{ \ \ when \ \ }\ n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.38)

Then

\left|F_{n}\right|\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{n}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|\right\},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be non-increasing satisfying
condition (
1.38
). Then

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right|+q_{0}\right)

\leq\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}-\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)+q_{0}\right)

\leq\frac{2q_{0}-q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{2q_{0}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{c}{n}.

If we apply (
1.22
) in Corollary
1.16
and invoke equalities (
1.29
) and (
1.30
) we immediately get that

\left|F_{n}\right|\leq\left(\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right|+q_{0}\right)\right)\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

=\left(\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}-\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)+q_{0}\right)\right)\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

\leq\frac{2q_{0}-q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|\leq\frac{2q_{0}}{Q_{n}}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

\leq\frac{c}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|.

The proof is complete by combining the estimates above.

Corollary 1.31

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of
non-increasing numbers satisfying condition (
1.38
). Then

\sup_{n}\int_{G_{m}}\left|F_{n}\right|d\mu\leq c_{\alpha}<\infty,

where
c_{\alpha}
is an absolute constant depending only on
\alpha.

Proof
:
By applying Lemma
1.30
we readily get the proof. So,
we leave out the details.

Lemma 1.32

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of
non-increasing numbers,
0<\alpha<1,\ \
and

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{\alpha}}\right),\text{ \ \ when }\ n\rightarrow\infty

(1.39)

and

q_{n}-q_{n+1}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2-\alpha}}\right),\text{ \ when }\ n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.40)

Then

\left|F_{n}\right|\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{n^{\alpha}}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{j}^{\alpha}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|\right\},

where
c_{\alpha}
is an absolute constant depending only on
\alpha.

Proof
:
According to the fact that
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is a sequence of non-negative and
non-increasing numbers we have two cases:

\begin{array}[]{ll}1.&\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}q_{k}\geq c>0,\\
2.&\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}q_{k}=0,\end{array}

In the first case we obtain that (
1.4
) and (
1.5
) are
satisfied. Since the case

\frac{n}{Q_{n}}=O\left(1\right),\ \text{when }\ n\rightarrow\infty,

have already been considered in Lemma
1.22
, we can exclude it.

Hence, we may assume that

q_{n}=o(1),\text{ \ \ \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.41)

By combining (
1.40
) and (
1.41
) we immediately get that

q_{n}=\overset{\infty}{\underset{l=n}{\sum}}\left(q_{l}-q_{l+1}\right)\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{l=n}{\sum}}\frac{c}{l^{2-\alpha}}\leq\frac{c}{n^{1-\alpha}}

and

Q_{n}=\overset{n-1}{\underset{l=0}{\sum}}q_{l}\leq\overset{n}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}\frac{c}{l^{1-\alpha}}\leq cn^{\alpha}.

Let
M_{n}<k\leq M_{n+1}.
It is easy to see that

Q_{k}\left|D_{sM_{n}}\right|\leq cM_{n}^{\alpha}\left|D_{sM_{n}}\right|

(1.42)

and

\left(sM_{n}-1\right)q_{k-1}\left|K_{sM_{n}-1}\right|

(1.43)

\leq ck^{\alpha-1}M_{n}\left|K_{sM_{n}-1}\right|\leq cM_{n}^{\alpha}\left|K_{sM_{n}-1}\right|.

Let

n=s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}+s_{n_{2}}M_{n_{2}}+\dots+s_{n_{r}}M_{n_{r}},\ \ n_{1}>n_{2}>\dots>n_{r},

and

n^{\left(k\right)}=s_{n_{k+1}}M_{n_{k+1}}+\dots+s_{n_{r}}M_{n_{r}},\ 1\leq s_{n_{l}}\leq m_{l}-1,\ l=1,\dots,r.

By combining (
1.42
), (
1.43
) and Lemma
1.28
we have that

\left|Q_{n}F_{n}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\left(M_{n_{1}}^{\alpha}\left|D_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}}\right|+\overset{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}-1}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}\left|\left(n^{\left(1\right)}+l\right)^{\alpha-2}\right|\left|lK_{l}\right|+M_{n_{1}}^{\alpha}\left|K_{s_{n_{1}}M_{n_{1}}-1}\right|\right).

+c_{\alpha}\left|Q_{n^{\left(1\right)}}F_{n^{\left(1\right)}}\right|

By repeating this process
r
times we get that

\left|Q_{n}F_{n}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left(M_{n_{k}}^{\alpha}\left|D_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}\right|+\overset{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}-1}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}\left(n^{\left(k\right)}+l\right)^{\alpha-2}\left|lK_{l}\right|+M_{n_{k}}^{\alpha}\left|K_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}-1}\right|\right)

:=I+II+III.

We combine Corollary
1.5
and Lemma
1.11
and invoke (
1.13
) in Lemma
1.3
to obtain that

I\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{\left|n\right|}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha}\left|D_{s_{k}M_{k}}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{\left|n\right|}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha}\left|D_{M_{k}}\right|\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{\left|n\right|}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha}\left|K_{M_{k}}\right|

and

III\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{n_{k}}^{\alpha-1}\left|M_{n_{k}}K_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}-M_{n_{k}}D_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{n_{k}}^{\alpha}\left|K_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}\right|+c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{n_{k}}^{\alpha}\left|D_{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha}\left|K_{M_{k}}\right|.

Next, we can rewrite
II
as

II=c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\overset{s_{n_{k+1}}M_{n_{k+1}}-1}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}\left(n^{\left(k\right)}+l\right)^{\alpha-2}\left|lK_{l}\right|

+c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\overset{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}-1}{\underset{l=s_{n_{k+1}}M_{n_{k+1}}}{\sum}}\left(n^{\left(k\right)}+l\right)^{\alpha-2}\left|lK_{l}\right|

:=II_{1}+II_{2}.

For
II_{1}
we find that

II_{1}\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}s_{n_{k+1}}^{\alpha-2}M_{n_{k+1}}^{\alpha-2}\overset{s_{n_{k+1}}M_{n_{k+1}}-1}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}\left|lK_{l}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha-2}\overset{M_{k}-1}{\underset{l=1}{\sum}}\left|lK_{l}\right|

=c_{\alpha}\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha-2}\overset{k}{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}\overset{M_{i}-1}{\underset{l=M_{i-1}}{\sum}}\left|lK_{l}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha-2}\overset{k}{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}M_{i}\overset{i}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha-1}\overset{k}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}M_{j}|K_{M_{j}}|

=c_{\alpha}\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}M_{j}|K_{M_{j}}|\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{k=j}{\sum}}M_{k}^{\alpha-1}

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}M_{j}^{\alpha}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|.

Moreover,

II_{2}\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\overset{s_{n_{k}}M_{n_{k}}-1}{\underset{l=s_{n_{k+1}}M_{n_{k+1}}}{\sum}}l^{\alpha-2}\left|lK_{l}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\overset{n_{k}}{\underset{i=n_{k+1}+1}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha-2}\overset{M_{i+1}-1}{\underset{l=M_{i}}{\sum}}\left|lK_{l}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\overset{n_{k}}{\underset{i=n_{k+1}+1}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha-2}M_{i}\overset{i}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|

=c_{\alpha}\overset{r}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\overset{n_{k}}{\underset{i=n_{k+1}+1}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha-1}\overset{i}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha-1}\overset{i}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|

\leq c_{\alpha}\overset{n_{1}}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}M_{j}^{\alpha}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|.

The proof is complete by just combining the estimates above.

Corollary 1.33

Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be
a sequence of non-increasing numbers satisfying the conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
). Then

\sup_{n}\int_{G_{m}}\left|F_{n}\right|d\mu\leq c_{\alpha}<\infty,

where
c_{\alpha}
is an absolute constant depending only on
\alpha.

Proof
:
If we use Lemma
1.32
we readily get the proof. Thus, we leave out the details.

Lemma 1.34

Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a
sequence of non-increasing numbers satisfying the conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
). Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{l}^{\alpha}M_{k}}{m^{\alpha}M_{N}},\text{
\ for }x\in I_{N}^{k,l},

where
k=0,\dots,N-2,

l=k+2,\dots,N-1
. Moreover,

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{k}}{M_{N}},\text{ \ {for} \ }x\in I_{N}^{k,N},\text{ \ }k=0,\dots,N-1.

Here
c_{\alpha}
is an absolute constant depending only on
\alpha.

Proof
:
Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},
where
k=0,\dots,N-2,

l=k+2,\dots,N-1.

Since
x-t\in I_{N}^{k,l},
for
t\in I_{N}
if we apply Lemma
1.32
with (
1.15
) and (
1.16
) in Lemma
1.12
we
can conclude that

\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}}\overset{l}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|

(1.44)

\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}}\overset{l}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha}M_{k}\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{l}^{\alpha}M_{k}}{m^{\alpha}}.

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},
for some
0\leq k<l\leq N-1.
Since
x-t\in I_{N}^{k,l},
for
t\in

I_{N}
and
m\geq M_{N}
from (
1.44
) we
readily obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{l}^{\alpha}M_{k}}{m^{\alpha}M_{N}}.

(1.45)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},

k=0,\dots,N-1.
Then, by applying Lemma
1.32
, we have that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(1.46)

\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|m\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},

k=0,\dots,N-1,

t\in

I_{N}
and
x_{q}\neq t_{q},
where
N\leq q\leq\left|m\right|-1.
By using Lemma
1.11
and estimate (
1.46
) we get that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(1.47)

\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}}\underset{i=0}{\overset{q-1}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{q}^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}M_{N}}\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{k}}{M_{N}}.

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},

k=0,\dots,N-1,

t\in I_{N}
and
x_{N}=t_{N},\dots,x_{\left|m\right|-1}=t_{\left|m\right|-1}.
By again applying Lemma
1.11
and estimate (
1.46
) we
have that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}}\overset{\left|m\right|-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}^{\alpha}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{k}}{M_{N}}.

(1.48)

The proof follows by combining (
1.45
), (
1.47
) and (
1.48
).

Corollary 1.35

Let
0<\alpha<1,

m\geq M_{N}
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers satisfying the conditions
(
1.39
) and (
1.40
). Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{\alpha}
, depending only on
\alpha,
such that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{l}^{\alpha}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{1+\alpha}},\text{ \ for }x\in I_{N}^{k,l}

where
k=0,\dots,N-1,

l=k+2,\dots,N
.

Proof
:
The proof readily follows Lemma
1.34

if we use additional condition
n\geq M_{N}
.

Remark 1.36

For some sequences
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
of non-increasing numbers
conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
) can be true or false independently.

1.6
Introduction to the theory of Martingale Hardy spaces

The
\sigma
-algebra generated by the intervals

\left\{I_{n}\left(x\right):x\in G_{m}\right\}

will be denoted by
\digamma_{n}\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right).

A sequence
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
of
integrable functions
f^{\left(n\right)}
is said to be a martingale with
respect to the
\sigma
-algebras
\digamma_{n}\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
if (for details see e.g. Weisz
[
83
]
)

1)

f_{n}
is
\digamma_{n}
measurable for all
n\in\mathbb{N},

2)

S_{M_{n}}f_{m}=f_{n}
for all
n\leq m.

The martingale
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is
said to be
L_{p}
-bounded (
0<p\leq\infty
) if
f^{\left(n\right)}\in L_{p}
and

\left\|f\right\|_{p}:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|f_{n}\right\|_{p}<\infty.

If
f\in L_{1}\left(G_{m}\right),
then it is easy to show that the
sequence
F=\left(E_{n}f:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is a martingale. This
type of martingales is called regular. If
1\leq p\leq\infty
and
f\in L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
then
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is
L_{p}
-bounded and

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\left\|E_{n}f-f\right\|_{p}=0

and consequently
\left\|F\right\|_{p}=\left\|f\right\|_{p}

(see
[
46
]
). The converse of the latest statement holds also if
1<p\leq\infty
(see
[
46
]
): for an arbitrary
L_{p}
-bounded martingale

f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
there exists a
function
f\in L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
for which
f^{\left(n\right)}=E_{n}f.
If
p=1,
then there exists a function
f\in L_{1}\left(G_{m}\right)
of the preceding type if and only if
f
is uniformly
integrable (see
[
46
]
), namely, if

\underset{y\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\int_{\left\{\left|f_{n}\right|>y\right\}}\left|f_{n}\left(x\right)\right|d\mu\left(x\right)=0.

Thus the map
f\rightarrow f:=

\left(E_{n}f:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is
isometric from
L_{p}
onto the space of
L_{p}
-bounded martingales when
1<p\leq\infty.
Consequently, these two spaces can be identified with each
other. Similarly, the space
L_{1}\left(G_{m}\right)
can be identified
with the space of uniformly integrable martingales.

Analogously, the martingale
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is said to be
weak-L_{p}
-bounded (
0<p\leq\infty
) if
f^{\left(n\right)}\in L_{p}
and

\left\|f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|f_{n}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}<\infty.

The maximal function of a martingale
f
is defined by

f^{\ast}:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|f^{(n)}\right|.

In the case
f\in L_{1}(G_{m}),
the maximal functions are also given by

f^{\ast}\left(x\right):=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{\left|I_{n}\left(x\right)\right|}\left|\int_{I_{n}\left(x\right)}f\left(u\right)d\mu\left(u\right)\right|.

For
0<p<\infty
the Hardy martingale spaces
H_{p}
consist of all
martingales for which

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}:=\left\|f^{\ast}\right\|_{p}<\infty.

If
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is a martingale, then the Vilenkin-Fourier coefficients must be
defined in a slightly different manner:

\widehat{f}\left(i\right):=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{G_{m}}f^{\left(k\right)}\overline{\psi}_{i}d\mu.

The next Lemma can be found in
[
84
]
(see also book
[
55
]
):

Lemma 1.37

If
f\in L_{1},
then the sequence
F:=\left(S_{M_{n}}f:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is a martingale and

\left\|F\right\|_{H_{p}}\sim\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{M_{n}}f\right|\right\|_{p}.

Moreover, if
F:=

\left(S_{M_{n}}f:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is a regular
martingale generated by
f\in L_{1},
then

\widehat{F}\left(k\right)=\int_{G}f\left(x\right)\psi_{k}\left(x\right)d\mu\left(x\right)=\widehat{f}\left(k\right),\text{ \qquad}k\in\mathbb{N}.

A bounded measurable function
a
is a p-atom if there exist an interval
I

such that

\int_{I}ad\mu=0,\text{ \ \ }\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq\mu\left(I\right)^{-1/p},\text{ \ \ supp}\left(a\right)\subset I.

Next, we note that the Hardy martingale spaces
H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)

for
0<p\leq 1
have atomic characterizations (see e.g. Weisz
[
83
,
84
]
):

Lemma 1.38

A martingale
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is in
H_{p}\left(0<p\leq 1\right)
if and only if there exist a
sequence
\left(a_{k},k\in\mathbb{N}\right)
of p-atoms and a sequence
\left(\mu_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right)
of real numbers such that, for
every
n\in\mathbb{N},

\qquad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mu_{k}S_{M_{n}}a_{k}=f^{\left(n\right)},\text{
\ \ a.e.,}

(1.49)

where

\qquad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\mu_{k}\right|^{p}<\infty.

Moreover,

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}\backsim\inf\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\mu_{k}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p},

where the infimum is taken over all decomposition of
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
of the form (
1.49
).

By using atomic characterization it can be easily proved that the following
Lemmas hold (see e.g. Weisz
[
84
]
):

Lemma 1.39

Suppose that an operator
T
is sub-linear and for some
0<p\leq 1

\int\limits_{\overset{-}{I}}\left|Ta\right|^{p}d\mu\leq c_{p}<\infty

for every
p
-atom
a
, where
I
denotes the support of the atom. If
T
is bounded from
L_{\infty\text{ }}
to
L_{\infty},
then

\left\|Tf\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

Moreover, if
p<1,
then we have the weak (1,1) type estimate

\lambda\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\text{ }\left|Tf\left(x\right)\right|>\lambda\right\}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{1}

for all
f\in L_{1}.

Lemma 1.40

Suppose that an operator
T
is sub-linear and for some
0<p\leq 1

\underset{\lambda>0}{\sup}\lambda^{p}\mu\left\{x\in\overset{-}{I}:\text{ }\left|Tf\right|>\lambda\right\}\leq c_{p}<+\infty

for every
p
-atom
a
, where
I
denote
the support of the atom. If
T
is bounded from
L_{\infty\text{
}}
to
L_{\infty},
then

\left\|Tf\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

Moreover, if
p<1,
then

\lambda\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\text{ }\left|Tf\left(x\right)\right|>\lambda\right\}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{1},

for all
f\in L_{1}.

The concept of modulus of continuity in
H_{p}

\left(p>0\right)

is defined by

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right):=\left\|f-S_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

We need to understand the meaning of the expression
f-S_{M_{n}}f
where
f
is a martingale and
S_{M_{n}}f
is function. So, we give an explanation in the following remark:

Remark 1.41

Let
0<p\leq 1.
Since

S_{M_{n}}f=f^{\left(n\right)},\text{ \ for }f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p}

and

\left(S_{M_{k}}f^{\left(n\right)}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right)=\left(S_{M_{k}}S_{M_{n}}f,k\in\mathbb{N}\right)

=\left(S_{M_{0}}f,\ldots,S_{M_{n-1}}f,S_{M_{n}}f,S_{M_{n}}f,\ldots\right)

=\left(f^{\left(0\right)},\ldots,f^{\left(n-1\right)},f^{\left(n\right)},f^{\left(n\right)},\ldots\right)

we obtain that

f-S_{M_{n}}f=\left(f^{\left(k\right)}-S_{M_{k}}f:k\in\mathbb{N}\right)

is a martingale, for which

\left(f-S_{M_{n}}f\right)^{\left(k\right)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&k=0,.\ldots,n,\\
f^{\left(k\right)}-f^{\left(n\right)},&k\geq n+1,\end{array}\right.

(1.50)

Watari
[
79
]
showed that there are strong connections between

\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right),\text{ \ }E_{M_{n}}\left(L_{p},f\right)\text{ \ \ and\ \ \ }\left\|f-S_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{p},\ \ p\geq 1,\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}.

In particular,

\frac{1}{2}\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)\leq\left\|f-S_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{p}\leq\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)

(1.51)

and

\frac{1}{2}\left\|f-S_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{p}\leq E_{M_{n}}\left(L_{p},f\right)\leq\left\|f-S_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{p}.

Remark 1.42

Since

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}\sim\left\|f\right\|_{p},

when
p>1
, by applying (
1.51
), we obtain that

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)\sim\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right).

1.7
Examples of
p
-atoms and
H_{p}
maringales

The next two Examples can be found in the papers
[
9
]
and
[
10
]

by Blahota, Gát and Goginava (see also :

Example 1.43

Let
0<p\leq 1
and
\lambda=\sup_{n}m_{n}.
Then the
function

a_{k}:=\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}}\right)

is a p-atom. Moreover,

\left\|a_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq 1.

Proof
:
Here we present the proof from Tephnadze

[
67
]

and
[
68
]
. Since

\text{supp}(a_{k})=I_{\alpha_{k}},\text{ \ \ }\int_{I_{\alpha_{k}}}a_{k}d\mu=0

and

\left\|a_{k}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}M_{\alpha_{k}+1}\leq M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}=(\text{supp }a_{k})^{-1/p},

we conclude that
a_{k}
is a p-atom.

Moreover, by using the orthonormality of Vilenkin functions, we find that

S_{M_{n}}\left(D_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&n=0,...,\alpha_{k},\\
D_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}},&n\geq\alpha_{k}+1,\end{array}\right.

and

\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{M_{n}}\left(D_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}\left(x\right)-D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\left(x\right)\right)\right|

=\left|D_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}\left(x\right)-D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\left(x\right)\right|,\text{ \ for all \ }x\in G_{m}.

If we invoke Lemma
1.37
we obtain that

\left\|a_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}=\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left\|D_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right\|_{H_{p}}

=\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left\|D_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right\|_{p}

=\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(\int_{I_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}\backslash\text{ }I_{{}_{\alpha_{k}+1}}}M_{\alpha_{k}}^{p}d\mu+\int_{I_{{}_{\alpha_{k}+1}}}\left(M_{\alpha_{k}+1}-M_{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{p}d\mu\right)^{1/p}

=\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(\frac{m_{\alpha_{k}}-1}{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}M_{\alpha_{k}}^{p}+\frac{\left(m_{\alpha_{k}}-1\right)^{p}}{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}M_{\alpha_{k}}^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\cdot M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1-1/p}\leq 1.

The proof is complete.

Example 1.44

Let
0<p\leq 1
and

f_{k}=D_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}.

Then

\widehat{f}_{k}\left(i\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&i=M_{{}_{2n_{k}}},...,M_{{}_{2n_{k}+1}}-1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise,}\end{array}\right.

(1.52)

and

S_{i}f_{k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}D_{i}-D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}},&i=M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1,...,M_{{}_{2n_{k}+1}}-1,\\
f_{k},&i\geq M_{2n_{k}+1},\\
0,&\text{otherwise. }\end{array}\right.

(1.53)

Moreover,

\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\lambda M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1-1/p},

(1.54)

where
\lambda=\sup_{n}m_{n}.

Proof
:
The proof follows by using Example
1.43
in the case when
\alpha_{k}=2n_{k}
. We leave out the details.

The next three examples of regular martingales will be used frequently and
it can be found in Persson and Tephnadze
[
52
]
:

Example 1.45

Let
M_{k}\leq n<M_{k+1}
and
S_{n}f
be the n-th partial sum
with respect to Vilenkin systems, where
f\in H_{p}
for some
0<p\leq 1.

Then
S_{n}f\in L_{1}
for every fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}
and

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{M_{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}

\leq\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}.

Proof
:
We consider the following martingale

f_{\#}=\left(S_{M_{l}}S_{n},\text{ }l\in\mathbb{N}\right)

f_{\#}:=\left(S_{M_{k}}S_{n}f,\text{ }k\geq 1\right)=\left(S_{M_{0}},...,S_{M_{k}}f,...,\text{ }S_{n}f,...,S_{n}f,..\right).

It immediately follows that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{M_{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}.

The proof is complete.

Example 1.46

Let
M_{k}\leq n<M_{k+1}
and
\sigma_{n}f
be n-th Fejér
means with respect to Vilenkin systems, where
f\in H_{p}
for some
0<p\leq 1.
Then
\sigma_{n}f\in L_{1}
for every fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}
and

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|\sigma_{M_{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|\widetilde{\sigma}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}.

Proof
:
We consider the following martingale

f_{\#}=\left(S_{M_{k}}\sigma_{n}f,\text{ }k\geq 1\right)

=\left(\frac{M_{0}\sigma_{M_{0}}}{n},...,\frac{M_{k}\sigma_{M_{k}}f}{n},...,\text{ }\sigma_{n}f,...,\sigma_{n}f,...\right).

and if we follow analogous steps as in Example
1.45
we readily can
complete the proof. Thus, we leave out the details.

The next five Examples of martingales will be used many times to prove
sharpness of our main results (c.f. the papers
[
66
]
,
[
69
]
,

[
71
]
,
[
72
]
,
[
73
]
by Tephnadze).

Example 1.47

Let
0<p\leq 1,

\lambda=\sup_{n}m_{n},

\left\{\lambda_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a sequence of real numbers
\mathbb{R}
, such that

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|^{p}\leq c_{p}<\infty

(1.55)

and
\left\{a_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a sequence of
p
-atoms,
defined by

a_{k}:=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}\right).

(1.56)

Then
\,f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right),

where

f^{\left(n\right)}:=\sum_{\left\{k:\text{ }2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k}

is a martingale,
f\in H_{p}\
and

\widehat{f}(j)

(1.57)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\lambda_{k}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda},&j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

Moreover,

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=O\left(\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }2\alpha_{k}\geq n\right\}}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p},\text{\ \ when \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty,

(1.58)

for all
0<p\leq 1.

Let
M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}\leq j\leq M_{2\alpha_{l}},

l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.
Then

S_{j}f=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}}=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{\lambda_{\eta}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right).

(1.59)

Let
M_{2\alpha_{l}}\leq j<M_{2\alpha_{l}+1},

l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.
Then

S_{j}f=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}+\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{2\alpha_{l}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}}{\lambda}

(1.60)

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{\lambda_{\eta}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}}{\lambda}.

Proof
:
Since

S_{M_{n}}a_{k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}a_{k},&\alpha_{k}<n,\\
0,&\alpha_{k}\geq n,\end{array}\right.

by applying Lemma
1.38
and (
1.55
) we can conclude that
f\in H_{p}.
Furthermore, in view of (
1.50
) in Remark
1.41

we immediately get also the estimate of
H_{p}
modulus of continuity of the
martingale
f
.

Let
j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}.
By using orthonormality
of Vilenkin functions we find that

\widehat{a_{k}}(j)=\int_{G_{m}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}\right)\overline{\psi}_{i}d\mu=0

and

\widehat{f}(j)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{G_{m}}\sum_{\left\{k:\text{
}2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k}\overline{\psi}_{i}d\mu

(1.61)

=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\left\{k:\text{ }2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}\int_{G_{m}}a_{k}\overline{\psi}_{i}d\mu=0.

Let
j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, for every
l\in\mathbb{N}
we can
conclude that

\widehat{a_{l}}(j)=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\int_{G_{m}}\left(D_{M_{2\alpha_{l}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}\right)\overline{\psi}_{i}d\mu=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\delta_{l,k},

where
\delta_{l,k}
is Kronecker symbol. It follows that

\widehat{f}(j)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\left\{l:\text{ }2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{l}\int_{G_{m}}a_{l}\overline{\psi}_{i}d\mu

(1.62)

=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\left\{l:\text{ }2\alpha_{l}<n\right\}}\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\delta_{l,k}=\frac{\lambda_{k}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}.

Let
M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}+1\leq

j\leq M_{2\alpha_{l}}.
In the view of (
1.59
) we can conclude that

S_{j}f=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}}f=\sum_{v=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}

(1.63)

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\sum_{v=M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}^{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\frac{\lambda_{\eta}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\psi_{v}

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{\lambda_{\eta}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right).

Let
M_{2\alpha_{l}}\leq j<M_{2\alpha_{l}+1}.
We apply (
1.60
)
and invoke (
1.11
) in Lemma
1.2
to obtain that

S_{j}f=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}f+\sum_{v=M_{2\alpha_{l}}}^{j-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}

(1.64)

=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}f+\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\sum_{v=M_{2\alpha_{l}}}^{j-1}\psi_{v}

=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}f+\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}\right)

=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}f+\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}}{\lambda}.

If we take (
1.63
) obtained by
j=M_{2\alpha_{l}}
into
account we can rewrite (
1.64
) as

S_{j}f=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{\lambda_{\eta}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)

+\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}\right).

By using (
1.50
) we get that

\left(f-S_{M_{n}}f\right)^{\left(k\right)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&k=0,.\ldots,n,\\
f^{\left(k\right)}-f^{\left(n\right)},&k\geq n+1,\end{array}\right.

(1.65)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&k=0,\ldots,n,\\
\sum_{l=n+1}^{k}\mu_{l}S_{M_{n}}a_{l},&k\geq n+1.\end{array}\right.

According to Lemma
1.38
we conclude that (
1.65
) is an
atomic decomposition of the martingale
f-S_{M_{n}}f\in H_{p}
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)\backsim\left(\sum_{k=n+1}^{\infty}\left|\mu_{k}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p}<\infty.

The proof is complete.

Sometimes, we will use the martingale defined in the next example.

Example 1.48

Let
0<p\leq 1,

\lambda=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n},

\left\{\lambda_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a sequence of real numbers
\mathbb{R}
, such that

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|^{p}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

(1.66)

and
\left\{a_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a sequence of
p
-atoms,
defined by

a_{k}:=\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\right),

where
\left|\alpha_{k}\right|:=\max

\{j\in\mathbb{N}:

\left(\alpha_{k}\right)_{j}\neq 0\}
and
\left(\alpha_{k}\right)_{j}

denotes the
j
-th binary coefficient of
\alpha_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.
Then
\,f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right),
where

f^{\left(n\right)}:=\sum_{\left\{k:\text{ }\left|\alpha_{k}\right|<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k}.

is a martingale,
f\in H_{p}\
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=O\left(\sum_{\left\{k:\text{ }\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\geq n\right\}}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p},\text{\ \ when \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty,

(1.67)

for all
0<p\leq 1.
Moreover

\widehat{f}(j)

(1.68)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\lambda_{k}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda},&j\in\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

Let
M_{\left|\alpha_{l-1}\right|+1}\leq j\leq M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|},

l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.
Then

S_{j}f=S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{l-1}\right|+1}}=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{\lambda_{\eta}M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}}}\right).

(1.69)

Let
M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}\leq j<M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|+1},

l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.
Then

S_{j}f=S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}+\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}^{1/p-1}\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}D_{j-M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}}}{\lambda}

(1.70)

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{\lambda_{\eta}M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{l}M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}^{1/p-1}\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}D_{j-M_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}}{\lambda}.

Proof
:
The proof is quite analogously to the proof of Example
1.47
.
Hence, we leave out the details.

For our further investigations it is convenient to fix sequence
\left\{\lambda_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.
In many cases such appropriate choice
can be the following sequence

\left\{\lambda/\alpha_{k}^{1/2}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.

Example 1.49

Let
0<p\leq q\leq 1,

\lambda=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}
and
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be an
increasing sequence of positive integers such that

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}^{p/2}}<\infty,

(1.71)

\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{\eta}^{1/2}}<\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}},

(1.72)

and

\frac{32\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}<\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}&p=q\\
\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1/q}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}&\text{\thinspace}p<q\end{array}\right.\text{\ }

(1.73)

For the given sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
, which
satisfy conditions (
1.71
)-(
1.73
) we define a martingale
\,f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right),

where

f^{\left(n\right)}:=\sum_{\left\{k:\text{ }2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k},

and

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\ \ \ \text{and \ \ }a_{k}:=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}\right).

Then
\,f\in H_{p}
and

\widehat{f}(j)

(1.74)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}},&j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

For every
l\in\mathbb{N}
we have the following identities

S_{j}f=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}S_{M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}},&M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}\leq j\leq M_{2\alpha_{l}},\text{ }\\
S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}},&M_{2\alpha_{l}}\leq j<M_{2\alpha_{l}+1}.\end{array}\right.

(1.75)

Moreover,
