\left|S_{j}f\right|\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}},\text{ \ for all }0\leq j\leq M_{2\alpha_{k}}\text{
and }0<p\leq q\leq 1

(1.76)

and

\left|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}}f\right|\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}},\text{ }0<p<q<1.

(1.77)

Proof
:
First, we note that such an increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
which satisfies conditions (
1.71
)-(
1.73
), can be constructed.

By applying (
1.71
) we conclude that condition (
1.55
) is satisfied
and we can invoke Lemma
1.71
to obtain that the martingale
\,f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p}.

Since

\frac{\lambda_{k}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}},

according to (
1.61
) and (
1.62
), we find that Fourier coefficients
are given by equality (
1.74
).

Let
M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}+1\leq

j\leq M_{2\alpha_{l}},
where
1\leq l\leq k.
In view of (
1.74
) we can conclude that

S_{j}f=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}}f=\sum_{v=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}

(1.78)

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\sum_{v=M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}^{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{\eta}^{1/2}}\psi_{v}

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{\eta}^{1/2}}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right).

Let
M_{2\alpha_{l}}\leq j<M_{2\alpha_{l}+1},
where
1\leq l\leq k.
We
apply (
1.74
) again and invoke (
1.11
) in Lemma
1.74
to obtain
that

S_{j}f=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}f+\sum_{v=M_{2\alpha_{l}}}^{j-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}

(1.79)

=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}f+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}}\sum_{v=M_{2\alpha_{l}}}^{j-1}\psi_{v}

=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}f+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}\right)

=S_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}f+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{l}}\psi_{2\alpha_{l}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}}.

By combining (
1.78
) and (
1.79
) we get the identities given by (
1.75
).

Moreover, if we take (
1.78
) obtained by letting
j=M_{2\alpha_{l}}
we can
rewrite (
1.79
) as

S_{j}f=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{\eta}^{1/2}}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)

(1.80)

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{\eta}^{1/2}}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{l}}}}\right).

Let
M_{2\alpha_{l-1}+1}-1\leq

j\leq M_{2\alpha_{l}},
where
1\leq l\leq k.
Then, according to (
1.72
) and (
1.80
), we get that

\left|S_{j}f\right|\leq\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{\lambda M_{2\alpha_{\eta-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{\eta-1}^{1/2}}

(1.81)

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-2}\frac{\lambda M_{2\alpha_{\eta-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{\eta-1}^{1/2}}+\frac{\lambda M_{2\alpha_{l-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{l-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{l-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{l-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}.

Let
M_{2\alpha_{l}}\leq j<M_{2\alpha_{l}+1},
where
1\leq l\leq k-1.
In
view of (
1.72
) and (
1.80
) we can conclude that

\left|S_{j}f\right|\leq\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}\frac{\lambda M_{2\alpha_{\eta-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{\eta-1}^{1/2}}+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p-1}j}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}}

(1.82)

\leq\frac{\lambda M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}}+\frac{\lambda M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}}=\frac{\lambda M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{l}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}.

Estimates (
1.82
) and (
1.81
) insure us that (
1.76
) is
satisfied.

Let
0<p<q<1
. By combining (
1.73
) and (
1.74
) with the expression (
1.81
) obtained by taking
\ j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}
we see that

\left|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}}f\right|=\left|\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}+S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}f\right|

=\left|\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}+S_{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}+1}}f\right|

\geq\left|\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\right|-\left|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}+1}}f\right|

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1/q}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

The proof is complete.

Remark 1.50

It is obvious that if we cancel condition (
1.73
) in
Example
1.49
we still get that
\,f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p}
and equality (
1.74
) and estimate (
1.76
) is valid. Moreover, (
1.80
)-(
1.81
) are still
fulfilled.

In the next chapter we many times will use a martingale, with the
following fixed sequence
\left\{\lambda_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}:

Example 1.51

Let
0<p\leq 1
,
\lambda=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\
and
\left\{\Phi_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be sequence of
non-negative and nondecreasing numbers, such that

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}^{p/4}}<\infty.

(1.83)

For this given sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
,
which satisfies condition (
1.83
) we define a martingale
\,f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right),
where

f^{\left(n\right)}:=\sum_{\left\{k:\text{ }2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k}

and

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda}{\Phi_{M_{2_{\alpha_{k}}}}^{1/4}}\text{,\ \ }a_{k}=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}\right).

Then
\,f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p}.
Moreover,

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2_{\alpha_{k}}}}^{1/4}},&j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(1.84)

Proof
:
The proof is quite analogously to the proof of Example
1.47
. Hence, we leave out the details.

Sometimes an appropriate choice of sequence will be the following

\left\{\lambda_{k}=\lambda/M_{k}^{1/p}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.

Example 1.52

Let
0<p\leq 1,

\lambda=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\
and

f^{\left(n\right)}=\sum_{\left\{k:2M_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k},

where

\lambda_{k}:=\frac{\lambda}{M_{k}^{1/p}}\text{ \ \ and \ \ }a_{k}:=\frac{M_{2M_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{2M_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{2M_{k}}}\right).

Then
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p},
for
all
0<p\leq 1.
Moreover,

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{M_{2M_{i}}^{1/p-1}}{M_{i}^{1/p}},&\text{\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{2M_{i}},...,M_{2M_{i}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }i\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2M_{i}},...,M_{2M_{i}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(1.85)

and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{1/p}}\right),\text{ \ \ when \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.86)

Let
M_{2M_{i}}\leq j<M_{2M_{i}+1},

l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.
Then

S_{j}f=S_{M_{2M_{i}}}+\frac{M_{{}_{2M_{i}}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{M_{2M_{i}}}D_{j-M_{2M_{i}}}}{M_{i}^{1/p}}.

(1.87)

Proof
:
This example is a simple consequence of Example
1.47
if we take
\alpha_{k}=M_{k}
and
\lambda_{k}=\lambda/M_{k}^{1/p}.
Since

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|^{p}\leq c\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{M_{k}}\leq c\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}}<c<\infty,

we obtain that
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p},
for all
0<p\leq 1.

In view of (
1.67
) we can conclude that

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)\backsim\left(\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }2M_{k}\geq n\right\}}\frac{1}{M_{k}}\right)^{1/p}\leq\left(\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }2^{k+1}\geq n\right\}}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\right)^{1/p}

\leq\left(\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }k\geq\log_{2}^{n}-1\right\}}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\right)^{1/p}\leq\left(\frac{1}{2^{\log_{2}^{n}-1}}\right)^{1/p}

=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{1/p}}\right)\rightarrow\infty,\text{ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

If we apply (
1.60
) obtained by taking
\alpha_{k}=M_{k}
we immediately get
equality (
1.87
). So the proof is complete.

2
Fourier coefficients and partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier
series on Martingale Hardy spaces

2.1
Some classical results on the Vilenkin-Fourier coefficients and
partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series

According to the Riemann-Lebesgue lemma (for details see e.g. the book
[
55
]
) we obtain that

\widehat{f}\left(k\right)\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ when \ \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty,

for each
f\in L_{1}.

Moreover,

\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\omega_{1}\left(\frac{1}{M_{N}},f\right),\text{ }

where
M_{N}\leq n\leq M_{N+1}
and
f\in L_{1}.

It is well-known (see e.g. the books
[
2
]

and
[
55
]

) that if
f\in L_{1}
and the Vilenkin series

T\left(x\right)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}c_{j}\psi_{j}\left(x\right)

convergences to
f
in
L_{1}
-norm, then

c_{j}=\int_{G_{m}}f\overline{\psi}_{j}d\mu:=\widehat{f}\left(j\right),

i.e. in this case the approximation series must be a Vilenkin-Fourier
series. An analogous result is true also if the Vilenkin series convergences
uniformly on
G_{m}
to an integrable function
f
.

Since
H_{1}\subset L_{1}
it yields that
\widehat{f}\left(k\right)\rightarrow 0
when
k\rightarrow\infty,
for every
f\in H_{1}.
The classical
inequality of Hardy type is well known in the trigonometric as well as in
the Vilenkin-Fourier analysis. Namely,

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(k\right)\right|}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}},

where the function
f
belongs to the Hardy space
H_{1}
and
c
is an
absolute constant. This was proved in the trigonometric case by Hardy and
Littlewood
[
37
]
(see also the book
[
11
]
) and for the
Walsh system it was proved in the book
[
55
]
.

Weisz
[
81
,
83
]
generalized this result for the bounded Vilenkin systems and proved
that there is an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(k\right)\right|^{p}}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ }\left(f\in H_{p},\text{ }0<p\leq 2\right)

(2.1)

Paley
[
48
]
proved that the Walsh-Fourier coefficients of a function
f\in L_{p}

\left(1<p<2\right)
satisfy the condition

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{f}\left(2^{k}\right)\right|^{2}<\infty.

This results fails to hold for
p=1.
However, it in fact holds for
functions
f\in L_{1},
such that the maximal function
f^{\ast}
belongs
L_{1},
i.e
f\in H_{1}
(see e.g the book
[
11
]
)
.

Let
0<p\leq 1
and
f\in H_{p}.
For Vilenkin systems the
following inequality was proved by Simon and Weisz
[
62
]
:

\left(\underset{k=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}M_{k}^{2-2/p}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{k}-1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{f}\left(jM_{k}\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

(2.2)

By using the Lebesgue constants we easily obtain that
S_{n_{k}}f

convergence to
f
in
L_{1}
-norm, for every integrable function
f
,
if and only if
\sup_{k}L_{n_{k}}\leq c<\infty.
There are various results
when
p>1.
It is also well-known that (see e.g. the book

[
55
]
)

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{p},\text{ \ when \ }p>1,

but it can be proved also a more stronger result (see e.g. the book
[
55
]
):

\left\|S^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{p},\text{ \ when \ }f\in L_{p},\text{ \ }p>1.

Uniform and point-wise convergence and some approximation properties of
partial sums in
L_{1}
norms was investigated by Goginava
[
30
]
(see
also
[
31
]
), Shneider
[
64
]
and Avdispahić and Memić
[
1
]
.
Fine
[
12
]
derived sufficient conditions for the uniform convergence,
which are in complete analogy with the Dini-Lipschitz conditions. Gulićev

[
36
]
estimated the rate of uniform convergence of a Walsh-Fourier series
using Lebesgue constants and modulus of continuity. Uniform convergence of
subsequences of partial sums was investigated also in Goginava and
Tkebuchava
[
34
]
. This corresponding problem was considered for Vilenkin
groups
G_{m}
by Fridli
[
13
]
and Gát
[
21
]
.

It is known (for details see e.g. the books
[
55
]

and
[
81
,
83
]
) that the subsequence
S_{M_{n}}
of the partial
sums is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue
space
L_{p},
for all
p>0.
However, (see Tephnadze
[
72
]
) there
exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}

\left(0<p<1\right),
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|S_{M_{n}+1}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

The reason of the divergence of
S_{M_{n}+1}f
is that when
0<p<1
the
Fourier coefficients of
f\in H_{p}
are not uniformly bounded (see
Tephnadze
[
71
]
). However, Simon
[
60
]
proved that there exists
an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p},\text{ \ \ \ }\left(0<p<1\right)

for all
f\in H_{p}.

It is also well-known that Vilenkin systems do not form bases in the
space
L_{1}.
Moreover, there is a function in the Hardy space
H_{1},

such that the partial sums of
f
are not bounded in the
L_{1}
-norm.
However, in Gát
[
18
]
the following strong convergence result was
obtained:

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f-f\right\|_{1}}{k}=0

for all
f\in H_{1}.

For the trigonometric analogue see Smith
[
63
]
and for the Walsh-Paley
system see Simon
[
58
]
.

Moreover, there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}\text{ \ }\left(n=2,3...\right)

and

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}}{k}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}},

for all
f\in H_{1}

.

2.2
Estimations of the Vilenkin-Fourier coefficients on
martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we prove that the Fourier coefficients of
\ f\in H_{p},

0<p<1,
are not uniformly bounded. Moreover, we
prove a new estimate of the Vilenkin-Fourier coefficients (see Theorem
2.1
). By applying this estimate we can obtain an independent proof
of (
2.1
) and (
2.2
).

The next theorem can be found in Tephnadze
[
71
]
:

Theorem 2.1

a)Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.\text{\ }

b) Let
0<p<1
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\frac{n^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{n}}=\infty.

(2.3)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|}{\Phi_{n}}=\infty.

Proof
:
Since

\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|\right)^{p}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|^{p},\text{
\ }\left(0<p\leq 1\right)

by applying Lemma
1.38
we obtain that the proof of part a) will
be complete if we show that

\frac{\left|\widehat{a}\left(n\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\leq c<\infty\text{ },\text{ when }0<p<1,

for every
p
-atom
a,
where
I
denotes the support of the atom.

Let
a
be an arbitrary
p
-atom with support
\ I
and
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.
We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
\widehat{a}\left(n\right)=0
when
n\leq M_{N}.
Therefore, we can suppose that
n>M_{N}.

According to the estimate
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}

we can write that

\frac{\left|\widehat{a}\left(n\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\leq\frac{1}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}d\mu

(2.4)

\leq\frac{1}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\int\limits_{I_{N}}M_{N}^{1/p}d\mu=\frac{M_{N}^{1/p}\left|I_{N}\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

\leq\frac{M_{N}^{1/p-1}}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\ \leq c<\infty,\text{
\ when }0<p<1.

It follows that

\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}

and the proof of the part a) is complete.

Let
0<p<1
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing, non-negative sequence, satisfying condition (
2.3
).
Then, for every
0<p<1,
there exists an increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
of positive integers such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}=\infty

and

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{p/2}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}<\infty.

(2.5)

Let
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be from
Example
1.47
, where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}}.

(2.6)

In view of (
2.5
) we conclude that (
1.55
) is satisfied and by using Example
1.47
we obtain that
f\in H_{p}.

By now using (
1.81
) with
\lambda_{k}
defined by (
2.6
)
we readily see that

\widehat{f}(j)=

(2.7)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2},&\text{\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N},\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}\text{.}\end{array}\right.

By combining (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) we find that

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\frac{\widehat{f}(n)}{\Phi_{n}}\geq\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\widehat{f}(M_{2\alpha_{k}})}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}

\geq\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}

\geq\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}=\infty.

The proof is complete.

2.3
Hardy and Paley type inequalities on martingale Hardy spaces

By using Theorem
2.1
we prove Hardy type inequality (
2.1
)
for
0<p\leq 1
. Moreover, we also show (see Tephnadze
[
69
]
) that the
sequence
\left\{1/k^{2-p}:k\geq 0\right\}
in the inequality below can
not be improved:

Theorem 2.2

a)Let
0<p\leq 1
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(k\right)\right|^{p}}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.\text{ \ }

b) Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing
sequence, satisfying the condition

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\Phi_{n}=+\infty.

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\text{ }\underset{k=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(k\right)\right|^{p}\Phi_{k}}{k^{2-p}}=\infty,\text{ }\left(0<p\leq 2\right).

Proof
:
By applying Lemma
1.38
we obtain that the proof of the part a)
will be complete, if we show that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{a}\left(k\right)\right|^{p}}{k^{2-p}}<c<\infty.

for every
p
-atom
a

\left(0<p\leq 1\right),
where
I
denotes the
support of the atom
.
Let
a
be an arbitrary
p
-atom with support
\ I

and
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.
Analogously we may assume that
I=I_{N}
and
n>M_{N}.

First, we consider the case
0<p<1.
According to (
2.4
) in Theorem
2.1
we readily get that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{a}\left(k\right)\right|^{p}}{k^{2-p}}

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\left|\frac{\widehat{a}\left(k\right)}{k^{1/p-1}}\right|^{p}

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\frac{M_{N}^{1-p}}{k^{1-p}}

\leq M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\,<\infty.

Let
p=1.
Hence, Schwatz and Bessel inequalities imply that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{a}\left(k\right)\right|}{k}

(2.8)

\leq\left(\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{a}\left(k\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k^{2}}\right)^{1/2}

\leq\frac{1}{M_{N}^{1/2}}\left\|a\right\|_{2}

=\frac{1}{M_{N}^{1/2}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|a\right|^{2}d\mu\right)^{1/2}

=\frac{1}{M_{N}^{1/2}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}M_{N}^{2}d\mu\right)^{1/2}

=\frac{1}{M_{N}^{1/2}}M_{N}^{1/2}<c<\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Next we note that for every
0<p<1
there exists an increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}\geq 2:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
of positive integers such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Phi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}^{1/2}=\infty

and

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}^{p/4}}<\infty.

(2.9)

Let
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be the martingale defined in
Example
1.51
. According to (
2.9
) we conclude that
f\in H_{p}.

Moreover, by using (
1.84
) from Example
1.51
and obvious estimates we find
that

\underset{l=1}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(l\right)\right|^{p}\Phi_{l}}{l^{2-p}}

=\underset{n=1}{\overset{k}{\sum}}\underset{l=M_{2\alpha_{n}}}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{n}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(l\right)\right|^{p}\Phi_{l}}{l^{2-p}}

\geq\underset{l=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(l\right)\right|^{p}\Phi_{l}}{l^{2-p}}

\geq c\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\underset{l=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(l\right)\right|^{p}}{l^{2-p}}

\geq c\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1-p}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{p/4}}\underset{l=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{l^{2-p}}

\geq c\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1-p}\underset{l=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}^{2-p}}

\geq c\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1-p}\frac{1}{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}^{1-p}}

\geq c\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ when \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

By using Theorem
2.1
we can also prove a Paley type inequality (
2.2
) for
0<p\leq 1
. Moreover, we show (see Tephnadze
[
69
]
) that the sequence
\left\{1/M_{k}^{2-2/p}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
in
the inequality below can not be improved:

Theorem 2.3

a)Let
0<p\leq 1
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\left(\underset{k=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}M_{k}^{2-2/p}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{k}-1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{f}\left(jM_{k}\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

b) Let
0<p\leq 1
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\Phi_{n}=+\infty.

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{k=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\Phi_{M_{k}}}{M_{k}^{2/p-2}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{k}-1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{f}\left(jM_{k}\right)\right|^{2}=\infty.

Proof
:
First, we consider case
0<p<1.
By applying Lemma
1.38
for the
proof of the part a) it suffices to show that

\underset{k=N+1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}M_{k}^{2-2/p}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{k}-1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{a}\left(jM_{k}\right)\right|^{2}\leq c<\infty

for every
p
-atom
a
with support
\ I=I_{N}.

According to (
2.4
) in Theorem
2.1
we readily get that

\underset{k=N+1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}M_{k}^{2-2/p}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{k}-1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{a}\left(jM_{k}\right)\right|^{2}

\leq\underset{k=N+1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}m_{k}^{2-2/p}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{k}-1}{\sum}}\left|\frac{\widehat{a}\left(jM_{k}\right)}{\left(jM_{k}\right)^{1/p-1}}\right|^{2}

\leq\underset{k=N+1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}m_{k}^{2-2/p}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{k}-1}{\sum}}\left(\frac{M_{N}^{1/p-1}}{\left(jM_{k}\right)^{1/p-1}}\right)^{2}

\leq M_{N}^{2/p-2}\underset{k=N+1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}m_{k}^{3}\frac{1}{M_{k}^{2/p-2}}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

Analogously to (
2.8
) we can prove this result also in the case when
p=1
,
so the proof of part a) is complete for every
0<p\leq 1.

On the other hand, to prove part b) we use the construction of martingale
from Example
1.51
(see also part b) of Theorem
2.2
). According to (
1.84
) in Example
1.51
we get that

\underset{l=1}{\overset{k}{\sum}}M_{2\alpha_{l}}^{2-2/p}\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{l}}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{\alpha_{l}}-1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{f}\left(jM_{2\alpha_{l}}\right)\right|^{2}

\geq M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{2-2/p}\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{f}\left(jM_{2\alpha_{k}}\right)\right|^{2}

\geq cM_{2\alpha_{k}}^{2-2/p}\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{2/p-2}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}

\geq c\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \qquad when \ \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

2.4
Maximal operators of partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series on martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we will consider weighted Maximal operators of partial
sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series and prove
(H_{p},L_{p})
and
(H_{p},weak-L_{p})
type inequalities. We also show sharpness of these
theorems in a special sense.

The next theorem can be found in Tephnadze
[
72
]
.

Theorem 2.4

a) Let
0<p<1.
Then the

maximal operator

\widetilde{S}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

b) Let
0<p<1
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{n}}=+\infty.

(2.10)

Then

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left\|\frac{S_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}f_{k}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}}=\infty.

Proof
:
Let
0<p<1.

Since
\overset{\sim}{S}_{p}^{\ast}
is bounded
from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
by Lemma
1.39
we obtain that
the proof of part a) will be complete if we show that

\int\limits_{\overline{I}}\left|\overset{\sim}{S}_{p}^{\ast}a\right|^{p}d\mu\leq c<\infty

for every
p
-atom
a,
where
I
denotes the support of the atom
.

Let
a
be a
p
-atom with support
\ I
and
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}.
We
may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
S_{n}\left(a\right)=0

when
n\leq M_{N}.
Therefore, we can suppose that
n>M_{N}.

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
it yields that

\left|S_{n}\left(a\right)\right|\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|D_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|D_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq M_{N}^{1/p}\int_{I_{N}}\left|D_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
0<p<1
and
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}.
From Lemma
1.6
we
get that

\frac{\left|S_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\leq\frac{cM_{N}^{1/p-1}M_{s}}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}.

(2.11)

Since
n>M_{N}
we can conclude that

\frac{\left|S_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\leq cM_{s},\text{\ \ for \ }x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},\text{ \ }0\leq s\leq N-1.

(2.12)

The expression on the right-hand side of (
2.12
) does not depend on
n.

Therefore,

\left|\widetilde{S}_{p}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq cM_{s}.

(2.13)

By combining (
1.1
) and (
2.13
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{p}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\widetilde{S}_{p}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{s}^{p}}{M_{s}}<c_{p}<\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Let
0<p<1.
Under condition (
2.10
) there exists positive integers
\left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\left(M_{2n_{k}}+2\right)^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}=\infty,\text{ \ \ }0<p<1.

To prove part b) we will apply the
p
-atoms from Example
1.44
.
By combining (
1.52
) and (
1.53
) we obtain that

\frac{\left|S_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}f_{k}\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}=\frac{\left|D_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}-D_{M_{2n_{k}}}\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}

=\frac{\left|\psi_{M_{2n_{k}}}\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}.

Hence,

\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\frac{\left|S_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}\geq\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}\right\}=1.

(2.14)

By combining (
1.54
) and (
2.14
) we have that

\frac{\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}\left(\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\frac{\left|S_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}f_{k}\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}\geq\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}\right\}\right)^{1/p}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}}

\geq\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1-1/p}}

=\frac{\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+2\right)^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ when \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

The next corollary is very important for our further investigation:

Corollary 2.5

Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

Proof
:
According to part a) of Theorem
2.4
we conclude that

\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\right\|_{p}

\leq\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

The proof is complete.

We also mention the following consequences of Theorem
2.4
:

Corollary 2.6

Let
0<p<1
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition (
2.10
). Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{\Phi_{n}}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Corollary 2.7

Let
0<p<1
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition (
2.10
). Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that the following maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p}.

The next result can be found in Tephnadze
[
72
]
.

Theorem 2.8

a) Let
f\in H_{1}.
Then the

maximal operator

\widetilde{S}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\log\left(n+1\right)}\text{\ }

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1}
to the space
L_{1}.

b) Let
q_{n}=M_{2n}+M_{2n-2}+...+M_{0}
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log n}{\Phi_{n}}=+\infty.

(2.15)

Then

\sup_{k}\frac{\left\|\frac{S_{q_{n_{k}}}f_{k}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}+1}}\right\|_{1}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{1}}}=\infty.

Proof
:

Since
\overset{\sim}{S}^{\ast}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}

to
L_{\infty},
according Lemma
1.39
it is sufficies to show that

\int\limits_{\overline{I}}\left|\overset{\sim}{S}^{\ast}a\right|d\mu\leq c<\infty

for every
p
-atom
a,
where
I
denotes the support of the atom
.
We may
assume that
I=I_{N}.
Since
S_{n}\left(a\right)=0
when
n\leq M_{N},

we can suppose that
n>M_{N}.

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}
we have that

\left|S_{n}\left(a\right)\right|\leq M_{N}\int_{I_{N}}\left|D_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}.
From Lemma
1.6
we get that

\frac{\left|S_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|}{\log\left(n+1\right)}\leq\frac{M_{s}}{\log\left(n+1\right)}.

Since
n>M_{N}
we can conclude that

\frac{\left|S_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|}{\log\left(n+1\right)}\leq\frac{cM_{s}}{N}.

(2.16)

The expression on the right-hand side of (
2.16
) does not depend on
n.
Thus,

\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{cM_{s}}{N},\text{ \ \ for \ }x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},\text{ \ }0\leq s\leq N-1.

(2.17)

By combining (
1.1
) and (
2.17
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq\frac{c}{N}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{s}}{M_{s}}\leq\frac{cN}{N}<c<\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Next, we note that under condition (
2.15
) there exists
\left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\},
such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log q_{n_{k}+1}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}+1}}}=\infty.

Let
f_{k}
be 1-atom from Example
1.44
. By applying
Corollary
1.10
with (
1.54
) we have that

\frac{\left\|\frac{S_{q_{n_{k}}}f_{k}\left(x\right)}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}+1}}}\right\|_{1}}{\left\|f_{k}\left(x\right)\right\|_{H_{1}}}

\geq\frac{1}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{1}}}\left(\left\|\frac{D_{q_{n_{k}}}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}+1}}}\right\|_{1}-\left\|\frac{D_{M_{2n_{k}}}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}+1}}}\right\|_{1}\right)

\geq\frac{c}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}+1}}}\left(\log q_{n_{k}}-1\right)

\geq\frac{c\log q_{n_{k}+1}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}+1}}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{
when }k\rightarrow\infty.

Hence, also part b) is proved so the proof is complete.

The next corollary is very important for our further investigation:

Corollary 2.9

Let
f\in H_{1}.
Then there exists an absolute
constant
c,
such that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{1}\leq c\log\left(n+1\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

Proof
:
According to part a) of Theorem
2.8
we readily conclude that

\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{\log\left(n+1\right)}\right\|_{1}

\leq\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\log\left(n+1\right)}\right\|_{1}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

The proof is complete.

We also point out two more consequences of Theorem
2.8
:

Corollary 2.10

Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be
any non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition (
2.15
). Then there
exists a martingale
f\in H_{1}
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{\Phi_{n}}\right\|_{1}=\infty.

Corollary 2.11

Let
0<p<1
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition (
2.15
). Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1}
such that the following maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{1}.

Now, we formulate a theorem about boundedness of a restricted maximal operator of
partial sums. This result is presented for the first
time.

Theorem 2.12

a) Let
0<p\leq 1
and
\alpha_{k}
be a subsequence of
positive numbers, such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(\alpha_{k}\right)=\varkappa<\infty.

(2.18)

Then the maximal operator

\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}f:=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{\alpha_{k}}f\right|

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

b) Let
0<p<1\ \
and
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a
subsequence of positive numbers satisfying the condition

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(\alpha_{k}\right)=\infty.

(2.19)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|S_{\alpha_{k}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty,\text{ \ \ }\left(0<p<1\right).

Proof
:
By using (
1.14
) in Lemma
1.3
and Corollary
1.5
we
easily conclude that if condition (
2.18
) holds, then

\left\|D_{\alpha_{k}}\right\|_{1}\leq c\overset{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}{\underset{j=\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\left\|D_{M_{j}}\right\|_{1}

\leq\overset{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}{\underset{j=\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}1=\rho\left(\alpha_{k}\right)+1\leq c<\infty.

It follows that
\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}.
By Lemma
1.39
we obtain that the
proof of part a) will be complete if we show that

\int\limits_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{p}d\mu\leq c<\infty

for every
p
-atom
a
with support
I=I_{N}
. Since
S_{\alpha_{k}}\left(a\right)=0
when
\alpha_{k}\leq M_{N},
we can suppose that
\alpha_{k}>M_{N}.

Let
t\in I_{N}
and
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},

1\leq s\leq\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle-1.
By using (
1.14
) in Lemma
1.3
we get that

D_{\alpha_{k}}\left(x-t\right)=0

and

\left|S_{\alpha_{k}}\left(a\right)\right|=0.

(2.20)

Let
0<p\leq 1,

t\in I_{N}
and
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},

\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\leq s\leq N-1.
By applying the fact
that
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}
and Lemma
1.6
we
find that

\left|S_{\alpha_{k}}\left(a\right)\right|

(2.21)

\leq M_{N}^{1/p}\int_{I_{N}}\left|D_{\alpha_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1/p-1}M_{s}.

Set

\varrho:=\min_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle.

Then, in view of (
2.20
) and (
2.21
) we can conclude that

\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|=0,\text{ \ \ for }\ x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},\ 0\leq s\leq\varrho

(2.22)

and

\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1/p-1}M_{s},\text{ for \ }x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},\text{ \ }\varrho\leq s\leq N-1.

(2.23)

By the definition of
\varrho
there exists at least one index
k_{i_{0}}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that
\varrho=\left\langle\alpha_{k_{i_{0}}}\right\rangle.
It follows that

N-\varrho=N-\left\langle\alpha_{k_{i_{0}}}\right\rangle\leq\left|\alpha_{k_{i_{0}}}\right|-\left\langle\alpha_{k_{i_{0}}}\right\rangle

(2.24)

\leq\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(\alpha_{k}\right)=\varkappa<c<\infty

and

\frac{M_{N}^{1-p}}{M_{\varrho}^{1-p}}\leq\lambda^{\left(N-\varrho\right)\left(1-p\right)}\leq\lambda^{\varkappa\left(1-p\right)}<c<\infty,

(2.25)

where
\lambda=\sup_{k}m_{k}.

Let
p=1.
We combine (
2.22
)-(
2.24
) and invoke identity (
1.1
) to obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=\varrho}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=\varrho+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{s}}{M_{s}}=c\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=\varrho+1}{\sum}}1

\leq\varkappa<c<\infty.

Let
0<p<1.
According to (
1.1
) by using (
2.22
), (
2.23
) and (
2.25
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=\varrho}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\widetilde{S}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=\varrho+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{s}^{p}}{M_{s}}=c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=\varrho+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{s}^{1-p}}

\leq\frac{c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}}{M_{\varrho}^{1-p}}<c<\infty.

The proof of part a) is proved.

Under condition (
2.19
) we readily get that

\sup_{\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}}{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}}=\infty.

Moreover, there exists a sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\subset\left\{n_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
such that
\alpha_{0}\geq 3
and

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}=\infty

and

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}<c<\infty.

(2.26)

Let
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be the martingale defined in Example
1.48
, where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}}.

(2.27)

Under condition (
2.26
) we can conclude that
f\in H_{p}.

By now using (
1.68
) with
\lambda_{k}
defined by (
2.27
)
we readily see that

\widehat{f}(j)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2},&\text{\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N},\\
0,&j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

Let
M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<j<\alpha_{k}.
By using (
1.70
) obtained by
l=k
in the case when
\lambda_{k}
are given by
expression (
2.27
) we immediately get that

S_{\alpha_{k}}f=S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f+M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}

:=I+II.

According to part a) of Theorem
2.12
for
I
we have that

\left\|I\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\leq\left\|S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}<\infty.

Under condition (
2.19
) we can conclude that

\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\neq\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ and \ \ \ \ \ }\left\langle\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right\rangle=\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle.

Let
x\in I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}.
By using Lemma
1.8
we obtain that

\left|D_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right|\geq cM_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}

and

\left|II\right|=M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}\left|D_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right|\geq M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+1\right)/2}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}.

It follows that

\left\|II\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\geq c_{p}\left(M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+1\right)/2}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}\right)^{p}\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\text{ }\left|II\right|\geq c_{p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+1\right)/2}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-1\right)/2}\right\}

\geq c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1+p\right)/2}\mu\left\{I_{{}_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}}^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle-1,\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\right\}\geq\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}.

Hence, for large
k
,

\left\|S_{\alpha_{k}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\geq\left\|II\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}-\left\|I\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\|II\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\geq\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1-p\right)/2}}\rightarrow\infty,\ \text{when}\ \ k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

We also mention the following consequences:

Corollary 2.13

Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}
. Then the maximal operator

\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{M_{n}}f\right|

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

Corollary 2.14

Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}
. Then the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N_{+}}}\left|S_{M_{n}+M_{n-1}}f\right|

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

Corollary 2.15

Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}
. Then the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N_{+}}}\left|S_{M_{n}+1}f\right|

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

2.5
Norm convergence of partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series on martingale Hardy spaces

By applying Corollaries
2.5
and
2.9
we find
necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity of
martingale Hardy spaces
H_{p},
for which the partial sums of
Vilenkin-Fourier series convergence in
L_{p}
-norm. We also study sharpness
of these results. All results in this section can be found in Tephnadze

[
72
]
.

Theorem 2.16

Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

Remark 2.17

We note that the asymptotic behaviour of the sequence
\left\{n^{1/p-1}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
in Theorem
2.16
can not be improved (c.f. part b) of Theorem
2.4
).

Proof
:
According to Corollaries
2.5
and
2.13
if we invoke Example
1.45
we can conclude
that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\left\|S^{\#}f\right\|_{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}

(2.28)

\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}+c_{p}n^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}

\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

The proof is complete.

Theorem 2.18

Let
f\in H_{1}.
Then there exists an absolute
constant
c
such that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{1}}\leq c\log n\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

Remark 2.19

We note that the asymptotic behaviour of the sequence
\left\{\log n:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
in Theorem
2.18
can not be improved (c.f. part b) of Theorem
2.8
).

Proof
:
According to Corollaries
2.9
and
2.13
if we invoke Example
1.45
we can write that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{1}}\leq\left\|S^{\#}f\right\|_{1}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{1}

\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}+c\log n\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}

\leq c\log n\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

The proof is complete.

Theorem 2.20

Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}
. Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\left\|S_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

Proof
:
In the view of Corollary
2.13
and Example
1.45
we can conclude that

\left\|S_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq n}\left|S_{M_{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

The proof is complete.

Theorem 2.21

Let
0<p<1,

f\in H_{p}
and
M_{k}<n\leq M_{k+1}
. Then
there is an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-1}\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{k}},f\right).

Proof
:
Let
0<p<1
and
M_{k}<n\leq M_{k+1}.
By using Corollary
2.5

we immediately get that

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{{H_{p}}}^{p}

\leq\left\|S_{n}f-S_{M_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{{H_{p}}}^{p}

=\left\|S_{n}\left(S_{M_{k}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{{H_{p}}}^{p}

\leq c_{p}\left(n^{1-p}+1\right)\left\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{{H_{p}}}^{p}

c_{p}\left(n^{1-p}+1\right)\left\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{{H_{p}}}^{p}

\leq c_{p}n^{1-p}\omega_{H_{p}}^{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{k}},f\right).

The proof is complete.

Theorem 2.22

Let
f\in H_{1}
and
M_{k}<n\leq M_{k+1}
. Then there is
an absolute constant
c
such that

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}}\leq c\lg n\omega_{H_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{k}},f\right).

Proof
:
Let
M_{k}<n\leq M_{k+1}.
Then, by using Corollary
2.9
, we
immediately get that

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}}

\leq\left\|S_{n}\left(S_{M_{k}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{1}}+\left\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}}

\leq c\left(\log n+1\right)\left\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}}

\leq c\log n\omega_{H_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{k}},f\right).

The proof is complete.

Theorem 2.23

a) Let
0<p<1,

f\in H_{p}
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}^{1/p-1}}\right),\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|S_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{when \ }\,\,\,k\rightarrow\infty.

b) For every
0<p<1
there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
, for which

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}^{1/p-1}}\right),\text{ \ when \ \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|S_{k}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{when\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
Let
0<p<1,

\ f\in H_{p}
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}^{1/p-1}}\right),\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

By using Theorem
2.21
we immediately get that

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Let
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be the martingale defined in Example
1.47
, where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}},\text{ where }\lambda=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}.

(2.29)

Since

\overset{\infty}{\sum_{k=0}}\frac{\lambda^{p}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1-p}}<c<\infty

we conclude that
f\in H_{p}.
Moreover, according to (
1.58
) we find
that

\omega_{H_{p}}(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f)=O\left(\overset{\infty}{\sum_{k=n}}\frac{1}{M_{k}^{1/p-1}}\right)

=O\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}^{1/p-1}}\right),\ when\ \ n\rightarrow\infty.

By now using (
1.57
) with
\lambda_{k}
defined by (
2.29
)
we readily see that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(2.30)

According to (
2.30
) and Corollary
1.5
we can establish
that

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}

\geq\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}f+\widehat{f}(M_{2\alpha_{k}})\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}

\geq\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(\|\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\|_{weak-L_{p}}-\|f-S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}f\|_{weak-L_{p}}\right)

\geq\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(1-o\left(1\right)\right)=1.

This completes the proof of the part b) and the proof is complete.

Theorem 2.24

a) Let
f\in H_{1}\
and

\omega_{H_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|S_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}}\rightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{when\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty.

b) There exists a martingale
f\in H_{1},
for which

\omega_{H_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{2M_{n}}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}}\right),\ \ \ \text{when}\ \ n\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|S_{k}f-f\right\|_{1}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{when\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
Let
f\in H_{1}
and

\omega_{H_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\ \ when\ \ n\rightarrow\infty.

By using Corollary
2.9
we immediately get that

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

For the proof of part b) we use the martingale defined in Example
1.52
, for
p=1.
Then,
f\in H_{1}\
and by applying (
1.86
) we can conclude that

\omega_{H_{1}}(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\ \ when\ \ n\rightarrow\infty.

By combining (
1.85
) and (
1.87
) with Corollaries
1.10
and
1.5
we find that

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\|S_{q_{M_{k}}}f-f\|_{1}

=\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\|\frac{\psi_{M_{M_{k}}}D_{q_{M_{k}}}}{M_{2k}}+S_{M_{M_{k}}}f-f\|_{1}

-\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(\frac{1}{M_{2k}}\|D_{q_{M_{k}}}\|_{1}+\|S_{M_{M_{k}}}f-f\|_{1}\right)

\geq c-\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(\overset{\infty}{\sum_{i=k+1}}\frac{1}{M_{2i}}-\frac{1}{M_{2k}}\right)=c>0.

The proof is complete.

2.6
Strong convergence of partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series on martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we prove a Hardy type inequality for partial sums with respect to
Vilenkin systems. This result was first proved by Simon
[
60
]
. Here we
use some new estimations and present a simpler proof, which is due to in
Tephnadze
[
72
]
. We also show sharpness of this result in the special
sense (see Tephnadze
[
69
]
).

Theorem 2.25

(Simon
[
60
]
) a) Let
0<p<1\
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then
there is an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

b) Let
0<p<1
and
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\Phi_{n}=+\infty.

(2.31)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\text{ }\underset{k=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\Phi_{k}}{k^{2-p}}=\infty.

Proof
:
Let
0<p<1.
By applying (
1.1
) with (
2.11
) in Theorem
2.4
we have that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-p}}

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\frac{S_{k}a\left(x\right)}{k^{1/p-1}}\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\frac{S_{k}a\left(x\right)}{k^{1/p-1}}\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\frac{M_{N}^{1/p-1}M_{s}}{k^{1/p-1}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k^{2-p}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}M_{s}^{p}

\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k^{2-p}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}M_{s}^{p-1}d\mu

+c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

This completes the proof of the part a).

Next, we note that under condition (
2.31
) there exists an increasing
sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}\geq 2:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
of
positive integers such that

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}^{p/4}}<\infty.

(2.32)

Let
f=\left(f^{\left(k\right)}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be the martingale defined in Example
1.51
. By using (
2.32
) we conclude that the martingale
\,f\in H_{p}.

Let
M_{\alpha_{k}}\leq j<M_{\alpha_{k}+1}.
By using (
1.84
) if we
repeat the steps which leads to (
1.70
) obtained by
l=k
in the case
when
\lambda_{k}=1/\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}^{1/4}
we obtain that

S_{j}f=\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}^{1/4}}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{2_{\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)

+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/4}}

:=I+II.

We calculate each term separately. By using Corollary
1.5
with condition
\alpha_{n}\geq 2

\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

for
I
we find that

D_{M_{\alpha_{n}}}=0,\text{ for }x\in G_{m}\backslash I_{1}

and

I=0,\text{ \qquad for }x\in G_{m}\backslash I_{1}.

Denote by
\mathbb{N}_{0}
the subset of positive integers
\mathbb{N}_{+},

for which
\left\langle n\right\rangle=0.
Then every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

M_{k}<n<

M_{k+1}

\left(k>1\right)
can be written as

n=n_{0}M_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}n_{j}M_{j},

where
n_{0}\in\left\{1,...,m_{0}-1\right\}
and
n_{j}\in\left\{0,...,m_{j}-1\right\},

~(j\in\mathbb{N}_{+}).

Let
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x\in G_{m}\backslash I_{1}=I_{0}\backslash I_{1}.
According to Lemma
1.8
we find that

\left|D_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right|\geq cM_{0}\geq c>0

and

\left|II\right|=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/4}}\left|D_{{}_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}\left(x\right)\right|=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/4}}.

It follows that

\left|S_{j}f\left(x\right)\right|=\left|II\right|=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/4}},\text{
\qquad for }x\in G_{m}\backslash I_{1},

and

\left\|S_{j}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}

(2.33)

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{2\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/4}}\mu\left(x\in G_{m}\backslash I_{1}:\left|S_{j}f\left(x\right)\right|>\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{2\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/4}}\right)^{1/p}

=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{2\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/4}}\left|G_{m}\backslash I_{1}\right|\geq\frac{cM_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{1/4}}.

Since

\underset{\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}:M_{k}\leq n\leq M_{k+1},\right\}}{\sum}1\geq cM_{k},

where
c
is an absolute constant, by applying (
2.33
) we obtain that

\underset{j=1}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{j}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\Phi_{j}}{j^{2-p}}

\geq\underset{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{j}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\Phi_{j}}{j^{2-p}}

\geq\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\underset{\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}:M_{2\alpha_{k}}\leq j\leq M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}\right\}}{\sum}\frac{\left\|S_{j}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}}{j^{2-p}}

\geq c\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1-p}}{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{p/4}}\underset{\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}:M_{2\alpha_{k}}\leq j\leq M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}\right\}}{\sum}\frac{1}{j^{2-p}}

\geq c\Phi_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{3/4}M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1-p}\underset{\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}:M_{2\alpha_{k}}\leq j\leq M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}\right\}}{\sum}\frac{1}{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}^{2-p}}

\geq c\frac{\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{3/4}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}}\underset{\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}:M_{2\alpha_{k}}\leq j\leq M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}\right\}}{\sum}1

\geq c\Phi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{3/4}\rightarrow\infty,\ \text{when \ \ \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Hence, also the part b) is proved so the proof is complete.

2.7
An application concerning estimations of Vilenkin-Fourier
coefficients

The following inequalities follow from our results:

Corollary 2.26

Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{\ }

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(k\right)\right|^{p}}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p},\text{ \ }\left(f\in H_{p},\text{ }0<p<1\right)

and

\left(\underset{k=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}M_{k}^{2-2/p}\underset{j=1}{\overset{m_{k}-1}{\sum}}\left|\widehat{f}\left(jM_{k}\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

Remark 2.27

The first inequality was first proved by Tephnadze
[
73
]
, the second
by Weisz
[
81
,
83
]
and the third also by Weisz
[
62
]
. The proofs
are different and simpler then the original ones.

Proof
:
Let
0<p<1.
Since

\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|=\left|S_{n+1}f-S_{n}f\right|\leq\left|S_{n+1}f\right|+\left|S_{n}f\right|

(2.34)

and

\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

\leq\frac{\left|S_{n+1}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}+\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{n^{1/p-1}}.

By using part a) of Theorem
2.4
we can write that

\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

\leq\left\|\frac{S_{n+1}f}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\right\|_{p}+\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{n^{1-p}}\right\|_{p}

\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

It follows that

\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}

and the first inequality is proved.

To prove the second inequality we use (
2.34
) again. We find that

\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{2/p-1}}

(2.35)

\leq\frac{\left|S_{n+1}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{2/p-1}}+\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{n^{2/p-1}}.

By combining (
2.35
) and Theorem
2.4
we get that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(n\right)\right|^{p}}{n^{2-p}}

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=1}{\sum}}\left(\left\|\frac{S_{n+1}f}{\left(n+1\right)^{2/p-1}}\right\|_{p}+\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{n^{2/p-1}}\right\|_{p}\right)^{p}

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=1}{\sum}}\left(\frac{\left\|S_{n+1}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{\left(n+1\right)^{2-p}}+\frac{\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{n^{2-p}}\right)

\leq 2\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{n^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

and also the second inequality is proved. The third inequality can be proved
analogously so we leave the details.

The proof is complete.

3
Vilenkin-Fejér means on martingale Hardy spaces

3.1
Some classical results on Vilenkin-Fejér means

In the one-dimensional case Yano
[
78
]
proved that

\left\|K_{n}\right\|\leq 2\ \ \ (n\in\mathbb{N}).

Consequently,

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{p}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ \ when \ \ \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ }(f\in L_{p},\text{ \ }1\leq p\leq\infty).

However (see
[
39
,
55
]
) the rate of convergence can not be better then

O\left(n^{-1}\right)

\left(n\rightarrow\infty\right)
for
non-constant functions. a.e, if
f\in L_{p},

1\leq p\leq\infty
and

\left\|\sigma_{M_{n}}f-f\right\|_{p}=o\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}}\right),\text{ \ when \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty,

then

f
is a constant function.

Fridli
[
14
]
used dyadic modulus of continuity to characterize the set
of functions in the space
L_{p}
, whose Vilenkin-Fejér means converge
at a given rate. It is also known that (see e.g books
[
2
]
and

[
55
]
)

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{p}

\leq c_{p}\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{N}},f\right)+c_{p}\sum_{s=0}^{N-1}\frac{M_{s}}{M_{N}}\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{s}},f\right),\text{ \ }\left(1\leq p\leq\infty,\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right).

By applying this estimate, we immediately obtain that if
f\in lip\left(\alpha,p\right),
i.e.,

\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}^{\alpha}}\right),\text{ \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty,

then

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{p}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}O\left(\frac{1}{M_{N}}\right),&\text{if }\alpha>1,\\
O\left(\frac{N}{M_{N}}\right),&\text{if }\alpha=1,\\
O\left(\frac{1}{M_{N}^{\alpha}}\right),&\text{if }\alpha<1\text{. }\end{array}\right.

Weisz
[
85
]
considered the norm convergence of Fejér means of
Vilenkin-Fourier series and proved that

\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ \ }p>1/2\text{ \ \ and \ \ \ }f\in H_{p}.

(3.1)

This result implies that

\frac{1}{n^{2p-1}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p},\text{ \ \ \ }\left(1/2<p<\infty\right).

If (
3.1
) hold for
0<p\leq 1/2,
then we would have that

\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p},\text{ \ \ \ }\left(0<p\leq 1/2\right).

(3.2)

However, in Tephnadze
[
66
]
it was shown that the assumption
p>1/2
in (
3.1
) is essential. In particular, is was proved that there exists a
martingale
f\in H_{1/2}
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{1/2}=+\infty.

For Vilenkin systems in
[
70
]
it was proved that (
3.2
) holds,
though inequality (
3.1
) is not true for
0<p\leq 1/2.

In the one-dimensional case the weak type inequality

\mu\left(\sigma^{\ast}f>\lambda\right)\leq\frac{c}{\lambda}\left\|f\right\|_{1},\text{ \qquad}\left(f\in L_{1},\text{ \ \ \ }\lambda>0\right)

can be found in Zygmund
[
88
]
for the trigonometric series, in Schipp

[
54
]
for Walsh series and in Pál, Simon
[
49
]
for bounded Vilenkin
series. Fujji
[
16
]
and Simon
[
57
]
verified that
\sigma^{\ast}

is bounded from
H_{1}
to
L_{1}
. Weisz
[
85
]
generalized this result
and proved the boundedness of
\sigma^{\ast}
from the martingale space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}
for
p>1/2
. Simon
[
56
]
gave a
counterexample, which shows that boundedness does not hold for
0<p<1/2.

The counterexample for
p=1/2
due to Goginava
[
27
]
, (see also
[
9
]
and
[
10
]
). Weisz
[
86
]
proved that
\sigma^{\ast}
is
bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}
. In
[
67
]
and
[
68
]
(for Walsh system see
[
28
]
) it was proved
that the maximal operator
\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}
with respect to
Vilenkin systems defined by

\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)},

where
0<p\leq 1/2
and
\left[1/2+p\right]
denotes integer part of
1/2+p,
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

Moreover, the order of deviant behavior of the
n
-th Fejér mean was given
exactly. As a corollary we get that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

For Walsh-Kaczmarz system analogical theorems were proved in
[
33
]
and

[
69
]
.

For the one-dimensional Vilenkin-Fourier series Weisz
[
85
]
proved that
the maximal operator

\sigma^{\#}f=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right|

is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}
for
p>0.
For the Walsh-Fourier series Goginava
[
30
]
proved that the
operator
\left|\sigma_{2^{n}}f\right|
is not bounded from the
space
H_{p}
to the space
H_{p},
for
0<p\leq 1.

3.2
Divergence of Vilenkin-Fejér means on martingale Hardy spaces

Theorem 3.1

a)There exist a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}
such that

\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{1/2}=+\infty.

b) Let
0<p<1/2.
There exist a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=+\infty.

Remark 3.2

This result for
p=1/2
can be found in Tephnadze
[
66
]
. By
interpolation automatically follows the second part of this Theorem. Here,
we make a more concrete proof of this fact by even constructing a martingale

f\in H_{p},
for which the Vilenkin-Fejér means are not bounded in the
space
weak-L_{p}.

Proof
:
Let
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be the martingale defined in Example
1.49
in the case when
p=q=1/2.
We can write that

\sigma_{q_{\alpha_{k}}}f=\frac{1}{q_{\alpha_{k}}}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{q_{\alpha_{k}}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{q_{\alpha_{k}}}S_{j}f

:=I+II.

According to (
1.76
) in Example
1.49
we can
conclude that

\left|I\right|\leq\frac{1}{q_{\alpha_{k}}}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\left|S_{i}f\right|

(3.3)

\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{2}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{q_{\alpha_{k}}}\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{2}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}.

By applying (
1.75
) obtained by letting
l=k
we can rewrite
II
as

II=\frac{\left(q_{\alpha_{k}}-M_{2\alpha_{k}}\right)S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}{q_{\alpha_{k}}}

+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}q_{\alpha_{k}}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{q_{\alpha_{k}}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}

:=II_{1}+II_{2}.

According to (
1.76
) for
j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}
and
p=1/2
we find that

\left|II_{1}\right|\leq\left|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\right|\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{2}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}.

(3.4)

In view of (
3.3
) and (
3.4
) we invoke estimate (
1.73
)
for
p=q=1/2
to conclude that

\left|II_{1}\right|\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{2}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{2}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}

and

\left|I\right|\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{2}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{2}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}.

Let

x\in I_{2\alpha_{k}}^{2\eta,2\left(\eta+1\right)},\,\,\,\,\,\eta=\left[\frac{\alpha_{k}}{2}\right]+1,...,\alpha_{k}-3.

By applying Lemma
1.19
we have that

4q_{\alpha_{k}-1}\left|K_{q_{\alpha_{k}-1}}\left(x\right)\right|\geq M_{2\eta}M_{2\left(\eta+1\right)}\geq M_{2\eta}^{2}.

Hence, for
II_{2}
we readily get that

\left|II_{2}\right|=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}q_{\alpha_{k}}}\left|\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\sum_{j=1}^{q_{\alpha_{k}-1}}D_{j}\right|

=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{q_{\alpha_{k}}}\frac{q_{\alpha_{k}-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\left|K_{q_{\alpha_{k}}-1}\right|

\geq\frac{q_{\alpha_{k}-1}\left|K_{q_{\alpha_{k}}-1}\right|}{2\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\geq\frac{M_{2\eta}^{2}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

Since
M_{2\eta}\geq M_{\alpha_{k}}
we obtain that

\left|\sigma_{q_{\alpha_{k}}}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\left|II_{2}\right|-\left(\left|I\right|+\left|II_{1}\right|\right)

(3.5)

\geq\frac{1}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\left(M_{2\eta}^{2}-\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{2}}{2\alpha_{k}}\right).

From (
3.5
) it follows that

\left|\sigma_{q_{\alpha_{k}}}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{cM_{2\eta}^{2}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}},\,\,x\in I_{2\alpha_{k}}^{2\eta,2\left(\eta+1\right)},

where

\eta=\left[\frac{\alpha_{k}}{2}\right]+1,...,\alpha_{k}-3,\,

Thus,

\int_{G_{m}}\left|\sigma_{q_{\alpha_{k}}}f\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\geq\sum_{\eta=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]+1}^{\alpha_{k}-3}\sum_{x_{2\eta+3}=0}^{m_{2\eta+3}-1}...\sum_{x_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}-1}}=0}^{m_{2\alpha_{k}-1}-1}\int_{I_{2\alpha_{k}}^{2\eta,2\left(\eta+1\right)}}\left|\sigma_{q_{\alpha_{k}}}f\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\geq\frac{c}{\alpha_{k}^{1/4}}\sum_{\eta=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]+1}^{\alpha_{k}-3}\sum_{x_{2\eta+3}=0}^{m_{2\eta+3}-1}...\sum_{x_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}-1}}=0}^{m_{2\alpha_{k}-1}-1}\left|I_{2\alpha_{k}}^{2\eta,2\left(\eta+1\right)}\right|M_{2\eta}

\geq\frac{c}{\alpha_{k}^{1/4}}\sum_{\eta=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]+1}^{\alpha_{k}-3}\frac{m_{2\eta+3...}m_{2\alpha_{k}-1}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}M_{2\eta}

\geq\frac{c}{\alpha_{k}^{1/4}}\sum_{\eta=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]+1}^{\alpha_{k}-3}\frac{M_{2\eta}}{M_{2\eta+2}}

\geq\frac{c}{\alpha_{k}^{1/4}}\sum_{\eta=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]+1}^{\alpha_{k}-3}1

\geq\alpha_{k}^{3/4}\rightarrow\infty,\ \ when\ \ k\longrightarrow\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete so we turn to the proof of part b). Let
0<p<1/2.
Let
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be the martingale defined in Example
1.49
in the case when
0<p<q=1/2.
We can write
that

\sigma_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f

=\frac{1}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}S_{j}f+\frac{S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}

:=III+IV.

We combine (
1.76
) and (
1.77
) and invoke (
1.73
) in the
case when
p<q=1/2
to obtain the following estimates:

\left|III\right|\leq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}

\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}

and

\left|IV\right|\geq\frac{\left|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right|}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{2\alpha_{k}}

Let
x\in G_{m}.
We conclude that

\left|\sigma_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\left|IV\right|-\left|III\right|

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{2\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

It follows that

\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\left(\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|\sigma_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}+1}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\right\}\right)^{1/p}

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ when }k\rightarrow\infty.

Hence, also part b) is proved so the proof is complete.

3.3
Maximal operators of Vilenkin-Fejér means on martingale
Hardy spaces

In this subsection we consider weighted Maximal operators of Fejér means of
Vilenkin-Fourier series and prove
(H_{p},L_{p})
and
(H_{p},weak-L_{p})

type inequalities. In all cases we also show sharpness in a special sense.

First we note the following consequence of Theorem
3.1
:

Corollary 3.3

a) There exist a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}
such that

\left\|\sigma^{\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}=+\infty.

b) Let
0<p<1/2.
There exist a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\left\|\sigma^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=+\infty.

The next theorem can be found in Tephnadze
[
68
]
, but here we will
give a simpler proof of part b).

Theorem 3.4

a) Let
0<p<1/2.
Then the

maximal operator

\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}

is bounded from the Hardy martingale space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

b) Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing
sequence satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}{\Phi_{n}}=+\infty.

(3.6)

Then

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left\|\frac{\sigma_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}f_{k}}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\right\|_{weal-L_{p}}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}}=\infty.

Proof
:
First place we note that
\sigma_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}

to
L_{\infty}
(see (
1.23
) in Corollary
1.16
). Hence, by
Lemma
1.39
the proof of Theorem
3.4
will be
complete if we show that

\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}}\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}a\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\right)^{p}d\mu\leq c<\infty

for every p-atom
a,
where
I
denotes the support of the atom
.

Let
a
be an arbitrary p-atom with support
\ I
and
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.
We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)=0
when
n\leq M_{N}
. Therefore we can suppose that
n>M_{N}
.

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
it follows that

\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}

\leq\frac{1}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{cM_{N}^{1/p}}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
From Corollary
1.21
we
can deduce that

\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}

(3.7)

\leq\frac{c_{p}M_{N}^{1/p}}{M_{N}^{1/p-2}}\frac{M_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}}=c_{p}M_{l}M_{k}.

The expression on the right-hand side of (
3.7
) does not depend on
n.
Thus,

\left|\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{p}M_{l}M_{k},\text{ \ for \ }x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.

(3.8)

By using (
3.8
) with identity (
1.1
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,j\in\{l+1,...,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}...m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}

+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{N}^{p}M_{k}^{p}

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}}{M_{l}}+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{1-p}}

:=I+II.

We estimate each term separately.

I=c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}^{1-2p}}\frac{M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}}{M_{l}^{2p}}

(3.9)

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}^{1-2p}}

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{l\left(1-2p\right)}}

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{k\left(1-2p\right)}}<c_{p}<\infty.

It is obvious that

II\leq\frac{c_{p}}{M_{N}^{1-2p}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}

(3.10)

\leq\frac{c_{p}}{M_{N}^{1-2p}}<c<\infty.

The proof of the part a) is complete by combining the estimates above.

Let
0<p<1/2.
Under condition (
3.6
) there exists an increasing
sequence of positive integers
\left\{\lambda_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\lambda_{k}^{1/p-2}}{\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}}=\infty.

It is evident that for every
\lambda_{k}
there exists a positive integers

m_{k}^{,}
such that
q_{{}_{m_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}}}<\lambda_{k}<2q_{{}_{m_{k}^{,}}}.
Since
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

is a non-decreasing function we have that

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{M_{2m_{k}^{,}}^{1/p-2}}{\Phi_{M_{2m_{k}^{,}}+1}}

\geq\frac{1}{2}\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{\left(M_{2m_{k}^{,}}+1\right)^{1/p-2}}{\Phi_{M_{2m_{k}^{,}}+1}}

\geq c\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\lambda_{k}^{1/p-2}}{\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}}=\infty.

Let
\ \left\{n_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\subset\left\{m_{k}^{,}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a sequence of positive numbers
such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{M_{2n_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}=\infty

and
f_{k}
be the atom defined in Example
1.44
.

By combining (
1.52
) and (
1.53
) in Example
1.44
we find
that

\frac{\left|\sigma_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}f_{k}\right|}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1\right)}\left|\overset{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}S_{j}f_{k}\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1\right)}\left|S_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}f_{k}\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1\right)}\left|D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}-D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1\right)}\left|\psi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1\right)}

\geq\frac{c}{M_{{}_{{}_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}}\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}.

Hence,

\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\frac{\left|\sigma_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\geq\frac{c}{M_{{}_{{}_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}}\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\right\}

\geq\mu\left(G_{m}\right)=1.\qquad

Therefore, by using (
1.54
) in Example
1.44
we get that

\frac{\frac{c}{M_{{}_{{}_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}}\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\left(\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\frac{\left|\sigma_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\geq\frac{c}{M_{{}_{{}_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}}\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\right\}\right)^{1/p}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}}

\geq\frac{c}{M_{{}_{{}_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}}\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1/p-1}=\frac{cM_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1/p-2}}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\rightarrow\infty\text{,\qquad when }k\rightarrow\infty.

Thus, also part b) is proved so the proof is complete.

We also point out the following consequence of Theorem
3.4
, which we need later on.

Corollary 3.5

a) Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there
exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

Proof
:
According to part a) of Theorem
3.4
we conclude that

\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n}f}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\right\|_{p}

\leq\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\right\|_{p}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

The proof is complete.

We also mention the following corollaries:

Corollary 3.6

Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

be any non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition (
3.6
). Then
there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n}f}{\Phi_{n}}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Corollary 3.7

Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

be any non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition (
3.6
). Then the following maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p}.

The next theorem can be found in Tephnadze
[
67
]
.

Theorem 3.8

a) The maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{\sigma}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{1/2}.

b) Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}{\Phi_{n}}=+\infty.

(3.11)

Then

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left\|\frac{\sigma_{q_{n_{k}}}f_{k}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}\right\|_{1/2}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{1/\text{ }2}}}=\infty.

Proof
:
First we note that
\sigma_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(see (
1.23
) in Corollary
1.16
). Hence, according to Lemma
1.39
, to prove part a) it suffices to show that

\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}}\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}a\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\right)^{1/2}d\mu\leq c<\infty

for every 1/2-atom
a,
where
I
denotes the support of the atom
.

Let
a
be an arbitrary 1/2-atom with support
\ I
and
