\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.
We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)=0
when
n\leq M_{N}
. Therefore we can suppose that
n>M_{N}
.

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{2}
we obtain that

\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}

\leq\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{M_{N}^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
Then, from Corollary
1.21

it follows that

\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}

(3.12)

\leq\frac{cM_{N}^{2}}{N^{2}}\frac{M_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}}=\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{N^{2}}.

The expression on the right-hand side of (
3.12
) does not depend on
n.
Therefore,

\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{N^{2}},\text{ \ for \ }x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.

(3.13)

By applying (
3.13
) with identity (
1.1
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,j\in\{l+1,...,N-1\}}^{m_{j}-1}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|\overset{\sim}{\sigma}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}\ldots m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}\frac{M_{l}^{1/2}M_{k}^{1/2}}{N}

+c\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}\frac{M_{N}^{1/2}M_{k}^{1/2}}{N}:=I+II.

We estimate each term separately.

I\leq\frac{c}{N}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{1/2}}{M_{l}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{c}{N}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}1\leq c<\infty.

Moreover,

II\leq\frac{1}{M_{N}^{1/2}N}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}M_{k}^{1/2}\leq\frac{c}{N}<c<\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Let
\ \left\{\lambda_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be an
increasing sequence of positive integers such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log^{2}\left(\lambda_{k}+1\right)}{\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}}=\infty.

It is evident that for every
\lambda_{k}
there exists a positive integers

m_{k}^{,}
such that
q_{m_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}}\leq\lambda_{k}<q_{m_{k}^{\prime}+1}<c

q_{m_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}}.
Since
\Phi_{n}
is
a non-decreasing function we have that

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{\left(m_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{\Phi_{q_{m_{k}^{,}}}}\geq c\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log^{2}\left(\lambda_{k}+1\right)}{\Phi_{\lambda_{k}}}=\infty.

Let
\ \left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\subset\left\{m_{k}^{\prime}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a subsequence of positive numbers
\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{n_{k}^{2}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}=\infty

and let
f_{k}
be the atom defined in Example
1.44
. We combine (
1.52
) and (
1.53
) in Example
1.44
and invoke (
1.11
) in
Lemma
1.2
to obtain that

\frac{\left|\sigma_{q_{n_{k}}}f_{k}\right|}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}q_{n_{k}}}\left|\overset{q_{n_{k}}}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}S_{j}f_{k}\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}q_{n_{k}}}\left|\overset{q_{n_{k}}}{\underset{j=M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}{\sum}}S_{j}f_{k}\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}q_{n_{k}}}\left|\overset{q_{n_{k}}}{\underset{j=M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}{\sum}}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}\right)\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}q_{n_{k}}}\left|\overset{q_{n_{k}-1}}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(D_{j+M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}\right)\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}q_{n_{k}}}\left|\overset{q_{n_{k}-1}}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}D_{j}\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}\frac{q_{n_{k}-1}}{q_{n_{k}}}\left|K_{q_{n_{k}-1}}\right|.

Let
x\in

I_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{2s,2l}
. Then, in view of Lemma
1.19
, we
can conclude that

\frac{\left|\sigma_{q_{n_{k}}}f_{k}(x)\right|}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}\geq\frac{cM_{2s}M_{2l}}{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}.

Hence,

\int_{G_{m}}\left|\frac{\sigma_{q_{n_{k}}}f_{k}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}\right|^{1/2}d\mu

\geq\text{ }\overset{n_{k}-3}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\overset{n_{k}-1}{\underset{l=s+1}{\sum}}\overset{m_{2l+1}}{\underset{x_{2l+1=0}}{\sum}}...\overset{m_{2n_{k}-1}}{\underset{x_{2n_{k}-1}=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{2s,2l}}\left|\frac{\sigma_{q_{n_{k}}}f_{k}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}\right|^{1/2}d\mu

\geq c\overset{n_{k}-3}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\overset{n_{k}-1}{\underset{l=s+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{{}_{2l+1}}...m_{2n_{k}-1}}{M_{2n_{k}}}\frac{M_{2s}^{1/2}M_{2l}^{1/2}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}^{1/2}M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1/2}}

\geq c\overset{n_{k}-3}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\overset{n_{k}-1}{\underset{l=s+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{2s}^{1/2}}{M_{2l}^{1/2}M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1/2}\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}^{1/2}}

\geq\frac{cn_{k}}{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1/2}\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}^{1/2}}.

From (
1.54
) in Example
1.44
we have that

\frac{\left(\int_{G_{m}}\left|\frac{\sigma_{q_{n_{k}}}f_{k}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}\right|^{1/2}d\mu\right)^{2}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{1/\text{ }2}}}

\geq\frac{cn_{k}^{2}}{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}M_{2n_{k}}

\geq\frac{cn_{k}^{2}}{\Phi_{q_{n_{k}}}}\rightarrow\infty,\qquad\text{when \qquad}k\rightarrow\infty.

Thus, also part b) is proved so the proof is complete.

We also point out the following consequence of Theorem
3.8
, which we need later on.

Corollary 3.9

Let
f\in H_{1/2}.
Then there exists an absolute
constant
c
such that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{1/2}\leq c\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

Proof
:
According to part a) of Theorem
3.8
we readily conclude
that

\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n}f}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\right\|_{1/2}

\leq\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\right\|_{1/2}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

The proof is complete.

We also mention the following corollaries:

Corollary 3.10

Let

be any non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition (
3.11
). Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2},
such that

Corollary 3.11

Let

be any non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition (
3.11
). Then the following maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the Lebesgue space

L_{1/2}.

The next results was proved by Tephnadze
[
53
]
.

Theorem 3.12

a) Let
0<p\leq 1/2
and
\left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a subsequence of positive numbers such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(n_{k}\right)=\varkappa<c<\infty.

(3.14)

Then the maximal operator

\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}f:=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}f\right|

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

b) Let
0<p<1/2\ \
and
\left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a
subsequence of positive numbers satisfying the condition

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty.

(3.15)

Then there exists an martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty,\text{ \ \ }\left(0<p<1/2\right).

Proof
:
Since
\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty},
by using Lemma
1.39
, we obtain that the proof
of part a) is complete if we show
that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|<c<\infty,

for every
p
-atom
a
with support
\ I_{N}
and
\mu\left(I_{N}\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.
Analogously to Theorem
3.8
we may assume that

n_{k}>M_{N}
.

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
we find that

\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}\left(a\right)\right|\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(3.16)

\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq M_{N}^{1/p}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{i,j}\
and
i<j<\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle.
Then
x-t\in I_{N}^{i,j}
for
t\in I_{N}\ \
and, according to Lemma
1.11

we obtain that

\left|K_{M_{l}}\left(x-t\right)\right|=0,\text{ \ for all }\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle\leq\text{ }l\leq\left|n_{k}\right|.

By applying (
3.16
) and (
1.22
) in Lemma
1.16
we get
that

\displaystyle\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{N}^{1/p}\overset{\left|n_{k}\right|}{\underset{l=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{l}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)=0,\text{ }

\displaystyle\text{for }x

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle I_{N}^{i,j},\text{ \ }0\leq i<j<\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle\leq l\leq\left|n_{k}\right|.

Let
x\in I_{N}^{i,j},\,
where
\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle\leq j\leq N.
Then, in view of Corollary
1.21
, we have that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{i}M_{j}}{M_{N}^{2}}.

By using again (
3.16
) we obtain that

\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1/p-2}M_{i}M_{j}.

Set

\varrho:=\min_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle.

Then

\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|=0,\text{ \ \ for }\ x\in I_{N}^{i,j},\ 0\leq i<j\leq\varrho

(3.17)

and

\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1/p-2}M_{i}M_{j},\text{ for \ }x\in I_{N}^{i,j},\text{ \ }i<\varrho\leq j\leq N-1.

(3.18)

Analogously to (
2.24
) we can conclude that

N-\varrho\leq\varkappa

(3.19)

and

\frac{M_{N}^{1-2p}}{M_{\varrho}^{1-2p}}\leq\lambda^{\left(N-\varrho\right)\left(1-2p\right)}\leq\lambda^{\varkappa\left(1-2p\right)}<c<\infty,

(3.20)

where
\lambda=\sup_{k}m_{k}.

Let
p=1/2.
By combining (
3.17
)-(
3.19
) with (
1.1
) we
get that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,N}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu

\leq\overset{\varrho-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=\varrho}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu

+\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\varrho}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu+\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,N}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu

\leq c\overset{\varrho}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}^{1/2}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=\varrho+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{j}^{1/2}}

+\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\varrho}{\sum}}M_{i}^{1/2}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{j}^{1/2}}+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{i}^{1/2}}{M_{N}^{1/2}}

\leq cM_{\varrho}^{1/2}\frac{1}{M_{\varrho}^{1/2}}+c\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\varrho}{\sum}}M_{i}^{1/2}\frac{1}{M_{i}^{1/2}}+c

\leq N-\varrho+c\leq c<\infty.

Let
0<p<1/2.
By combining (
3.17
), (
3.18
) and (
3.20
) with (
1.1
) we have that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{p}d\mu

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{p}d\mu

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\leq\overset{\varrho-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=\varrho}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{p}d\mu

+\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\varrho}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{p}d\mu+\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,N}}\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{\ast,\vartriangle}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}\overset{\varrho}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}^{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=\varrho+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{j}^{1-p}}

+M_{N}^{1-2p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\varrho}{\sum}}M_{i}^{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{j}^{1-p}}+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{i}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}

\leq\frac{c_{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}}{M_{\varrho}^{1-2p}}+c_{p}\leq c_{p}\lambda^{\left(\left|n_{k}\right|-\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle\right)\left(1-2p\right)}+c_{p}<\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Let
\left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be a sequence of positive
numbers satisfying condition (
3.15
). Then

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}}{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}}=\infty.

(3.21)

Under condition (
3.21
) there exists a sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\subset\left\{n_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
such that

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1-2p\right)/2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1-2p\right)/2}}<c<\infty.

Let
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be
the martingae defined in Example
1.48
, where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}\text{, \ \ \ \ }\lambda=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}.

(3.22)

By applying (
1.38
) we can conclude that
f\in H_{p}.

By now using (
1.68
) with
\lambda_{k}
defined by (
3.22
) we obtain that

\widehat{f}(j)

(3.23)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2},&j\in\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

Therefore,

\sigma_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}f=\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}S_{j}f

:=I+II.

Let
M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<j\leq\alpha_{k}.
Then,
according to (
3.23
) we have that

S_{j}f=S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f+M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}D_{j-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}.

(3.24)

By applying (
3.24
) we can rewrite
II
as

II=\frac{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f

+\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}D_{j-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}

:=II_{1}+II_{2}.

In view of Corollary
2.13
for
II_{1}
we find that

\left\|II_{1}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\leq\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{p}\left\|S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}<\infty.

By using part a) of Theorem
3.12
for
I
we obtain that

\left\|I\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}=\left(\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{p}\left\|\sigma_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

Under condition (
3.15
) we can conclude that

\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\neq\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ and \ \ \ \ \ \ }\left\langle\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right\rangle=\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle.

Let
x\in

I_{{}_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}}^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle-1,\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}.

According to Lemma
1.18
we get that

\left|II_{2}\right|=\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}{\alpha_{k}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{j}\right|

=\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}{\alpha_{k}}\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right|

\geq cM_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p-1}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right|

\geq cM_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p-1}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+2\right)/2}.

It follows that

\left\|II_{2}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\geq c_{p}\left(M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+2\right)/2}\right)^{p}\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\text{ }\left|II_{2}\right|\geq c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+2\right)/2}\right\}-c_{p}

\geq c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2-p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/2+p}\mu\left\{I_{{}_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}}^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle-1,\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\right\}

\geq\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2-p}}{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/2-p}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{
\ when \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

By now combining the estimates above we can conclude that

\left\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\geq\left\|II_{2}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}-\left\|II_{1}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}-\left\|I\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\|II_{2}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\geq\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2-p}}{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/2-p}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{
when }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

From the part a) of Theorem
3.12
follows immediately the
following well-known results of Weisz
[
85
]
:

Corollary 3.13

Let
p>0.
Then the maximal operator

\sigma^{\#}f:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right|

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

Proof
:
It is obvious that

\left|M_{n}\right|=\left[M_{n}\right]=n

and

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(M_{n}\right)=0<c<\infty.

It follows that condition (
3.14
) is satisfied and the proof is
complete by just applying part a) of Theorem
3.12
.

Our next results reads:

Theorem 3.14

Let
0<p\leq 1.
Then the operator
\left|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right|
is not bounded from the martingale Hardy space

H_{p}
to the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}.

Remark 3.15

This result for the Walsh system can be found in Goginava
[
30
]
and for bounded Vilenkin systems in the paper of Persson and Tephnadze
[
52
]
.

Proof
:
Let
f_{k}
be the martingale from Example
1.44
. By combining (
1.11
) in Lemma
1.2
and (
1.52
), (
1.53
) in Example
1.44
we find that

\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}

=\frac{1}{M_{2n_{k}+1}}\overset{M_{2n_{k}+1}}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}S_{j}f_{k}

=\frac{1}{M_{2n_{k}+1}}\overset{M_{2n_{k}+1}}{\underset{j=M_{2n_{k}}+1}{\sum}}S_{j}f_{k}

=\frac{1}{M_{2n_{k}+1}}\overset{M_{2n_{k}+1}}{\underset{j=M_{2n_{k}}+1}{\sum}}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{2n_{k}}}\right)

=\frac{1}{M_{2n_{k}+1}}\overset{M_{2n_{k}}}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(D_{j+M_{2n_{k}}}-D_{M_{2n_{k}}}\right)

=\frac{\psi_{M_{2n_{k}}}}{M_{2n_{k}+1}}\overset{M_{2n_{k}}}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}D_{j}=\frac{\psi_{M_{2n_{k}}}}{m_{2n_{k}}}K_{M_{2n_{k}}}.

It is evident that

S_{M_{N}}\left(\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|\right)

=\int_{G_{m}}\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}}}f_{k}\left(t\right)\right|D_{M_{N}}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)

\geq\int_{I_{2n_{k}}}\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}}}f_{k}\left(t\right)\right|D_{M_{N}}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)

\geq c\int_{I_{2n_{k}}}\left|K_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}\left(t\right)\right|D_{M_{N}}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)

\geq cM_{2n_{k}}\int_{I_{2n_{k}}}D_{M_{N}}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right).

Since

\psi_{j}\left(t\right)=1\text{, \ \ \ for \ \ }t\in I_{2n_{k}},\text{ \ \ }j=0,1,...,M_{2n_{k}}-1

we obtain that

S_{M_{N}}\left(\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|\right)

\geq cM_{2n_{k}}\frac{1}{M_{2n_{k}}}D_{M_{N}}\left(x\right),\text{ \ }N=0,1,...,M_{N}-1

and

\sup_{1\leq N<M_{2n_{k}}}S_{M_{N}}\left(\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|\right)

\geq\sup_{1\leq N<M_{2n_{k}}}S_{M_{N}}\left(\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|\right)

\geq\sup_{1\leq N<M_{2n_{k}}}D_{M_{N}}\left(x\right).

Let
x\in I_{s}/I_{s+1},
for some
s=0,1,\cdots,2n_{k}-1.
Then, from
Corollary
1.5
it follows that

\sup_{1\leq N<M_{2n_{k}}}S_{M_{N}}\left(\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|\right)\geq cM_{s}.

According to Lemma
1.37
for every
0<p\leq 1
it yields that

\left\|\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\right|\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

(3.25)

=\left\|\sup_{1\leq N<2n_{k}}S_{M_{N}}\left(\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|\right)\right\|_{p}^{p}

\geq\int_{G_{m}}\left(\sup_{1\leq N<M_{2n_{k}}}S_{M_{N}}\left(\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|\right)\right)^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\geq\overset{2n_{k}-1}{\underset{s=1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left(\sup_{1\leq N<M_{2n_{k}}}S_{M_{N}}\left(\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|\right)\right)^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

Let
0<p<1.
Then

\left\|\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\right|\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}\geq c\overset{2n_{k}-1}{\underset{s=1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{s}^{p}}{M_{s}}=c_{p}>0.

Let
p=1.
We find that

\left\|\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\right|\right\|_{H_{1}}\geq c\overset{2n_{k}-1}{\underset{s=1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{s}}{M_{s}}\geq cn_{k}.

(3.26)

By combining (
1.54
) and (
3.25
) with (
3.26
) in Example
1.44
we can conclude that

\frac{\left\|\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\right|\right\|_{H_{p}}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}}\geq\frac{c_{p}}{M_{2n_{k}}^{1-1/p}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ \ when \ \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ \ }0<p<1

and

\frac{\left\|\left|\sigma_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}f_{k}\right|\right\|_{H_{1}}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{1}}}\geq cn_{k}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ \ when \ \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 3.16

Let
p>0.
Then the maximal operator

\sigma^{\#}:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right|

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}
, but it is not
bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Hardy space
H_{p}.

Proof
:
Since

\left|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right|\leq\sigma^{\#}f

(3.27)

this result follows immediately from Corollary
3.13
and
Theorem
3.14
so the proof is complete.

Corollary 3.17

Let
p>0.
Then the operator
\left|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right|
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the
space Lebesgue
L_{p},
but is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Hardy space
H_{p}.

Proof
:
By using (
3.27
) this result immediately follows Corollary
3.13
and Theorem
3.14
, so the proof is
complete.

3.4
Norm convergence of Vilenkin-Fejér means on martingale Hardy
spaces

In this section we find necessary and sufficient conditions for the
modulus of continuity of martingale Hardy spaces
H_{p}
for which the
partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series convergence in
L_{p}
-norm. We also
study sharpness of these results.

Theorems
3.18
-
3.22
can be found in
Persson and Tephnadze
[
52
]
and Theorem
3.23
-
3.24
are proved in Tephnadze
[
71
]
.

Theorem 3.18

Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-2}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\ \ \ \left(0<p<1\right).

Remark 3.19

We note that the asymptotic behaviour of the sequence
\left\{n^{1/p-2}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
in Theorem
3.18
can not be improved (c.f. part b) of Theorem
3.4
).

Proof
:
According to Theorem
3.18
and Corollary
3.9
if we invoke Example
1.46
we can conclude
that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\left\|\sigma^{\#}f\right\|_{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}

(3.28)

\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}+c_{p}n^{1/p-2}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}

\leq c_{p}n^{1/p-2}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

The proof is complete.

Theorem 3.20

Let
f\in H_{1/2}.
Then there exists an absolute
constant
c
such that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}\leq c\log^{2}n\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}.

Remark 3.21

We note that the asymptotic behaviour of the sequence of sequence
\left\{\log^{2}n:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
in Theorem
3.18
can not be improved (c.f. part b) of Theorem
3.8
).

Proof
:
According to Theorem
3.18
and Corollary
3.9
if we invoke Example
1.46
we can write that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}\leq\left\|\sigma^{\#}f\right\|_{1/2}+\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{1/2}

\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}+c\log^{2}n\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}

\leq c\log^{2}n\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}.

The proof is complete.

Theorem 3.22

Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}
. Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\ \ \ \left(0<p<1\right).

Proof
:
In the view of Corollary
3.16
and Example
1.46
we can conclude that

\left\|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq n}\left|\sigma_{M_{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

The proof is complete.

Theorem 3.23

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and

\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}^{1/p-2}}\right)\text{when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(3.29)

Then

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

b) Let
0<p<1/2
.
Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
for which

\omega\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)_{H_{p}}=O\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}^{1/p-2}}\right)\text{ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{when\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
Let
f\in H_{p},

0<p<1/2,
and
M_{N}<n\leq M_{N+1}.
By a routine
calculation we immediately get that

\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f

(3.30)

=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{N}}S_{k}S_{M_{N}}f+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=M_{N}+1}^{n}S_{k}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f

=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{N}}S_{k}f+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=M_{N}+1}^{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f

=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{N}}S_{k}f+\frac{n-M_{N}}{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f

=\frac{M_{N}}{n}\sigma_{M_{N}}f-\frac{M_{N}}{n}S_{M_{N}}f

=\frac{M_{N}}{n}\left(S_{M_{N}}\sigma_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right)

=\frac{M_{N}}{n}S_{M_{N}}\left(\sigma_{M_{N}}f-f\right).

Hence,

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}\leq\left\|\sigma_{n}f-\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|S_{M_{N}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

=\left\|\sigma_{n}\left(S_{M_{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|S_{M_{N}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

\leq c_{p}\left(n^{1-2p}+1\right)\omega_{H_{p}}^{p}\left(\frac{1}{M_{N}},f\right)+\left\|S_{M_{N}}\left(\sigma_{M_{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{p}^{p}.

Let
p>0
and
\ f\in H_{p}.
According to Corollary
2.13

and Theorem
3.22
we get that

\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ when }k\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ \ }\left(p>0\right)

(3.31)

and

\|\sigma_{M_{k}}f-f\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ when }k\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ \ }\left(p>0\right).

(3.32)

Hence,

\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

=\frac{M_{N}^{p}}{n^{p}}\left\|S_{M_{N}}\left(\sigma_{M_{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

\leq\left\|S_{M_{N}}\left(\sigma_{M_{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

\leq\left\|\sigma_{M_{N}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}\rightarrow 0,\text{ when }k\rightarrow\infty\text{.}

It follows that under condition (
3.29
) we have that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

This completes the proof of part a) so we turn to the part b).

First, we consider case
0<p<1/2.
Let
f=\left(f^{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be the martingale defined in Lemma
1.47
, where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}.\text{ \ }

By combining (
1.55
) and (
1.58
) in Lemma
1.47
we
conclude that the martingale
f\in H_{p}\ \
and

\omega_{H_{p}}(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f)\leq\left(\sum_{\left\{k,2\alpha_{k}\geq n\right\}}\frac{1}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1-2p}}\right)^{1/p}

=O\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}^{1/p-2}}\right),\text{ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

A simple calculation gives that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{2\alpha_{i}},&\text{\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{i}},...,M_{2\alpha_{i}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }i\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&j\notin\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{i}},...,M_{2\alpha_{i}+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

(3.33)

By using (
3.33
) we obtain that

\|\sigma_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}

=\|\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}\sigma_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}f}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}+\frac{S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}f}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}-\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}f}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}-\frac{f}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}\|_{weak-L_{p}}

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}\|\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\|_{weak-L_{p}}

-\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}\|\sigma_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}

-\frac{1}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}\|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}-o\left(1\right)\text{, \ \ when \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Therefore,

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\|\sigma_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}

\geq\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}\geq c>0.

Hence also part b) is proved so the proof is complete.

Theorem 3.24

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}
and

\omega_{H_{1/2}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),\text{ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(3.34)

Then

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

b) There exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}
for which

\omega_{H_{1/2}}\left(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),\text{ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{1/2}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{when\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
Let
f\in H_{1/2}
and
M_{N}<n\leq M_{N+1}.
By using identity (
3.30
) we find that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}\leq\left\|\sigma_{n}f-\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}

+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}+\left\|S_{M_{N}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}

=\left\|\sigma_{n}\left(S_{M_{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}+\left\|S_{M_{N}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}

\leq c\left(\log^{2}n+1\right)\omega_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{M_{N}},f\right)+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}.
By combining (
3.31
), (
3.32
) and
equality (
3.30
) we have that

\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}

(3.35)

=\frac{M_{N}}{n}\left\|S_{M_{N}}\left(\sigma_{M_{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}\rightarrow 0\text{ when }k\rightarrow\infty\text{.}

It follows that under condition (
3.34
) we obtain that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

and the proof of part a) is complete.

Let
\ f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be a martingale from the Lemma
1.52
where
p=1/2
.
Then
\ f\in H_{1/2}
and

\omega_{H_{1/2}}(\frac{1}{M_{n}},f)=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),\text{
\ when }n\rightarrow\infty.

Hence,

\sigma_{q_{M_{k}}}f-f

(3.36)

=\frac{M_{2M_{k}}\sigma_{M_{2M_{k}}}f}{q_{M_{k}}}+\frac{1}{q_{M_{k}}}\sum_{j=M_{2M_{k}}+1}^{q_{M_{k}}}S_{j}f

-\frac{M_{2M_{k}}f}{q_{M_{k}}}-\frac{q_{M_{k}-1}f}{q_{M_{k}}}.

Let
M_{2M_{k}}<j\leq q_{M_{k}}.
By combining (
1.85
) and (
1.87
) we
have that

\frac{1}{q_{M_{k}}}\sum_{j=M_{{}_{2M_{k}}}+1}^{q_{M_{k}}}S_{j}f

=\frac{q_{M_{k}-1}S_{M_{2M_{k}}}f}{q_{M_{k}}}+\frac{M_{2M_{k}}\psi_{M_{{}_{2M_{k}}}}}{q_{M_{k}}M_{k}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{q_{M_{k}-1}}D_{j}

=\frac{q_{M_{k}-1}S_{M_{2M_{k}}}f}{q_{M_{k}}}+\frac{M_{2M_{k}}\psi_{M_{{}_{2M_{k}}}}q_{M_{k}-1}K_{q_{M_{k}-1}}}{q_{M_{k}}M_{k}^{2}}.

By using (
3.36
) we get that

\|\sigma_{q_{M_{k}}}f-f\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

(3.37)

\geq\frac{c}{M_{k}}\|q_{M_{k}-1}K_{q_{M_{k}-1}}\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

-\left(\frac{M_{2M_{k}}}{q_{M_{k}}}\right)^{1/2}\|\sigma_{M_{{}_{2M_{k}}}}f-f\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

-\left(\frac{q_{M_{k}-1}}{q_{M_{k}}}\right)^{1/2}\|S_{M_{2M_{k}}}f-f\|_{1/2}^{1/2}.

Let

x\in I_{2M_{k}}^{2s,2\eta},\,\,\,\,\,\,s=\eta+2,\eta+3,M_{k}-2.

By applying Lemma
1.19
we find that

q_{M_{k}-1}\left|K_{q_{M_{k}-1}}\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{M_{2\eta}M_{2s}}{4}.

Hence,

\int_{G_{m}}\left|q_{M_{k}-1}K_{q_{M_{k}-1}}\right|^{1/2}d\mu

(3.38)

\geq c\sum_{\eta=1}^{M_{k}-4}\sum_{s=\eta+2}^{M_{k}-2}\sum_{x_{2s+1}=0}^{m_{2s+1}-1}...\sum_{x_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}-1}}=0}^{m_{2M_{k}-1}-1}\int_{I_{2M_{k}}^{2s,2\eta}}\left|q_{M_{k}-1}K_{q_{M_{k}-1}}\right|^{1/2}d\mu

\geq c\sum_{\eta=1}^{M_{k}-4}\sum_{s=\eta+2}^{M_{k}-2}\frac{1}{2M_{2s}}M_{2s}^{1/2}M_{2\eta}^{1/2}\geq cM_{k}.

By combining (
3.37
) and (
3.38
) we obtain that

\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\|\sigma_{q_{M_{k}}}f-f\|_{1/2}\geq c>0.

Thus also the part b) is proved so the proof is complete.

3.5
Strong convergence of Vilenkin-Fejér means on martingale
Hardy spaces

The first result in this section is due to Blahota and Tephnadze
[
5
]
:

Theorem 3.25

a) Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there
exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

b) Let
0<p<1/2
and
\left\{\Phi_{k}\ :k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\
be
any non-decreasing sequence satisfying the
conditions
\Phi_{n}\uparrow\infty
and

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{k^{2-2p}}{\Phi_{k}}=\infty.

(3.39)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\underset{k=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}}{\Phi_{k}}=\infty.

Proof
:
According to Lemma
1.38
it suffices to show that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c<\infty

for every
p
-atom
a
with support
\ I
,
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.

We may assume that
I=I_{N}
and
n>M_{N}
.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
\sigma_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(see (
1.23
) in Corollary
1.16
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
we obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu\leq c\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{p}}{M_{N}}\leq c<\infty,\text{ \ }0<p<1/2.

Hence,

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-2p}}\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c<\infty.

It is easy to see that

\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|

\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|K_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\left\|a\left(x\right)\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq M_{N}^{1/p}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
Then, from Corollary
1.21

it follows that

\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{p}M_{l}M_{k}M_{N}^{1/p-2}.

(3.40)

By combining (
1.1
) and (
3.40
) if we invoke also estimates (
3.9
) and (
3.10
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

(3.41)

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,\text{ }j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}\dotsm m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}

+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{k}^{p}M_{N}^{1-p}

\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}}{M_{l}}+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}

\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}.

Let
0<p<1/2.
By using (
3.41
) we get that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)}{m^{2-2p}}

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{cM_{N}^{1-2p}}{m^{2-p}}<c<\infty.

and the proof of part a) is complete.

Under condition (
3.39
) there exists an increasing numbers
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
, such that

\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{cM_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}^{2-2p}}{\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}}=\infty.

There exists a sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\subset\left\{n_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
such that

\left|\alpha_{k}\right|>2,\text{ for \ }k\in\mathbb{N}

and

\sum_{\eta=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}^{1/2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}^{1-p}}

(3.42)

=m_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}^{1-p}\sum_{\eta=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}^{1/2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}^{1-p}}<c<\infty.

Let
f
be the martingale defined in Example
1.48
in the case when

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/p-1}}.

(3.43)

By applying (
3.42
) we can conclude that
f\in H_{p}.

By now using (
3.43
) with
\lambda_{k}
defined by (
1.68
)
we readily get that

\widehat{f}(j)

(3.44)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2p},&\text{\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},\dots,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\text{,}\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},\dots,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

Hence,

\sigma_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}f=\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}S_{j}f

(3.45)

:=III+IV.

Let
M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}<j<\alpha_{k}.
According to (
3.44
) and (
1.69
), we can write that

S_{j}f

(3.46)

=\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}\left(D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right)

+\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{j-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}.

By using (
3.46
) in
IV
we obtain that

IV=\frac{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}\left(D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right)

(3.47)

+\frac{\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}D_{j-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}

:=IV_{1}+IV_{2}.

We calculate each term separately. First
we define a set of positive numbers
n\in\mathbb{N}_{1,}
, for which
\left\langle n\right\rangle=1,
that is,

\mathbb{N}_{1}:=\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\text{ }n=n_{1}M_{1}+\sum_{i=2}^{\left|n\right|}n_{i}M_{i}\right\},

where
n_{1}\in\left\{1,\ldots,m_{1}-1\right\}
and
n_{i}\in\left\{0,\ldots,m_{i}-1\right\},
for
i\geq 2.

Let
\alpha_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
and
x\in I_{2}^{0,1}
. Since
\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\in\mathbb{N}_{2}\ \
and
\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\neq\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle
for every
\left|\alpha_{k}\right|>2,
by applying Lemma
1.18
, we find that

\left|IV_{2}\right|=\frac{c_{p}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}}{\alpha_{k}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{{}_{j}}\left(x\right)\right|

=\frac{c_{p}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}}{\alpha_{k}}\left|\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\left(x\right)\right|

\geq\frac{c_{p}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}}{\alpha_{k}}.

Let
x\in I_{2}^{0,1},

n\geq 2
and
1\leq s_{n}\leq m_{n}-1.
By
combining Corollary
1.5
, Lemma
1.11
and (
1.18
) in Lemma
1.13
we have that

K_{s_{n}M_{n}}\left(x\right)=D_{M_{n}}\left(x\right)=0,\text{ \ \ \ for
\ \ }n\geq 2.

Since
\left|\alpha_{k}\right|>2,

k\in\mathbb{\mathbb{N}}
, we obtain that

IV_{1}=0,\text{ \ for }x\in I_{2}^{0,1}.

(3.48)

Moreover, if we invoke (
1.69
) and (
1.70
) with (
3.43
) we
get that

S_{j}f=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(M_{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|+1}\right)\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|}}D_{{}_{j-M_{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|}}},\\
M_{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|}<j\leq M_{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|+1},\text{ \ \ \ }s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\\
0,\\
M_{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|+1}<j\leq M_{\left|\alpha_{s+1}\right|},\text{ }s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\end{array}\right.

and

III=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\sum_{v=M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}+1}^{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}D_{v-M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}

(3.49)

=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\sum_{v=1}^{\left(m_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}-1\right)M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}D_{v-M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}

=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)\left(m_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}-1\right)M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}K_{\left(m_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}-1\right)M_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\left(x\right)=0.

Let
0<p<1/2,

n\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
and
M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<n<M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}.
By combining (
3.45
)-(
3.49
) we find that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\geq\frac{c_{p}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2}}{\alpha_{k}^{p}}\mu\left\{x\in I_{2}^{0,1}:\text{ }\left|IV_{2}\right|\geq\frac{c_{p}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2p}}{\alpha_{k}}\right\}

\geq\frac{c_{p}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2}}{\alpha_{k}^{p}}\mu\left\{I_{2}^{0,1}\right\}\geq\frac{c_{p}\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}^{p}}.

Since

\underset{\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}_{1}:M_{k}\leq n\leq M_{k+1}\right\}}{\sum}1\geq cM_{k},

we obtain that

\underset{n=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}}{\Phi_{n}}

\geq\underset{\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}_{1}:\text{ }M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<n<M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right\}}{\sum}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}}{\Phi_{n}}

\geq\frac{c_{p}}{\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2}}\underset{\left\{\mathbb{N}_{1}:\text{ }M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<n<M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right\}}{\sum}\frac{1}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}^{p}}

\geq\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}^{1-p}}{\Phi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}^{1/2}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{
when \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Therefore also part b) is proved so the proof is complete.

Corollary 3.26

Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there
exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p},

Proof
:
By combining (
3.28
) and Theorem
3.25
we have that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\sigma^{\#}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}

\leq c\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}

\leq c_{p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}+c_{p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}

\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

and the proof is complete.

Corollary 3.27

Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there
exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\frac{1}{n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}}{k^{1-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p},

\frac{1}{n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}}{k^{1-2p}}=0,

and

\frac{1}{n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}}{k^{1-2p}}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

In Blahota and Tephnadze
[
5
]
also the endpoint case
p=1/2
was considered and
the following result was proved:

Theorem 3.28

Let
f\in H_{1/2}.
Then there exists an absolute
constant
c
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

Proof
:
In view of Lemma
1.38
it suffices to show that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}a\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{m}\leq c<\infty

for every
p
-atom
a
with support
\ I
,
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.

Analogously to the previous Theorems we may assume that
I=I_{N}
and
n>M_{N}
.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
\sigma_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(see (
1.23
) in Corollary
1.16
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{2}
we obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{1/2}}{M_{N}}\leq c<\infty.

Hence,

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu}{m}

\leq\frac{c}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m}\leq c<\infty.

It is easy to see that

\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|

\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|K_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\left\|a\left(x\right)\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq M_{N}^{2}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l<N.
Then, from Lemma
1.20
it
follows that

\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}M_{N}}{m}.

(3.50)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},\,0\leq k<N.
Then, according to Lemma
1.20
,
we have that

\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq cM_{N}^{2}\frac{M_{k}}{M_{N}}\leq cM_{k}M_{N}.

(3.51)

By combining (
1.1
) with (
3.50
) and (
3.51
) we find that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,\text{ }j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}\dotsm m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}\frac{M_{l}^{1/2}M_{k}^{1/2}M_{N}^{1/2}}{m^{1/2}}

+c\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{k}^{1/2}M_{N}^{1/2}

\leq cM_{N}^{1/2}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{1/2}M_{k}^{1/2}}{m^{1/2}M_{l}}

+c\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{1/2}}{M_{N}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{cM_{N}^{1/2}N}{m^{1/2}}+c.

It follows that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)}{m}

\leq\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\left(\frac{cM_{N}^{1/2}N}{m^{3/2}}+\frac{c}{m}\right)<c<\infty.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 3.29

Let
f\in H_{1/2}.
Then

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2},\text{ }

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}}{k}=0

and

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}}{k}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

Proof
:
According to Theorem
3.28
we have that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}}{k}

\leq\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}+\left\|\sigma^{\#}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}

\leq\frac{c}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}+\left\|f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}

\leq\frac{c}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}+\frac{c}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}

\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

The first inequality is proved. Analogously we can prove the second and third
statement. We leave out the details.

4
Vilenkin-Nörlund means on martingale
Hardy spaces

4.1
Some classical results on Vilenkin-Nörlund means

It is well-known in the literature that the so-called Nörlund means are
generalizations of the Fejér, Cesàro and logarithmic means. The Nörlund
summation is a general summability method. Therefore it is of prior
interest to study the behavior of operators related to Nörlund means of
Fourier series with respect to orthonormal systems.

In
[
69
]
it was proved that there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},

(0<p\leq 1),
such that the maximal operator of Nörlund logarithmic means
L^{\ast}
is not bounded in the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.
Riesz logarithmic means with
respect to the trigonometric system was studied by a lot of authors.We
mention, for instance, the paper by Szasz
[
65
]
and Yabuta
[
76
]
.
These means with respect to the Walsh and Vilenkin systems were investigated
by Simon
[
56
]
and Gàt
[
18
]
. Blahota and Gát
[
8
]

considered norm summability of Nörlund logarithmic means and showed that
Riesz logarithmic means
R_{n}
have better approximation properties on some
unbounded Vilenkin groups than the Fejér means. Moreover, in
[
74
]

it was proved that the maximal operator of Riesz‘s means is bounded from the
Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}
for
p>1/2
but not
when
0<p\leq 1/2.

Móricz and Siddiqi
[
41
]
investigate the approximation properties of
some special Nörlund means of Walsh-Fourier series of
L_{p}
functions in
norm. The case when
\left\{q_{k}=1/k:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
was
excluded, since the methods of Móricz and Siddiqi are not applicable to Nörlund logarithmic means. In
[
19
]
Gát and Goginava proved some
convergence and divergence properties of the Nörlund logarithmic means of
functions in the class of continuous functions and in the Lebesgue space
L_{1}.
In particular, they gave a negative answer to the question of Móricz
and Siddiqi
[
41
]
. Gát and Goginava
[
20
]
proved that for each
measurable function satisfying

\phi\left(u\right)=o\left(u\log^{1/2}u\right),\text{ \ \ \ \ \ when \ \ \ }u\rightarrow\infty,

there exists an integrable function
f
such that

\int_{G_{m}}\phi\left(\left|f\left(x\right)\right|\right)d\mu\,\left(x\right)<\infty

and that there exists a set with positive measure such that the
Walsh-logarithmic means of the function diverges on this set.
Fridli, Manchanda and Siddiqi
[
15
]
improved and extended results of Móricz and Siddiqi
[
41
]
to dyadic homogeneous Banach spaces and Martingale Hardy spaces.

In
[
26
]
Goginava investigated the behavior of Cesàro means of
Walsh-Fourier series in detail. In the two-dimensional case approximation
properties of Nörlund and Cesàro means were considered by Nagy (see
[
42
]
,
[
43
]
and
[
45
]
). The maximal operator
\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}

\left(0<\alpha<1\right)
of the
\left(C,\alpha\right)
means of
Vilenkin systems was investigated by Weisz
[
80
]
. In this paper Weisz
proved that
\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}
is bounded from the martingale space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}
for
p>1/\left(1+\alpha\right).
Goginava

[
24
]
gave a counterexample which shows that boundedness does not
hold for
0<p\leq 1/\left(1+\alpha\right).
Weisz and Simon
[
61
]

showed that the maximal operator
\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}
is bounded from
the Hardy space
H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
to the space
weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
.

4.2
Maximal operators of Nörlund means on martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we first state our main result concerning the maximal
operator of the Nörlund summation method (see (
1.2
)). We also show
that this result is in a sense sharp. The proof can be found in Persson,
Tephnadze and Wall
[
50
]
.

Theorem 4.1

a) The maximal operator
t^{\ast}
of the
summability method (
1.2
) with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.

The statement in a) is sharp in the following sense:

b) Let
0<p<1/2
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a non-decreasing sequence
satisfying the condition

\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{n}}\geq\frac{c}{n},\text{ \ \ }\left(c>0\right).

(4.1)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Proof
:
Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be non-decreasing. By combining
(
1.29
) and (
1.30
) and using Abel transformation we get that

\left|t_{n}f\right|\leq\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}q_{n-j}S_{j}f\right|

\leq\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right|j\left|\sigma_{j}f\right|+q_{0}n\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|\right)

\leq\frac{c}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{n-j}-q_{n-j-1}\right)j+q_{0}n\right)\sigma^{\ast}f\leq\sigma^{\ast}f

so that

t^{\ast}f\leq\sigma^{\ast}f.

(4.2)

In view of (
4.2
) we can conclude that the maximal operators
t^{\ast}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.
The proof of part a) is complete.

Let
f:=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be the martingale defined in Example
1.49
in the case when
0<p<q=1/2
. We can write that

t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1-j}S_{j}f+\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f

:=I+II.

According to (
1.76
) in Example
1.49
we can conclude
that

\left|I\right|\leq\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{j}\left|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1-j}f\right|

(4.3)

\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1-j}

\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}.

If we now apply (
1.77
) for
II
we find that

\left|II\right|=\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\left|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right|\geq\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

(4.4)

Without lost the generality we may assume that
c=1
in (
4.1
). By
combining (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) we obtain that

\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right|\geq\left|II\right|-\left|I\right|

\geq\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

On the other hand,

\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\right\}=\mu\left(G_{m}\right)=1.

(4.5)

Let
0<p<1/2.
Then

\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\cdot\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\right\}

(4.6)

=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ when }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

The next Theorems (Theorems
4.2
-
4.4
) are due to Persson, Tephnadze and Wall
[
51
]
:

Theorem 4.2

a) Let
0<p<1/2
and the sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be non-decreasing. Then the

maximal operator

\widetilde{t}_{p,1}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}

is bounded from the Hardy martingale space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

Remark 4.3

Since the Fejér means are examples of Nörlund means with
non-decreasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
we
immediately obtain from part b) of Theorem
3.4
that the
asymptotic behaviour of the sequence of weights

\left\{1/\left(k+1\right)^{1/p-2}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

in Nörlund means in Theorem
4.2
can not be improved.

Proof
:
The idea of proof is similar to that of part a) of Theorem
3.4
, but in more general situation so we give the details.

First we note that
t_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}

(see Corollary
1.23
). Let
a
be an arbitrary p-atom, with
support
\ I
and
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.
We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
S_{n}\left(a\right)=t_{n}\left(a\right)=0,
when
n\leq M_{N}
. Therefore, we can suppose that
n>M_{N}
. Hence,

t_{n}\left(a\right)=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}q_{n-k}S_{k}\left(a\right)

(4.7)

=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}S_{k}\left(a\right)

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}\int_{I_{N}}a\left(x\right)D_{k}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right).

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
it follows that

\frac{\left|t_{n}\left(a\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{M_{N}^{1/p}}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
From Lemma
1.27
we can
deduce that

\frac{\left|t_{n}\left(a\right)\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}\leq\frac{c_{p}M_{N}^{1/p}}{M_{N}^{1/p-2}}\frac{M_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}}=c_{p}M_{l}M_{k}.

(4.8)

The expression on the right-hand side of (
4.8
) does not depend on
n.
Therefore,

\left|\widetilde{t}_{p,1}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{p}M_{l}M_{k},\text{ \ \ \ for \ \ \ }x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.

(4.9)

By combining (
1.1
) with (
4.9
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{t}_{p,1}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,j\in\{l+1,...,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|\widetilde{t}_{p,1}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\widetilde{t}_{p,1}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}...m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{N}^{p}M_{k}^{p}

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}}{M_{l}}+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{1-p}}<c<\infty.

The proof is complete by using this estimate and Lemma
1.39
.

Theorem 4.4

Let the sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be non-decreasing. Then the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{t}_{1}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{1/2}.

Remark 4.5

Since the Fejér means are examples of Nörlund means with
non-decreasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
we
immediately obtain from part b) of Theorem
3.8
that the
asymptotic behaviour of the sequence of weights

\left\{1/\log^{2}\left(n+1\right):n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

in Nörlund means in Theorem
4.4
can not be improved.

Proof
: The idea of proof is similar as that of part a) of Theorem
3.8
, but since this case is more general we give the details.

Analogously to Theorem
4.2
we may assume that
n>M_{N}

and
a
be a
p
-atom with support
I=I_{N}.
Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{2}
if we apply (
4.7
) we obtain that

\frac{\left|t_{n}\left(a\right)\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}

\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\frac{M_{N}^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
Then, from Lemma
1.27

it follows that

\frac{\left|t_{n}\left(a\right)\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\leq\frac{cM_{N}^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}\frac{M_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}}=\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}

and

\left|\widetilde{t_{1}}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{N^{2}}.

(4.10)

By combining (
1.1
) with (
4.10
) we find that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\widetilde{t_{1}}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,j\in\{l+1,...,N-1\}}^{m_{j}-1}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t_{1}}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\widetilde{t_{1}}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}...m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}\frac{M_{l}^{1/2}M_{k}^{1/2}}{N}

+c\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}\frac{M_{N}^{1/2}M_{k}^{1/2}}{N}

\leq c\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{1/2}}{NM_{l}^{1/2}}

+c\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}^{1/2}}\frac{M_{k}^{1/2}}{N}\leq c<\infty.

The proof is complete.

The next results deal with a Nörlund means with non-increasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.
We begin by stating a divergence
result for all such summability methods when
0<p<1/2
, which can be found
in Persson, Tephnade and Wall
[
50
]
.

Theorem 4.6

Let
0<p<1/2.
Then, for all Nörlund means with
non-increasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
there
exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Proof
:
For the proof we use the martingale defined in Example
1.49
(see
also the part b) of Theorem
4.1
).

It is obvious that for every non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
it automatically holds that

\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\geq\frac{1}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}.

Since

t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1-j}S_{j}f

+\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f:=I+II.

by combining (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) we see that

\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right|\geq\left|II\right|-\left|I\right|\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

Analogously to (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) we get that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 4.7

Let
0<p<1/2\ \
and
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with
non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}.
Then the maximal
operator
t^{\ast}
is not bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}

to the space
weak-L_{p},
that is there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},

such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Next, we present necessary condition for the Nörlund means with
non-increasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
, when
1/2\leq p<1,
which can be found in Persson, Tephnade and
Wall
[
50
]
.

Theorem 4.8

a) Let
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right),

0<\alpha\leq 1,
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a non-increasing sequence
satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{\alpha}}{Q_{n}}=c>0,\text{ }0<\alpha\leq 1.

(4.11)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

(4.12)

b) Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a non-increasing sequence satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{\alpha}}{Q_{n}}=\infty,\text{ \ \ \ }\left(0<\alpha\leq 1\right).

(4.13)

Then there exists an martingale
f\in H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)},
such
that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}=\infty.

(4.14)

Proof
:
Under condition (
4.11
) there exists an increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
of positive integers such that

\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\geq c,\text{ \ }k\in\mathbb{N}

and the estimates (
1.71
)-(
1.73
) are satisfied. To prove part a) we use the martingale defined in Example
1.49
in the case when
0<p<q=1/\left(1+\alpha\right).

Since

t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1-j}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f

:=I+II,

by combining (
4.3
) and (
4.4
), we get that

\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right|\geq\left|II\right|-\left|I\right|

=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}-\frac{2\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}.

Without lost the generality we may assume that
c=1
in (
4.11
). By
using (
1.73
) we find that

\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right|

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{2\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{4\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{\lambda M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{8\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}

and

\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{8\alpha_{k}}\cdot\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right|\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{8\alpha_{k}}\right\}

=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{8\alpha_{k}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ when }k\rightarrow\infty.

Which means that
4.12
holds and part (a) is proved.

Under condition (
4.13
) there exists an increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
which satisfies the conditions

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\leq c<\infty,

(4.15)

\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}Q_{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{\alpha/2+1}\leq Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2+1}

(4.16)

and

32\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha/2+1}<\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}},

(4.17)

where
\lambda=\sup_{n}m_{n}.

Let the martingale defined in the Example
1.47
, where
\lambda_{k}

is defined by

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}

for which the sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
satisfies conditions (
4.15
)-(
4.17
) and
a_{k}

are given by (
1.56
) for
p=1/\left(1+\alpha\right).
If we apply (
4.15
) analogously we can conclude that
\,f\in H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}.
Hence,

\widehat{f}(j)=

(4.18)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2},&\text{\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}\text{ \ }k\in\mathbb{N},\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\end{array}\right.

and

t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f

=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1-j}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f:=III+IV.

Let
M_{2\alpha_{s}}<

j\leq M_{2\alpha_{s}+1},
where
s=0,...,k-1.

From (
4.18
) we have that

\left|S_{j}f\right|\leq\left|\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}Q_{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}+1}^{1/2}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{\alpha/2}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)\right|

(4.19)

+Q_{M_{2\alpha_{s}}+1}^{1/2}M_{2\alpha_{s}}^{\alpha/2}\left|\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{s}}}}\right)\right|\leq 4\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{s}}+1}^{1/2}M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha/2+1}.

Let
M_{\alpha_{s-1}+1}+1\leq

j\leq M_{\alpha_{s}},
where
\ s=1,...,k.
Then

\left|S_{j}f\right|=\left|\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}Q_{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}+1}^{1/2}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{\alpha/2}\left(D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)\right|

\leq\lambda\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}Q_{M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{\eta}}^{\alpha/2+1}\leq 2\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{s-1}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{s-1}}^{\alpha/2+1}

\leq 2\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha/2+1}

and

\left|III\right|\leq\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1-j}\left|S_{j}f\right|

(4.20)

\leq 2\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha/2+1}\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1-j}

\leq 2\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha/2+1}.

If we apply (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) we get that

\left|IV\right|\geq Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}M_{\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}\frac{q_{0}\left|D_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}-D_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\right|}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}

(4.21)

-\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}\left|S_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}f\right|

\geq\frac{q_{0}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}}-2\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha/2+1}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}{4Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}}.

By combining (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) we find that

\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\right|\geq\left|IV\right|-\left|III\right|

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}{4Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}}-2\lambda Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}+1}M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha/2+1}\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}{8Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}}.

Hence, it yields that

\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{8Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}{8Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}}\right\}

=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}{8Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}}\mu\left(G_{m}\right)=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha/2}}{8Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{1/2}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ when \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Which means that
4.14
holds and the proof is complete.

Corollary 4.9

Let
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right),

0<\alpha\leq 1
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a non-increasing
sequence satisfying the condition (
4.11
). Then there exists a
martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\left\|t^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Corollary 4.10

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a non-increasing sequence satisfying the condition (
4.13
).
Then there exists an martingale
f\in H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
such
that

\left\|t^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}=\infty.

Our next result reads:

Theorem 4.11

a) The maximal operator
t^{\ast}
of the Nörlund
summability method with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\},
satisfying the condition (
1.39
) and (
1.40
) is bounded from the
Hardy space
H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
to the space
weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)},
for
0<\alpha\leq 1.

b) Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a non-increasing
sequence satisfying the conditions

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{\alpha}}{Q_{n}}\geq c_{\alpha}>0

(4.22)

and

\left|q_{n}-q_{n+1}\right|\geq c_{\alpha}n^{\alpha-2},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}\text{.}

(4.23)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
such
that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}=\infty.

Remark 4.12

Part a) of this result can be found in
[
50
]
, but below we give a
different proof. Part b) of the theorem is new.

Proof
:
By Lemma
1.40
the proof of part a) is complete if we show
that

t\mu\left\{x\in\overline{I_{N}}:t^{\ast}f\geq t^{1+\alpha}\right\}\leq c<\infty,\text{ \ \ \ \ }t\geq 0,

for every
1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
-atom
a.
We may assume that
a
is
an arbitrary
1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
-atom with support
\ I,

\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}
and
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
t_{m}\left(a\right)=0,
when
m\leq M_{N}.
Therefore we can suppose that
m>M_{N}.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
t_{m}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(the boundedness follows from Corollary
1.33
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq cM_{N}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}

we obtain that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq M_{N}^{1+\alpha}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N
and
m>M_{N}.
From Corollary
1.35
we get that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{l}^{\alpha}.

(4.24)

The expression on the right-hand side of (
4.24
) does not depend on
m.
Hence,

\left|t^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{l}^{\alpha}

(4.25)

Let
n\geq N.
According to (
4.25
) we conclude that

\mu\left\{x\in\overline{I_{N}}:t^{\ast}f\geq c_{\alpha}M_{n}^{1+\alpha}\right\}=0.

Thus, we can suppose that
0<n<N.
Let
\lambda=\sup_{n}m_{n}
and
\left[x\right]
denotes the integer part of
x.
It is obvious that for fixed
\lambda
there exists a positive number
\theta
so that
\lambda^{1/\theta}\leq 2.
Then, for every
k<n
, it yields that

M_{k}M_{n+\left[\left(n-k\right)/\theta\right]}^{\alpha}\leq M_{k}M_{n}^{\alpha}\lambda^{\left[\left(n-k-1\right)/\theta\right]}

\leq M_{k}M_{n}^{\alpha}\left(\lambda^{1/\theta}\right)^{n-k-1}\leq M_{k}M_{n}^{\alpha}2^{n-k-1}

\leq M_{k}M_{n}^{\alpha}m_{k}m_{k+1}...m_{n-1}\leq M_{n}^{1+\alpha}.

It is obvious that if
n+\left[\left(n-k-1\right)/\lambda\right]>N
,
for some
k<l<N
we readily get that

c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{l}^{\alpha}\leq c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{N}^{\alpha}\leq c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{n+\left[\left(n-k-1\right)/\lambda\right]}^{\alpha}\leq c_{\alpha}M_{n}^{1+\alpha}.

It follows that for such
k<l<N
we have the following estimate

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{\alpha}M_{n}^{1+\alpha},\text{ for }x\in I_{N}^{k,l}

and

\mu\left\{x\in I_{N}^{k,l}:t^{\ast}f\geq c_{\alpha}M_{n}^{1+\alpha}\right\}=0.

Therefore, we may assume that
n+\left[\left(n-k-1\right)/\lambda\right]\leq N.

By combining (
1.1
) and (
4.25
) we obtain that

\mu\left\{x\in\overline{I_{N}}:t^{\ast}f\geq c_{\alpha}M_{n}^{1+\alpha}\right\}

\leq\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=n}{\sum}}\overset{N}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,\text{ }j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\left|I_{N}^{k,l}\right|

+\overset{n}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N}{\underset{l=n+\left[\left(n-k-1\right)/\lambda\right]}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,\text{ }j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\left|I_{N}^{k,l}\right|

\leq\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=n}{\sum}}\overset{N}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}}+\overset{n}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N}{\underset{l=n+\left[\left(n-k\right)/\theta\right]}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}}\leq\frac{c}{M_{n}}.

Hence,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}M_{n}\mu\left\{x\in\overline{I_{N}}:t^{\ast}f\geq M_{n}^{1+\alpha}\right\}\leq c<\infty.

Part a) is proved.

Under condition (
4.22
) there exists an increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
of positive integers such that

\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}^{\alpha}}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}}>c_{\alpha}>0,\text{ \ }k\in\mathbb{N},

(4.26)

and estimates (
1.71
)-(
1.73
) are satisfied. To prove part b) of
Theorem
4.8
we use the martingale defined in Example
1.49
in the case when
p=q=1/\left(1+\alpha\right).

In particular, as we proved there, the martingale belongs to the space
H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}.

Moreover,

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}},&j\in\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\text{,}\\
0,&\text{\thinspace\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{2\alpha_{k}},...,M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

We can write that

t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}f=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}-j}S_{j}f

+\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}-j}S_{j}f:=I+II.

According to (
1.76
) we can conclude that

\left|I\right|\leq\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}-j}\left|S_{j}f\right|

(4.27)

\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha+1}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}-j}

\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha+1}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha}}{16\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}.

Let
x\in I_{s}/I_{s+1}
and
M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1\leq

j\leq M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}.
In view of the second inequality of (
1.75
) in the case
when
l=k
and
p=1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
we can write that

S_{j}f=S_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}f+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha}\psi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

Hence, it yields that

II=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}-j}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha}\psi_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}

+\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}-j}S_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}f

:=II_{1}+II_{2}.

By using again (
1.76
) we get that

\left|II_{2}\right|\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha+1}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}-j}

\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{\alpha+1}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha+1}}{16\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

Let
x\in I_{s}/I_{s+1},
for
\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]<s\leq\alpha_{k}.
Then, according to (
4.26
) we find that

\left|II_{1}\right|=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\left|\frac{\psi_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}D_{j}\right|

=\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}j\right|

\geq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}Q_{M_{2\alpha_{k}+1}}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}j\right|

\geq\frac{c}{\lambda^{\alpha}\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}j\right|.

We invoke Abel transformation and apply (
4.23
) to get that

\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}j\right|

=\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}\left(q_{M_{2s}-j}-q_{M_{2s}-j-1}\right)\frac{j\left(j+1\right)}{2}\right|

\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{2}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\sum_{j=\left[M_{2s}/2\right]}^{M_{2s}}\left|q_{M_{2s}-j}-q_{M_{2s}-j-1}\right|j^{2}

\geq c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{2}\sum_{j=\left[M_{2s}/2\right]}^{M_{2s}}\left|q_{M_{2s}-j}-q_{M_{2s}-j-1}\right|

\geq c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\left[M_{2s}/2\right]}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|

\geq c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\left[M_{2s}/2\right]}\frac{1}{j^{\alpha-2}}\geq c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{\alpha-1}M_{2s}^{2}\geq c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{\alpha+1}.

Hence,

\left|II_{1}\right|\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}j\right|\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{\alpha+1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

By now using the estimates above we obtain that

\int_{G_{m}}\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}f\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu\geq\left|II_{1}\right|-\left|II_{2}\right|-\left|I\right|

(4.28)
