\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{1+\alpha}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}-\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k}}^{\alpha+1}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{2s}^{1+\alpha}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

By combining (
4.27
) and (
4.28
) we find that

\int_{G_{m}}\left|t^{\ast}f\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu

\geq\sum_{s=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]}^{\alpha_{k}-1}\int_{I_{s}/I_{s+1}}\left|t_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}+M_{2s}}f\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu

\geq c_{\alpha}\sum_{s=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]}^{\alpha_{k}-1}\frac{M_{2s}}{M_{2s}\alpha_{k}^{1/2\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\geq c_{\alpha}\sum_{s=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]}^{\alpha_{k}-1}\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2\left(1+\alpha\right)}}

\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\sum_{s=\left[\alpha_{k}/2\right]}^{\alpha_{k}-1}1

\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}\alpha_{k}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\geq c_{\alpha}\alpha_{k}^{1/2}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ when \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Our next result reads:

Theorem 4.13

Let
f\in H_{p},
where
0

<p\,<1/\left()1+\alpha\right)
for some
0<\alpha\leq 1,
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers satisfying conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
). Then the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{t}_{p,\alpha}^{\ast}:=\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}}

is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

b) Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{\Phi_{n}}=+\infty.

(4.29)

Then there exists Nörlund means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying the conditions (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) such
that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left\|\frac{t_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}f_{k}}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\right\|_{weal-L_{p}}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}}=\infty.

Remark 4.14

Part a) can be found in the paper Blahota and Tephnadze
[
6
]
, while part b) has not been stated before for such a general case.

Proof
:
Since the Nörlund means
t_{n}
are bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}

(the boundedness follows from Corollary
1.33
), according to
Lemma
1.39
it actually suffices to show that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t}_{p,\alpha}^{\ast}a\right|^{p}d\mu<c

for some constant
c\
and every
p
-atom
a
. We may assume that
a
is an
arbitrary
p
-atom with support
\ I,

\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}
and

I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
S_{n}\left(a\right)=t_{n}\left(a\right)=0
when
n\leq M_{N}.
Therefore we can suppose that
n>M_{N}.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}

we obtain that

\left|t_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq M_{N}^{1/p}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l<N.
Then, from Lemma
1.34
we get
that

\left|t_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{c_{\alpha,p}M_{N}^{1/p-1}M_{l}^{\alpha}M_{k}}{n^{\alpha}}.

(4.30)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},\,0\leq k<N.
Then, according to Lemma
1.34
,
we have that

\left|t_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{\alpha,p}M_{N}^{1/p-1}M_{k}.

(4.31)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
Since
n>M_{N}
we can conclude
that

\frac{\left|t_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|}{n^{1/p-1-\alpha}}\leq c_{\alpha,p}M_{l}^{\alpha}M_{k}.

(4.32)

The expression on the right-hand side of (
4.32
) does not depend on
n.
Hence, we can conclude that

\left|\overset{\sim}{t}_{p,\alpha}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{\alpha,p}M_{l}^{\alpha}M_{k}

(4.33)

for
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.

By combining (
1.1
) and (
4.33
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t}_{p,\alpha}^{\ast}a\right|^{p}d\mu

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j}-1}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t}_{p,\alpha}^{\ast}a\right|^{p}d\mu

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t}_{p,\alpha}^{\ast}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\leq c_{\alpha,p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}}M_{l}^{\alpha p}M_{k}^{p}+c_{\alpha,p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{N}^{1-p}M_{k}^{p}

\leq c_{\alpha,p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}M_{k}^{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}^{1-\alpha p}}+c_{\alpha,p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}\leq c_{\alpha,p}<\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Let
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right).
Under condition (
4.29
) there
exists positive integers
n_{k}
such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\left(M_{2n_{k}}+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}=\infty,\text{ \ \ }0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right).

To prove part b) we apply the
p
-atoms defined in Example
1.44
.
Under conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
), if we invoke (
1.52
) and (
1.53
) we find that

\frac{\left|t_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}f_{k}\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}=\frac{\left|S_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}\right|}{Q_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}

=\frac{q_{0}\left|D_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}-D_{M_{2n_{k}}}\right|}{Q_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}=\frac{q_{0}\left|\psi_{M_{2n_{k}}}\right|}{Q_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}.

Hence,

\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\frac{\left|t_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}\right\}=1.

(4.34)

By combining (
1.54
) and (
4.34
) we have that

\frac{\frac{c_{\alpha}}{M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}\left(\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\frac{\left|S_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}f_{k}\left(x\right)\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}\geq\frac{q_{0}}{M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}\right\}\right)^{1/p}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}}

\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{2n_{k}}^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ when \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 4.15

Let
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p,\alpha},

depending only on
p
and
\alpha
, such that

\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p,\alpha}\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

Proof
:
According to part a) of Theorem
4.13
we conclude that

\left\|\frac{t_{n}f}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}}\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}}\right\|_{p}

\leq c_{p,\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 4.16

Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition (
4.29
). Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{t_{n}f}{\Phi_{n}}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Corollary 4.17

Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition (
4.29
). Then the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p}.

We now formulate our final result in this section.

Theorem 4.18

Let
f\in H_{1/(1+\alpha)},
where
0<\alpha\leq 1

and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers
satisfying the conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
). Then there exists an absolute
constant
c_{\alpha}
depending only on
\alpha
such that the maximal
operator

\overset{\sim}{t}_{\alpha}^{\ast}:=\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\log^{1+\alpha}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{1/(1+\alpha)}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{1/(1+\alpha)}.

b) Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log^{1+\alpha}\left(n+1\right)}{\Phi_{n}}=+\infty.

(4.35)

Then there exists Nörlund means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying the conditions (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) such
that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{n}\left|\frac{t_{n}f_{k}}{\Phi_{n}}\right|\right\|_{1/(1+\alpha)}}{\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/(1+\alpha)}}}=\infty.

Remark 4.19

Part a) of this result can be found in the paper Bhahota and Tephnadze
[
7
]
. Part b) has not been stated before for such a general case.

Proof
:
According to Lemma
1.39
the proof of part a) will be complete if
we show that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t}_{\alpha}^{\ast}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu<\infty

for every
1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
-atom
a.
We may assume that
a
is
an arbitrary
1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
-atom with support
\ I,

\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}
and
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
t_{m}\left(a\right)=0,
when
m\leq M_{N}.
Therefore we can suppose that
m>M_{N}.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
t_{m}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(the boundedness follows from Corollary
1.33
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}

we obtain that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\left\|a\left(x\right)\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq M_{N}^{1+\alpha}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l<N.
From Lemma
1.34
we get that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{l}^{\alpha}M_{N}^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}}.

(4.36)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},\,0\leq k<N.
In the view of Lemma
1.34
we
have that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{N}^{\alpha}.

(4.37)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
Since
n>M_{N}
we can conclude
that

\frac{\left|t_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|}{\log^{1+\alpha}n}\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{l}^{\alpha}}{N^{1+\alpha}}.

(4.38)

The expression on the right-hand side of (
4.38
) does not depend on
n.
Hence,

\left|\overset{\sim}{t_{\alpha}}^{\ast}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}M_{k}M_{N}^{\alpha}}{N^{1+\alpha}},

(4.39)

for
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.

According to (
1.1
) and (
4.39
) we obtain that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t_{\alpha}}^{\ast}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j}-1}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t_{\alpha}}^{\ast}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\overset{\sim}{t_{\alpha}}^{\ast}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu

\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{N}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}}M_{l}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}

+\frac{c_{\alpha}}{N}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{N}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}

\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{N}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{M_{l}}

+\frac{c_{\alpha}}{N}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{M_{N}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\leq c_{\alpha}<\infty.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Under condition (
4.35
) there exists a positive integers
m_{k}^{,}

such that
M_{2m_{k}^{,}+1}\leq\lambda_{k}<2M_{2m_{k}^{,}+1}.
Since
\Phi_{n}
is a non-decreasing function we have that

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{\left(m_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}\right)^{1+\alpha}}{\Phi_{M_{2m_{k}^{,}+1}}}\geq c\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log^{1+\alpha}\left(\lambda_{k}\right)}{\Phi_{M_{2\lambda_{k}+1}}}=\infty.

Let
\ \left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}\subset\left\{m_{k}^{\prime}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be a sequence of positive
numbers such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{n_{k}^{1+\alpha}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}}=\infty.

To prove part b) we use the
1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
-atoms defined in
Example
1.44
. If we apply (
1.11
) in Lemma
1.2
with (
1.52
) and (
1.53
) and invoke Abel transformation we get that

\frac{\left|t_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}f_{k}\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}}=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}Q_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\left|\sum_{j=M_{2n_{k}}+1}^{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}q_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}-j}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{2n_{k}}}\right)\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}Q_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}\left(D_{j+M_{2n_{k}}}-D_{M_{2n_{k}}}\right)\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}Q_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\left|\psi_{M_{2n_{k}}}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}D_{j}\right|

=\frac{1}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}Q_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}q_{M_{2s}-j}j\right|.

Let
x\in I_{2s}/I_{2s+1},

s=\left[n_{k}/2\right],...,n_{k}.
If we
again use abel transformation, then under the conditions (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) we find that

\frac{\left|t_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}f_{k}\right|}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\geq\frac{c}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2s}}\left(q_{M_{2s}-j}-q_{M_{2s}-j-1}\right)j^{2}\right|

\geq\frac{c}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}}\sum_{j=\left[M_{2s}\right]/2}^{M_{2s}}\left|q_{M_{2s}-j}-q_{M_{2s}-j-1}\right|j^{2}

\geq\frac{cM_{2s}^{2}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}}\sum_{j=\left[M_{2s}\right]/2}^{M_{2s}}\left|q_{M_{2s}-j}-q_{M_{2s}-j-1}\right|

\geq\frac{cM_{2s}^{2}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}}\sum_{j=1}^{\left[M_{2s}\right]/2}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|

\geq\frac{cM_{2s}^{2}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}}\sum_{j=1}^{\left[M_{2s}\right]/2}j^{\alpha-2}\geq\frac{cM_{2s}^{2}M_{2s}^{\alpha-1}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}}

\geq\frac{cM_{2s}^{\alpha+1}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}}\geq\frac{cM_{2s}^{\alpha+1}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha}}.

Hence,

\int_{G_{m}}\left(\sup_{n}\left|\frac{t_{n}f_{k}}{\Phi_{n}}\right|\right)^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu

\geq\text{ }\overset{n_{k}-1}{\underset{s=\left[n_{k}/2\right]}{\sum}}\int_{I_{2s}/I_{2s+1}}\left|\frac{t_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}f_{k}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}}+M_{2s}}}\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu

\geq\frac{c}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\text{ }\overset{n_{k}-1}{\underset{s=\left[n_{k}/2\right]}{\sum}}\int_{I_{2s}/I_{2s+1}}\frac{M_{2s}}{M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}d\mu

\geq\frac{c}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\text{ }\overset{n_{k}-1}{\underset{s=\left[n_{k}/2\right]}{\sum}}\frac{M_{2s}^{\alpha+1}}{M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\frac{1}{M_{2s}^{\alpha+1}}

\geq\frac{c}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\text{ }\overset{n_{k}-1}{\underset{s=\left[n_{k}/2\right]}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\geq\frac{cn_{k}}{M_{2n_{k}}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}.

Therefore, by also using (
1.54
) for
p=1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
we
have that

\frac{\left(\int_{G_{m}}\left(\sup_{n}\left|\frac{t_{n}f_{k}}{\Phi_{n}}\right|\right)^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu\right)^{1+\alpha}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}}

\geq\frac{cn_{k}^{1+\alpha}}{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{\alpha}\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}}M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{\alpha}\geq\frac{cn_{k}^{1+\alpha}}{\Phi_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{when \qquad}k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 4.20

Let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be any non-decreasing
sequence satisfying the condition (
4.35
). Then the following maximal operator

\sup_{n}\left|\frac{t_{n}f}{\Phi_{n}}\right|

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
to the
space
L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}.

4.3
Strong convergence of Nörlund means on martingale Hardy
spaces

The first result in this section is due to Persson, Tephnadze and Wall

[
51
]
.

Theorem 4.21

Let
0<p<1/2
,
f\in H_{p}
and the sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be non-decreasing
.
Then there
exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

Remark 4.22

Since the Fejér means are examples of Nörlund means with
non-decreasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
we
immediately obtain from part b) of Theorem
3.25
that
the asymptotic behaviour of the sequence of weights

\left\{1/k^{2-2p}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

in Nörlund means in Theorem
4.21
can not be improved.

Proof
:
The proof is similar to that of part a) of Theorem
3.25
, but since this case is more general we give the details.

By Lemma
1.38
the proof is complete if we show that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{m}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}

(4.40)

for every
p
-atom
a
with support
\ I
,
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.

We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
S_{n}\left(a\right)=t_{n}\left(a\right)=0,
when
n\leq M_{N}
. Therefore, we can suppose
that
n>M_{N}
.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since Nörlund means
t_{n}
with non-decreasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
are bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(the boundedness follows from Corollary
1.23
) and

\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
we obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{p}}{M_{N}}\leq 1,\text{ \ }0<p\leq 1/2.

Hence,

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-2p}}

(4.41)

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c<\infty.

It is easy to see that

\left|t_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|

=\left|\int_{I_{N}}a\left(t\right)\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)\right|

\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq M_{N}^{1/p}\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{n-k}D_{k}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
Then, in the view of Lemma
1.27
, we get that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c_{p}M_{l}M_{k}M_{N}^{1/p-2},\text{ for }0<p<1/2.

(4.42)

According to (
1.1
) with (
4.42
) we find that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu

(4.43)

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,\text{ }j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu

+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\leq c_{p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}\dotsm m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}

+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{k}^{p}M_{N}^{1-p}

\leq c_{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{p}M_{k}^{p}}{M_{l}}

+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}\leq cM_{N}^{1-2p}.

Moreover, according to (
4.43
), we get that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-2p}}

\leq c_{p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{N}^{1-2p}}{m^{2-2p}}<c<\infty,\text{ \ }\left(0<p<1/2\right).

Now, by combining this estimate with (
4.41
) we obtain (
4.40
)
so the proof is complete.

Also the next theorem is proved in Persson, Tephnadze and Wall
[
51
]
.

Theorem 4.23

Let
f\in H_{1/2}
and the sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be non-decreasing satisfying condition (
1.28
). Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

Proof
:
The proof is similar to that of part a) of Theorem
3.28
, but since this case is more general we present the details.

According to Lemma
1.38
it suffices to show that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{m}a\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{m}\leq c

for every
p
-atom
a
with support
\ I
,
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.

We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
S_{n}\left(a\right)=t_{n}\left(a\right)=0
when
n\leq M_{N}
. Therefore we can suppose
that
n>M_{N}
.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
t_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(the boundedness follows from Corollary
1.23
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{2}
we obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{1/2}}{M_{N}}\leq c<\infty.

Hence,

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu}{m}

(4.44)

\leq\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m}\leq c<\infty,\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}\text{.}

It is easy to see that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq M_{N}^{2}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l<N.
Then, in the view of Lemma
1.25
, we get that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}M_{N}}{m}.

(4.45)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N}.
Then, according to Lemma
1.25
, we find that

\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq cM_{k}M_{N}.

(4.46)

By combining (
4.45
) and (
4.46
) with (
1.1
) we can conclude
that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\leq c\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}\dotsm m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}\frac{M_{l}^{1/2}M_{k}^{1/2}M_{N}^{1/2}}{m^{1/2}}

+c\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{k}^{1/2}M_{N}^{1/2}

\leq cM_{N}^{1/2}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{1/2}M_{k}^{1/2}}{m^{1/2}M_{l}}+c\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{1/2}}{M_{N}^{1/2}}

\leq\frac{cM_{N}^{1/2}N}{m^{1/2}}+c.

It follows that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)}{m}

(4.47)

\leq\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\left(\frac{cM_{N}^{1/2}N}{m^{3/2}}+\frac{c}{m}\right)<c<\infty.

The proof is complete by just combining (
4.44
) and (
4.47
).

Next, we investigate Nörlund means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}.
At first we consider the case
0

<p\,<1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
where
0<\alpha<1.
For details see the
paper of Blahota and Tephnadze
[
6
]
.

Theorem 4.24

Let
f\in H_{p},
where
0

<p\,<1/\left(1+\alpha\right),

0<\alpha\leq 1
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\},
be a
sequence of non-increasing numbers satisfying the conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
). Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{\alpha,p},
depending
only on
\alpha
and
p
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}}{k^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}\leq c_{\alpha,p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

Proof
:
By Lemma
1.38
the it suffices to show that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{m}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}\leq c_{\alpha,p}<\infty

for every
p
-atom
a.
Analogously to the proofs of the previous theorems we
may assume that
a
be an arbitrary
p
-atom with support
I,\ \mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}
and
I=I_{N}
and
m>M_{N}.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
t_{m}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(the boundedness follows from Corollary
1.33
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
we obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{p}M_{N}^{-1}\leq 1.

Hence

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}

\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}\leq c_{\alpha,p}<\infty.

According to (
1.1
) and (
4.30
)-(
4.31
) we can conclude
that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j}-1}\frac{\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}

+\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}

\leq c_{\alpha,p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\left(\frac{M_{N}^{1-p}}{m^{2-p}}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{p\alpha}M_{k}^{p}}{M_{l}}+\frac{M_{N}^{1-p}}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}}\right).

Since

\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{l}^{p\alpha}M_{k}^{p}}{M_{l}}\leq\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}M_{k}^{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}^{1-p\alpha}}

\leq\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{k}^{1-p\alpha}}M_{k}^{p}\leq\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{k}^{1-p\left(\alpha+1\right)}}<c<\infty

and

\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}}\leq M_{N}^{p-1}<\infty

we obtain that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}

<c_{\alpha,p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m^{2-p}}+c_{\alpha,p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m^{2-\left(1+\alpha\right)p}}\leq c_{\alpha,p}<\infty.

The proof is complete.

The final result in this section is due to Blahota, Persson and
Tephnadze
[
7
]
.

Theorem 4.25

Let
f\in H_{1/(1+\alpha)}
where
0<\alpha\leq 1
and

\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers
satisfying the conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
). Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{\alpha}
depending only on
\alpha
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{m}f\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{m}\leq c_{\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}.

Proof
:

By Lemma
1.38
the proof is complete if we show
that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{m}a\right\|_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{m}\leq c_{\alpha}<\infty

for every
1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
-atom
a.
Analogously to the proofs
of previous resalts we may assume that
a
is an arbitrary
1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
-atom with support
\ I,

\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}

and
I=I_{N}
and
m>M_{N}.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
t_{m}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(the boundedness follows from Corollary
1.33
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1+\alpha}
we obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu

\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}/M_{N}\leq 1.

Hence

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}}\left|t_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu}{m}

\leq\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m}\leq c_{\alpha}<\infty.

According to (
1.1
) together with (
4.36
)-(
4.37
) we can conclude that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu}{m}

=\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,\text{ }j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu}{m}

+\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu}{m}

\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{N}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{m^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)+1}}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}M_{l}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\frac{m_{l+1}\dotsm m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}

+\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{mM_{N}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{N}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}.

Since

\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}M_{l}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\frac{m_{l+1}\dotsm m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}M_{l}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\frac{1}{M_{l}}

=\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{l}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}

\leq\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\frac{1}{M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\leq\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}1\leq N

and

\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}M_{k}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{N}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}

\leq M_{N}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}M_{N}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\leq M_{N}

we obtain that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|t_{m}a\right|^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}d\mu}{m}

\leq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{\log n}\left(\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{NM_{N}^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{m^{\alpha/\left(1+\alpha\right)+1}}+\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m}\right)<c_{\alpha}<\infty.

The proof is complete.

4.4
Maximal operators of Riesz and Nörlund logarithmic means on martingale Hardy spaces

In our previous sections we investigated Nörlund means with
non-increasing sequences
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
, but the
case when
q_{k}=1/k
was excluded, since this sequence does not satisfies the
condition (
1.39
) for any
0<\alpha\leq 1
. On the other hand, Riesz
logarithmic means are not examples of Nörlund means. In this subsection we
fill up this gap simultaneously for both cases.

Both theorems in this
section are due to Tephnadze
[
75
]
.

Theorem 4.26

a)

The maximal operator of Riesz logarithmic
means
R^{\ast}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.

b) Let
0<p\leq 1/2
. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\left\|R^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}=+\infty.

Proof
:
By using Abel transformation we obtain that

R_{n}f=\frac{1}{l_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\frac{\sigma_{j}f}{j+1}+\frac{\sigma_{n}f}{l_{n}}.

Consequently,

R^{\ast}f\leq c\sigma^{\ast}f.

(4.48)

Since
\sigma^{\ast}
is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{1/2}

to the space
weak-L_{1/2}
by using (
4.48
) we can conclude that

\left\|R^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/2}}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}

and the proof of part a) is complete.

Let
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be martingale defined in Example
1.49
.

Set
q_{n}^{s}=M_{2n}+M_{2s}-1,

n>s.
Then we can write that

R_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}f=\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}{\sum}}\frac{S_{j}f}{j}

=\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\sum}}\frac{S_{j}f}{j}+\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\underset{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}+1}{\overset{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}{\sum}}\frac{S_{j}f}{j}:=I+II.

According to (
1.76
) we have that

\left|I\right|\leq\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|S_{j}f\left(x\right)\right|}{j}

\leq\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2\alpha_{k}}-1}\frac{1}{j}

\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}.

Let
M_{2\alpha_{k}}\leq j\leq q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}.
According to the
second inequality of (
1.75
) in the case when
l=k,
we deduce that

S_{j}f=S_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}f+\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{{}_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}.

(4.49)

Hence, we can rewrite
II
as

II=\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\underset{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}{\sum}}\ \frac{S_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}f}{j}

(4.50)

+\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{{}_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}\frac{D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}{j}

:=II_{1}+II_{2}.

In view of (
1.76
) we find that

\left|II_{1}\right|\leq\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\underset{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}{\sum}}\ \frac{1}{j}\left|S_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}f\right|

(4.51)

\leq\left|S_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}f\right|\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}.

Let
0<p\leq 1/2,

x\in I_{2s}\backslash I_{2s+1}
for
s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right],\ldots,\alpha_{k}.
Since

\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{D_{{}_{j}}\left(x\right)}{{}_{j+M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}\geq\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{j}{{}_{j+M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}

\geq\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{j}{{}_{2M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}\geq\frac{cM_{2s}^{2}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}

we obtain that

\left|II_{2}\right|=\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\left|\psi_{{}_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{D_{j}}{{}_{j+M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}\right|

(4.52)

\geq\frac{c}{\alpha_{k}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\frac{M_{2s}^{2}}{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}\geq\frac{cM_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}M_{2s}^{2}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}.

By combining (
1.71
)-(
1.73
) with (
4.50
)-(
4.52
) we get that

\left|R_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}f\right|=\left|II_{2}-I-II_{1}\right|

\geq\left|II_{2}\right|-\left|I\right|-\left|II_{1}\right|\geq\left|II_{2}\right|-\frac{2\lambda M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}

\geq\frac{cM_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}M_{2s}^{2}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}-\frac{cM_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}

\geq\frac{cM_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}M_{2s}^{2}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}.

Hence,

\int_{G_{m}}\left|R^{\ast}f\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\geq c_{p}\sum_{s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right]}^{\alpha_{k}}\underset{I_{2s}\backslash I_{2s+1}}{\int}\left|R_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}f\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\geq c_{p}\overset{\alpha_{k}}{\underset{s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right]}{\sum}}\underset{I_{2s}\backslash I_{2s+1}}{\int}\frac{cM_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1-2p}M_{2s}^{2p}}{\alpha_{k}^{3p/2}}d\mu\left(x\right)

\geq c_{p}\sum_{s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right]}^{\alpha_{k}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1-2p}M_{2s}^{2p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{3p/2}}

\geq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{c_{p}2^{\alpha_{k}(1-2p)/3}}{\alpha_{k}^{3p/2}},&\text{\thinspace when \ }0<p<1/2,\\
c\alpha_{k}^{1/4},&\text{when \ }p=1/2,\end{array}\right.\rightarrow\infty\text{, when \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Theorem 4.27

Let
0<p\leq 1
. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\left\|L^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}=+\infty.

Proof
:
We write that

L_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}f=\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k},s}}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}{\sum}}\frac{S_{j}f}{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}-j}

(4.53)

=\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\underset{j=1}{\overset{M_{2\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}\frac{S_{j}f}{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}-j}+\frac{1}{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}\underset{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}{\sum}}\frac{S_{j}f}{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}-j}:=III+IV.

In the view of (
1.76
) for
III
we get the following estimate:

\left|III\right|\leq\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}}\frac{1}{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}-j}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\text{ }\leq\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}.

(4.54)

Moreover, according to the second inequality of (
1.75
) in the case
when
l=k,
(see also (
4.49
)) we can rewrite
IV
as

IV=\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}\frac{S_{M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}f}{q_{\alpha_{k},s}-j}

(4.55)

+\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}\psi_{{}_{M_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}\frac{D_{j-M_{{}_{2\alpha_{k}}}}}{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}-j}:=IV_{1}+IV_{2}.

By now applying (
1.76
) again we have that

\left|IV_{1}\right|\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}.

(4.56)

Let
x\in I_{2s}\backslash I_{2s+1},

s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right],...,\alpha_{k}.
Since

\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{D_{j}}{{}_{M_{2s}}-j}=\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{j}{{}_{M_{2s}}-j}=\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{M_{2s}}{{}_{M_{2s}}-j}-\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{M_{2s}-j}{{}_{M_{2s}}-j}

=c_{p}sM_{2s}-M_{2s}\geq c_{p}sM_{2s},

we obtain that

\left|IV_{2}\right|=\frac{1}{l_{q_{\alpha_{k},s}}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{1/2}}\left|\sum_{j=0}^{M_{2s}-1}\frac{D_{j}}{{}_{M_{2s}}-j}\right|

(4.57)

\geq\frac{c_{p}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}sM_{2s},\qquad x\in I_{2s}/I_{2s+1}.

By combining (
1.73
) with (
4.53
)-(
4.57
) for
x\in I_{2s}\backslash I_{2s+1},s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right],...,\alpha_{k}

and
0<p\leq 1
we get that

\left|L_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}f\left(x\right)\right|=\left|III+IV_{1}+IV_{2}\right|\geq\left|IV_{2}\right|-\left|III\right|-\left|IV_{1}\right|

\geq\frac{c_{p}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}sM_{2s}-\frac{2\lambda M_{\alpha_{k}}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}\geq\frac{c_{p}M_{2\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}^{3/2}}sM_{2s}.

Consequently,

\int_{G_{m}}\left|L^{\ast}f\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)\geq\sum_{s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right]}^{\alpha_{k}}\int_{I_{2s}\backslash I_{2s+1}}\left|L^{\ast}f\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\geq\sum_{s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right]}^{\alpha_{k}}\int_{I_{2s}\backslash I_{2s+1}}\left|L_{q_{\alpha_{k}}^{s}}f\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\geq c_{p}\sum_{s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right]}^{\alpha_{k}}\int_{I_{2s}\backslash I_{2s+1}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1-p}}{\alpha_{k}^{3p/2}}s^{p}M_{2s}^{p}d\mu\geq c_{p}\underset{s=\left[2\alpha_{k}/3\right]}{\overset{\alpha_{k}}{\sum}}\frac{M_{2\alpha_{k-1}}^{1-p}}{\alpha_{k}^{p/2}}M_{2s}^{p-1}

\geq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{c_{p}2^{\alpha_{k}(1-p)/3}}{\alpha_{k}^{p/2}},&\text{when }0<p<1,\\
c\alpha_{k}^{1/2},&\text{when \ }p=1,\end{array}\right.\rightarrow\infty\text{, when \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

4.5
Applications

First we consider Nörlund means
t_{n}
with monotone and
bounded sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.
The results in
our previous sections in particular imply the following results:

Theorem 4.28

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}
and
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with monotone and
bounded sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c
such that

\left\|t^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/2}}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}.

b) There exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2},
such that

\left\|t^{\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}=\infty.

Theorem 4.29

a) Let
p>1/2
,
f\in H_{p}
and
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with monotone
and bounded sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.
Then there
exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\left\|\overset{\sim}{t_{p,1}}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

b) Let
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be a
non-decreasing function satisfying the condition (
3.6
). Then the following
maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p}.

Theorem 4.30

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}
and
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with monotone and
bounded sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c
such that

\left\|\overset{\sim}{t_{1}}^{\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}.

b) Let
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be a
non-decreasing function satisfying the condition (
3.11
). Then the following
maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|B_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the
Lebesgue space
L_{1/2}.

Theorem 4.31

Let
0<p<1/2
,
f\in H_{p}
and
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with monotone and
bounded sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.
Then there exists
an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{m}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

Theorem 4.32

Let
f\in H_{1/2}
and
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with monotone and bounded
sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{m}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{m}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2},\text{ \ }n=2,3,\ldots.

Next we remark that
\sigma_{n}
are Nörlund means with monotone and
bounded sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
and
\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}
are concrete examples of Nörlund means with
non-decreasing and unbounded sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
but they have similar boundedness properties. The results in our previous sections in particular imply the following
results:

Theorem 4.33

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}.
Then there exists absolute constant
c
such that

\left\|\sigma^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/2}}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}

and

\left\|\kappa^{\alpha,\beta,\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/2}}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}.

b) There exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}
such that

\left\|\sigma^{\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}=\infty

and

\left\|\kappa^{\alpha,\beta,\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}=\infty.

Theorem 4.34

a) Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\left\|\overset{\sim}{\sigma_{p}}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}

and

\left\|\widetilde{\kappa}_{p}^{\alpha,\beta,\ast}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

b) Let
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be a
non-decreasing function satisfying the condition (
3.6
). Then the following
maximal operators

\text{\ }\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}\text{ \ \ \ and \ \ \ }\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

are not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p}.

Theorem 4.35

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c
such that

\left\|\overset{\sim}{\sigma}^{\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}

and

\left\|\widetilde{\kappa}^{\alpha,\beta,\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}.

b) Let
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be a
nondecreasing function satisfying the condition (
3.11
). Then the following
maximal operators

\text{\ }\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}\text{ \ \ \ and \ \ \ }\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

are not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the
Lebesgue space
L_{1/2}.

Theorem 4.36

Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

and

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\kappa_{m}^{\alpha,\beta}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

Theorem 4.37

Let
f\in H_{1/2}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{m}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2},\text{ \ }n=2,3,\ldots,

and

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\kappa_{m}^{\alpha,\beta}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{m}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2},\text{ }n=2,3,\ldots.

Furthemore, we note that it is obvious that Cesàro
\left(C,\alpha\right)
and Riesz
\left(R,\alpha\right)
means are examples of Nörlund means with non-increasing
sequence satisfying the conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
). It follows that
all results concerning such summability methods are true also for the Cesàro
\left(C,\alpha\right)
and Riesz
\left(R,\alpha\right)

means. Hence, the results in our previous sections in particular imply the
following results:

Theorem 4.38

a) Let
0<p<1/2.
Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty

and

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\beta_{n}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Theorem 4.39

a) Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
f\in H_{1/(1+\alpha)}.
Then there exists
absolute constant
c_{\alpha}
depending only on
\alpha
such that

\left\|\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\leq c_{\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}

and

\left\|\beta^{\alpha,\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}\leq c_{\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}.

b) Let
0<\alpha\leq 1.
Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/(1+\alpha)}
such that

\left\|\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}f\right\|_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}=\infty

and

\left\|\beta^{\alpha,\ast}f\right\|_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}=\infty.

Theorem 4.40

a) Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
,
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
and
f\in H_{p}.

Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{\alpha,p}
depending only on
\alpha
and
p
such that

\left\|\overset{\sim}{\sigma_{p}}^{\alpha,\ast}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{\alpha,p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}

and

\left\|\overset{\sim}{\beta_{p}}^{\alpha,\ast}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{\alpha,p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

b) Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be a non-decreasing function satisfying the condition (
4.29
). Then the following maximal operators

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}\text{ \ \ \ and \ \ \ }\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\beta_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

are not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p}.

Theorem 4.41

a) Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
f\in H_{1/(1+\alpha)}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{\alpha}
depending only on
\alpha
such that

\left\|\overset{\sim}{\sigma}^{\alpha,\ast}f\right\|_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\leq c_{\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}

and

\left\|\overset{\sim}{\beta}^{\alpha,\ast}f\right\|_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}\leq c_{\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}.

b) Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be a nondecreasing function satisfying the condition (
4.35
). Then the following maximal operators

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}\text{ \ \ and \ \ \ \ \ }\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\beta_{n}^{\alpha}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

are not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}.

Theorem 4.42

Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
,
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
and
f\in H_{p}.

Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{\alpha,p}
depending only on
\alpha
and
p
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}^{\alpha}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{\left(1+\alpha\right)\left(1-p\right)}}\leq c_{\alpha,p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

and

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\beta_{m}^{\alpha}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{\left(1+\alpha\right)\left(1-p\right)}}\leq c_{\alpha,p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

Theorem 4.43

Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
f\in H_{1/(1+\alpha)}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{\alpha}
depending only on
\alpha
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{m}\leq c_{\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}

and

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\beta_{m}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}{m}\leq c_{\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}^{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}.

Finally, we present some applications concerning almost everywhere convergence of some summability methods. To study almost everywhere convergence of some summability methods is one of
the most difficult topics in Fourier analysis. They involve techniques from
function theory and Hardy spaces.

In most applications the a.e. convergence of
\left\{T_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
can be established for
f
in some dense class of
L_{1}\left(G_{m}\right).
In particular, the following result play an important role for studying this type of questions (see e.g. the books
[
35
]
,
[
55
]
and

[
88
]
).

Lemma 4.44

Let
f\in L_{1}
and
T_{n}:L_{1}\rightarrow L_{1}
be some
sub-linear operators and

T^{\ast}:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|T_{n}\right|.

If

T_{n}f\rightarrow f\text{ a.e. \ \ for every }f\in S

where the set
S
is dense in the space
\ L_{1}
and the maximal operator
T^{\ast}
is bounded from the space
L_{1}
to the space
weak-L_{1},
that
is

\sup_{\lambda>0}\lambda\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\text{ }\left|T^{\ast}f\left(x\right)\right|>\lambda\right\}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{1},

then

T_{n}f\rightarrow f,\text{ a.e. \ \ for every }f\in L_{1}\left(G_{m}\right).

Remark 4.45

Since the Vilenkin function
\psi_{m}
is constant on
I_{n}(x)
for every
x\in G_{m}
and
0\leq m<M_{n},
it is clear that each
Vilenkin function is a complex-valued step function, that is, it is a finite
linear combination of the characteristic functions

\chi\left(E\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&x\in E,\\
0,&x\notin E.\end{array}\right.

On the other hand, notice that, by Corollary
1.5
(Paley lemma), it
yields that

\chi\left(I_{n}(t)\right)\left(x\right)=\frac{1}{M_{n}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{n}-1}\psi_{j}\left(x-t\right),\text{ \ \ }x\in I_{n}(t),

for each
x,t\in

G_{m}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus each step function is
a Vilenkin polynomial. Consequently, we obtain that the collection of step functions
coincides with a collection of Vilenkin polynomials
\mathscr{P}
. Since the
Lebesgue measure is regular it follows from Lusin theorem that given
f\in L_{1}
there exist Vilenkin polynomials
P_{1},P_{2}...,
such that
P_{n}\rightarrow f
a.e. when
n\rightarrow\infty.
This means that the
Vilenkin polynomials are dense in the space
L_{1}.

By using Lemma
4.44
, remarks
4.45
and the results in our previous sections we in particular obtain the
following a.e. convergence results.

Theorem 4.46

Let
f\in L_{1}
and
t_{n}
be the regular Nörlund means
with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
. Then

t_{n}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ a.e., \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.\text{\ }

Proof
:
Since

S_{n}P=P,\text{ for every }P\in\mathscr{P}

we obtain that

t_{n}P\rightarrow P\ \text{a.e.,}\ \ \ \text{when \ \ \ }\ n\rightarrow\infty,

for every regular Nörlund mean with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
, where
P\in\mathscr{P}
is dense in the space
L_{1}
(see Remark
4.45
)
.

On the other hand, by combining Lemma
1.40
and Theorem
4.1
we obtain that the maximal operator of Nörlund means, with
non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying conditions (
1.38
) is bounded from the space
L_{1}
to
the space
weak-L_{1},
that is,

\sup_{\lambda>0}\lambda\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\text{ }\left|t^{\ast}f\left(x\right)\right|>\lambda\right\}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{1}.

According to Lemma
4.44
we obtain that under condition (
1.38
)
we have a.e. convergence

t_{n}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ a.e., \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 4.47

Let
f\in L_{1}
. Then

\sigma_{n}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty,\text{\ \ \ }

and

\kappa_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
Since
\sigma_{n}
and
\kappa_{n}
are regular Nörlund means with
non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
(see (
1.5
) and (
1.10
)) the proof is complete by
just using Theorem
4.46
.

Theorem 4.48

Let
f\in L_{1}
and
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with
non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
). Then

t_{n}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ a.e., \ \ when\ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
By using part b) of Theorem
1.1
we get that every Nörlund mean,
with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is regular. Since

S_{n}P=P,\text{ for every }P\in\mathscr{P}

we obtain that
t_{n}P\rightarrow P,
when
n\rightarrow\infty,
where
P\in\mathscr{P}
is dense in the space
L_{1}
(see Remark
4.45
)
.

On the other hand, by combining Lemma
1.40
and part a) of Theorem
4.11
we obtain that the maximal operator of Nörlund means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
) is bounded from the
space
L_{1}
to the space
weak-L_{1},
that is

\sup_{\lambda>0}\lambda\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\text{ }\left|t^{\ast}f\left(x\right)\right|>\lambda\right\}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{1}.

According to Lemma
4.44
we obtain that under conditions (
1.39
)
and (
1.40
) we have a.e. convergence

t_{n}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ a.e., \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 4.49

Let
f\in L_{1}
. Then

\text{\ \ \ }\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ \ when \ }0<\alpha<1.

and

\beta_{n}^{\alpha}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ \ \ \ when \ }0<\alpha<1.

Proof
:
By combining (
1.6
)-(
1.7
) with (
1.8
)-(
1.9
)
we obtain that the Nörlund means
\sigma_{n}^{\alpha}
and
\beta_{n}^{\alpha}
, with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfy conditions (
1.39
) and (
1.40
).

Hence, the proof is complete by just using Theorem
4.48
.

Corollary 4.50

Let
f\in L_{1}
and
t_{n}
be Nörlund means with
monotone and bounded sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
. Then

\text{\ \ \ }t_{n}f\rightarrow f\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
The proof is analogously to the proofs of Theorems
4.46
and
4.48
so we leave out the details.

Theorem 4.51

Let
f\in L_{1}
. Then

\text{\ \ \ }R_{n}f\rightarrow f,\text{ \ \ \ a.e. \ \ \ \ when \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
The proof is analogously to the proofs of Theorems
4.46
and
4.48
so we leave out the details.
